DAWN OF DECAY — Changelog

Newest first. The Master Document holds current truth; this records what changed and when. (Earlier history lives in the retired Implementation Addendum.)

2026-08-14 — Batch BW: tranche 2, the Warrior nine (TRANCHE 2 COMPLETE)

LEAD WITH THE DEBT BEING PAID. Since Batch BP the Warrior three have been the visible shape of what the draft still owed — two cards apiece against everybody else's five, so a Berserker, a Warden or a Swordmaster was the one hero whose offer of three came up short. That is over. All twelve specs draft from five, the asymmetry is gone, and the draft holds 84 of a target ~96. What remains is a third tranche, and it is owed by all twelve equally rather than by three of them. The mechanically new thing is the STANCE-GATED ABILITY — the first cards in the game that are refused outright unless the Swordmaster is standing in the right guard.

Nine spec draft abilities, three per Warrior spec. Nothing else ships: no talent node, no magnitude, no existing ability changed, and no save version moves (still v10). ONE new unit-side field for nine abilities (Berserk's charge count) — everything with a duration is a STATUS, per BQ's standard.

STANCE-GATED ABILITIES ARE THE NEW MECHANIC, AND THE RULE IS WORTH MORE THAN THE TWO CARDS. READERS BRANCH AND FLIP; GATED ONES REQUIRE AND STAY. BP shipped the readers — Precision Strike and Feint branch on the stance and then switch it, each branch buying what the guard he is arriving in wants. A gated card is a different animal: it is refused outright in the wrong stance (unavailable, not a weaker branch) and casting it moves nothing. _ability_usable is the door, matching how Death Ray's Resonance gate and BV's nine are refused — so the greyed button, the bot's pool and the cast itself can never disagree. Getting the two card types backwards is the single easiest mistake to make on this spec, which is why it ships as a standing rule.

BERSERKER — spend the rage, take the risk, and finally get paid back. His pool was Blood Offering and Gut Rip: both single-target, both about him getting low, and nothing paid him back for being there.

  • Reckless Abandon — 0 Rage, 1.5, 4cd, self. He spends ALL his Rage; for 3 turns he deals +2% damage per 10 points SPENT (Perfect: 3%). Axis: the dump. A full bar is +20% and costs him every ability he might have cast in that window, which is the trade. It scales off what was actually spent, not off his maximum — a half-full Berserker buys half the buff. Refused outright at zero Rage (BO §5's rule). Builds with Blood Offering, Blood Tithe and Bloodied Momentum; a deliberate anti-synergy with Bloodwake, which pays him for holding Rage.
  • Berserk — 25 Rage, 2.0, 5cd, self. His next 3 strikes each land twice (Perfect: 4), and he takes 30% more damage for 3 turns. Axis: the risk dial at maximum, and the drawback IS the payoff — Blood Frenzy scales his damage with missing health, so taking 30% more drives him into his own power band. TWO DIFFERENT CLOCKS ON ONE CARD: the strikes are CHARGES and wait until spent, the 30% is an ordinary 3-turn status. Per BR §1 the charges count HITS, not casts, so Hack and Slash empties the whole bank in one cast and lands six blows. FLAGGED AS PROBABLY THE STRONGEST CARD IN THE WARRIOR SET AND SHIPPED TO BE WATCHED RATHER THAN PRE-TUNED.
  • Blood Debt — 20 Rage, 2.0, 4cd, one enemy, 10 BD. Strikes for 30% of Attack and marks the enemy for the rest of the battle; every time it bleeds out he heals 25% of his maximum health (Perfect: 35%). Axis: a reason to survive being low. THE MARK SURVIVES THE BLEEDOUT IT PAYS FOR, which is the clause most likely to be built as one-shot by accident and the whole card: Slaughterhouse drops a bleedout to 50 rather than 0, so the same marked enemy bleeds out repeatedly and one mark pays three or four times.

SWORDMASTER — the first stance-GATED cards.

  • Sever (requires Aggressive) — 25 Rage, 2.5, 4cd, one enemy, 15 BD. 40% of Attack, and if the target is Broken this ability's cooldown is cleared outright. Axis: the Break window rewards aggression — his Breaker lane opens a one-turn window and nothing let him swing repeatedly inside it. Builds with Shatterpoint, Punishment, Off Balance, Pressure Point and Guard Breaker, all of which live in that window.
  • Battle Poise (requires Defensive) — 25 Rage, 2.0, 4cd, self. For 3 turns every attack he parries takes a turn off all his cooldowns (Perfect: 4 turns). Axis: defence buys tempo. He is the only parry-stat hero in the game and all of it merely made hits smaller; this is the connection his three lanes have never had. It pays PER PARRY and goes through _tick_cooldowns, BQ's one implementation of cooldown reduction; it skips its own recast, because a defensive window that shortens its own return is a different and unbounded card.
  • Feigned Guard — 20 Rage, 1.0, 3cd, self. For 2 turns his abilities resolve as though cast from the other stance — AND satisfy that stance's gate — without switching him (Perfect: 3 turns). Axis: the only card in the game that lets a build have both halves of its own toggle. THE GATE-SATISFYING CLAUSE IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT A CONVENIENCE: without it this is a minor modifier; with it, an Aggressive build can cast Battle Poise and a Defensive build can cast Sever. It is satisfied in _ability_usable, not merely branched at resolution — two different sites, and only the first makes the card true. IT DOES NOT SWITCH THE STANCE AND MUST NOT BE BUILT TO, and his PASSIVE keeps reading the guard he is actually in, so an Aggressive build keeps Aggressive's damage bonus the whole time.

WARDEN — his bulk as a weapon, a permanent duel, and Block that pays the party. His pool was Covering Guard and Eye of the Storm: both defensive, and he had no offense at all.

  • Shield Slam — 25 Rage, 2.5, 3cd, one enemy. Damage equal to 15% of his maximum health (Perfect: 20%) plus 40 Break damage. Axis: his bulk as a weapon, and the Break contributes to a Warrior party's central axis rather than to his own damage number. It reads the maximum LIVE, so Heavy Plating's climb and every +15 Tenacity adds during the fight feed it.
  • Vendetta — 20 Rage, 1.5, 4cd, one enemy. For the rest of the battle that enemy can attack nobody but the Warden, and he takes 20% less damage from it (Perfect: 30%). Axis: a permanent duel — Eye of the Storm taunts everything for two turns, this locks one thing forever. It reuses the taunt system rather than writing a parallel one (a mocked status at -1 turns, exactly the shape The Whole Room installs), so the lock releases if the Warden falls for free: the targeting narrow re-resolves the taunter live and ignores a dead one.
  • Aegis Wall — 25 Rage, 2.0, 5cd, self. For 3 turns (Perfect: 4) every attack he BLOCKS heals all allies 8% of his maximum health. Axis: his signature stat feeds the party. Block negates an attack entirely and paid him nothing beyond that; this is the first thing that makes a block worth something to anyone else. It reads BLOCKS, not attacks taken — a blow that gets through pays nothing — and it reads the maximum LIVE.

ONE NAME COLLISION, FOUND BY BR §1's SWEEP AND RESOLVED RATHER THAN FLAGGED — THE CARD MOVED, NOT THE TALENT. The Warden's permanent duel was authored as GRUDGE, and wd_grudge is already a Warden Threat-lane talent (row 7: +25% damage against enemies his taunt binds), with a Rune of Grudges paying into the same term. Same spec, same lane, one row apart, and mechanically adjacent — the talent's effect is precisely what the card's taunt would feed. That is closer than BP's Precision Strike / Precision Strikes and closer than BV's Ghostpack / Ghost Pack, and unlike those it was avoidable: the talent's id is save-migrated and its ranks travel with it, while the card was unshipped. The card is VENDETTA. The two still compose exactly as intended — Grudge pays +25% against a target his taunt binds, and Vendetta is a taunt that never lapses. The other eight names are clean against every ability, talent node, status and rune, and the whole sweep ships as a test.

BREAK DAMAGE ASSIGNED DELIBERATELY (the standing rule since BO), AND REPORTED. §1 names one figure; the other two are this batch's. THREE of the nine are attacks and carry it: Shield Slam 40 (the brief's), Sever 15 — 40% of Attack for 25 Rage, priced beside Feint (35% / 12) and Precision Strike's Defensive branch (15% / 15), and worth noting that its Break matters most on the FIRST cast, because it is what opens the window the cooldown clause then exploits — and Blood Debt 10, one step under Feint on the same curve with Gut Rip's 20 at the 30-Rage end of it. The other six land no blow at all, so Break would have nothing to ride: that is a decision on each of them, not an omission on any.

TWO STATUSES SIT ON AN ENEMY, AND THAT PUT THEM IN A LIST THE OBVIOUS READING WOULD HAVE MISSED. blood_debt and vendetta are MARKS, so neither is in DEBUFF_IDS — a battle-long entry there reads as 999 turns remaining and a mender's longest-first Cleansing Rite would take it every single time (feinted's reason). But _dispellable_buffs is DERIVED from exactly that absence, so leaving them out of both lists would have made them beneficial statuses in the eyes of the one ability that strips those: a Mage's Dispel would have stripped the party's own marks FOR the enemy carrying them. Both are in DISPEL_NEVER. This is BU's Suffering trap arriving through the mark door. Vendetta's taunt half is a separate mocked status and is deliberately still cleansable, because mocked always has been — that is the same exposure The Whole Room's permanent taunt already carries, and a carve-out for one card would make two permanent taunts behave differently.

BERSERK'S DOUBLING EXPANDS THE HIT COUNT RATHER THAN RE-ENTERING THE STRIKE BODY, so every doubled blow is an ORDINARY blow: it rolls its own crit, miss and parry and carries his bleed buildup, which is what feeds Gut Rip and Blood Debt twice as fast. The target-list expansion beside it looks optional and is not: the strike loop reads strike_targets[hit_i] on the area and chosen-pair branches, so raising the hit count without growing the list runs the doubled strikes off its end. multi_hits and random_hits re-pick their own target every iteration and need no expansion, which is why the expansion is gated to the other branch.

BERSERK'S 30% IS DELIBERATELY NOT FOLDED INTO dmg_taken_bonus, the stat field talents and runes write. Two reasons, and the second is load-bearing: that field is permanent and this is a 3-turn status, and MEASURED RAGE zeroes dmg_taken_bonus outright to cancel Reckless Fury. Letting a talent delete a drafted card's drawback would delete the card, because the 30% is the payoff. Two effects sharing one field is how one of them silently stops existing.

THE BOT GOT NO ROTATION, ON PURPOSE — all nine ride BO §5's wrapper, so no existing rotation is re-weighted and no prior measurement stops being comparable. Four of the nine joined the self-cast list (Reckless Abandon, Berserk, Battle Poise, Feigned Guard and Aegis Wall have nothing to click); Shield Slam, Vendetta, Blood Debt and Sever each name one enemy and fall through to the ordinary target picker. The two stance gates need no bot rule at all: they live in _ability_usable, which the bot's drafted-pick wrapper already consults.

ONE GLOSSARY ENTRY ADDED (88 -> 89): stance_gated. "Requires Aggressive" is a rule the game has never had before, and a player meeting a greyed-out card needs to know why it is dark and what Feigned Guard does about it. The existing stance entry cross-links to it and now says two of his abilities REQUIRE a guard.

MASTER.HTML CORRECTED TOWARD THE CODE WHERE IT HAD DRIFTED, beside the ordinary update: the draft table's intro read "the fifty-four drafted abilities" against a table already holding seventy-five rows (BT, BU and BV each added rows without moving that sentence), and the Swordmaster group header still read "both cards read the stance and then switch it" against a lane that now holds five. Both now state what the code does.

2026-08-14 — Batch BV: tranche 2, the Hunter nine

LEAD WITH PREPARATION, because it is the first extra-turn mechanic in the game. PREPARATION (Survivalist) is 25 Mana for a plain sentence: after his NEXT turn he immediately takes another one. Nothing in Dawn of Decay has ever handed a hero a second action. Two things came close and neither is this — Rally hands an ALLY the next slot in the order (they still spend that turn; nobody gains one), and Instinctive Rotation makes one specific action, the companion swap, cost no turn. Preparation is a whole turn out of nowhere, and because it is a whole turn it carries a whole turn's costs: his own statuses tick again for it. Three rules were decided here rather than left to whichever branch the code fell into, and all three are in CLAUDE.md.

Nine spec draft abilities, three per Hunter spec. The Hunter pools go from two to five, joining the Mage and the Cleric. The Warrior three are the last third of tranche 2 owed, and tranche 3 after them. The draft holds 75 of a target ~96. Nothing else ships: no talent node, no magnitude, no existing ability changed, and no save version moves (still v10).

BEASTMASTER — the partnership protected, widened, and paid back. His pool was Twin Hunt and Call the Wilds: both about the companion striking. Nothing protected it, and Loyalty — uncapped, measured reaching 50 — dies with it.

  • Bloodbond — 20 Mana, 2.0, 4cd, self. The next blow that would fell a companion is refused, and the hunter takes half of it instead (Perfect: a quarter). Axis: protecting the single largest investment in the game. It is a placed guard, not a window — no duration, so it is never wasted on a turn nothing threatened the beast, and it holds until it fires. The half he takes can kill him, and the card says so: a guard that cannot cost anything is not a decision.
  • Savage Sweep — 25 Mana, 2.5, 4cd, self. His companion strikes the three lowest-health enemies and gains 3 Loyalty (Perfect: 5). Axis: the partnership made wide. Lowest-health rather than random, deliberately: it is a finisher, and a random spread would make it a worse Cinderfall.
  • Ghostpack — 25 Mana, 2.5, 5cd, self. For 3 turns (Perfect: 4) every companion he has summoned this battle strikes alongside his attacks at 40%, including the ones no longer standing. Axis: what rotating actually buys. It is the Pack build's payoff and deliberately near-worthless to a Lone Bond build, which fields one companion and never swaps.

SHARPSHOOTER — the multiplier half, the finish, and the field. His pool was Called Volley and Quarry's Mark: both about generating and keeping Focus. Nothing read the half past 100, where Focus stops buying crit chance and starts buying crit multiplier without a ceiling.

  • Crossfire — 25 Mana, 2.5, 4cd, one enemy, 10 BD. 30% of Attack, and for 3 turns (Perfect: 4) every critical hit also strikes 2 other enemies for 40% of that crit's damage. Axis: the crit build goes wide. Anti-synergy with Consistent Aim is deliberate and named — that node trades multiplier for chance, so a Consistent build wants a different card, and that is what makes this a build decision rather than a strict pick.
  • Calibrating Shot — 20 Mana, 1.5, 3cd, one enemy, 8 BD. 20% of Attack, and Focus equal to 10% of the target's missing health (Perfect: 15%). Axis: patience rewarded for finishing what he started. The reading is taken BEFORE this shot lands, so "a fresh enemy pays nothing" is literally true rather than nearly true — measured afterwards, the shot's own damage would have paid a few Focus every cast and quietly made the card's promise false.
  • Trophy Shot — 25 Mana, 2.5, 4cd, one enemy, 12 BD. 35% of Attack (Perfect: 45%). If this kills, his Focus is not reduced at all and carries whole to the next enemy. Axis: the kill stops costing him. If it does not kill it is an ordinary shot and the ordinary switch rules apply — the card's own text says so, because a conditional card that reads unconditional is a tooltip lying.

SURVIVALIST — hold the breadth, cash the breadth, buy a turn. His pool was Choking Smoke and Snare Line: both wide, both one-shot. His engine is breadth and breadth decays — Cripple runs out while he is applying Slow, and the count Trapper pays him for quietly shrinks.

  • Loaded Shot — 20 Mana, 2.0, 4cd, one enemy, 8 BD. 20% of Attack, and every harmful effect on the target is reset to full duration, the uncleansable ones included (Perfect: it also lands 2 turns of Poison). Axis: craft as maintenance, on a turn he would have been attacking anyway. It reads a new full_turns ledger written by add_status itself rather than a table of defaults, so a talent that lengthened a status lengthened its full value too. It can only lengthen: a re-applied Burn already standing longer than it started is left alone, and a permanent affliction stays permanent.
  • Hunt — 25 Mana, 2.5, 4cd, one enemy, 10 BD. 15% of Attack for every different harmful effect on the target (Perfect: 20%) — 60% at four, 90% at six. Axis: the count made into a weapon. Distinct effects, never stacks, and that is a property of one implementation rather than a second rule: it shares _status_count with the Trapper passive's own breadth term, so the card and the passive can never disagree about what breadth is.
  • Preparation — 25 Mana, 2.0, 5cd, self. After his next turn he immediately takes another (Perfect: the extra turn also refunds 15 Mana). Axis: an extra action IS extra breadth, for a spec where every status costs a whole cast.

PREPARATION'S THREE RULES, DECIDED HERE RATHER THAN BY WHICHEVER BRANCH THE CODE FELL INTO.

  • The extra turn is a FULL turn and it re-ticks his statuses. Designer's call: simpler and legible. It falls out of the implementation rather than being bolted on — he re-enters the ordinary initiative loop, so his damage-over-time effects bite, his buffs shorten and his cooldowns tick, exactly as on any turn. The cost is real without being punishing because ENEMY statuses tick on ENEMY turns, so the board he just armed is untouched by his second action.
  • It must not chain. Casting Preparation on the extra turn to earn another is an unbounded loop — a hang, not a balance question — so the cast is refused while one is pending. That refusal is load-bearing rather than tidy, and it is the negative control test_batch_bv drives directly.
  • The delay is what makes it a setup card rather than "take two turns now". A counter armed at 2 and decremented at the end of each of his turns is what makes that exact: the turn he cast on burns one and grants nothing, and his next turn grants the extra one.

THE Improvised INTERACTION IS REPORTED, NOT PRE-TUNED — AND THE MEASUREMENT DISAGREED WITH THE BRIEF. Improvised (Survivalist, Guerilla row 7) stops the first two abilities of each fight starting their cooldowns, and the brief predicted a Guerilla build "opens with Preparation twice — bounded at two". Two is the bound on the FREE casts, not on the opening. Driven honestly in test_batch_bv — cast whenever the gate allows, tick at the end of every turn — such a build gets three Preparations in its first five turns: two Improvised-funded, and then a third the shortened clock already allows, because the extra turn each cast buys is a REAL turn and ticks the cooldown with it. The card accelerates its own cooldown, which nothing in the brief anticipated and no reading of the code would have shown. What IS bounded is the thing that makes the mechanic safe, and it is asserted rather than described: every cast buys exactly one extra turn, the counter never rises above 2, and two can never be pending at once. Shipped as specified and flagged; the number to watch in play is the opening cadence, and the lever if it reads hot is Preparation's 5-turn cooldown.

WHAT "HIS COMPANION" RESOLVES TO UNDER THE PACK, decided rather than left to the first beasts[0] in the file. The general rule is worth more than either card: an ORDERED action goes to ONE companion; the passive strike-alongside goes to ALL of them. The alongside-strike already loops every beast and must, because that is what The Pack's own text promises; an ordered action must not, or the capstone silently doubles every card that names the beast.

  • Bloodbond is the exception that proves it: it names no beast at all. The guard is placed on the BOND, so under The Pack it covers both and is spent by whichever one first takes a killing blow. It is wired at the one place a companion is built, so a beast summoned after the guard was sworn is covered too.
  • Savage Sweep picks the highest-Loyalty standing companion — The Pack's own rule ("the deeper bond always keeps its place") read the other way round — and lands its 3 Loyalty where the boon curve compounds hardest. Three strikes whether one beast stands or two. Ties break on list order, so it is deterministic rather than merely usually-right.
  • REPORTED, NOT CHANGED: Twin Hunt (tranche 1) still picks beasts[0] by list order. It is an existing ability and this tranche changes none, so the inconsistency is the designer's to close — one call site.

GHOSTPACK COLLIDES WITH THE GHOST PACK TALENT NODE, and it is the closest collision the project has had. bm_ghost_pack is a Beastmaster Handler row 8 node — the same spec, so one hero can hold both — the two names are one space apart, and they are mechanically adjacent: the node makes a beastless hunter's attacks still draw a companion strike at 60%. Per the BR §1 rule this ships as specified and is flagged: a node's name is not an ability name, nothing resolves it, and pool_ability("Ghost Pack") returns null (asserted). Two things for the designer. They stack: a Beastmaster holding both while standing beastless gets the node's 60% strike and a 40% ghost for every kind he has summoned — up to 180% of Attack in extra strikes per attack for three turns. That is not pre-tuned. And renaming the card is one string.

THE CARD FIRES FOR EVERY KIND SUMMONED, STANDING OR NOT, and that reading was decided rather than inferred, because the two readings differ by a third of the card. Excluding the beasts currently standing would make the ability get smaller the moment one arrived, so a Pack build with two out would be paid less than one with none — the opposite of what a Pack payoff should feel like. It reuses kinds_summoned, the ledger Batch AY already keeps for Feral Momentum and Menagerie, rather than writing a second one, and it strikes through _ghost_hit, which credits the hunter — Batch BB repaired exactly that site after AY found it booking damage to nobody, and a second uncredited bodiless striker would have undone the repair silently.

ONE THING THE BRIEF DID NOT ANTICIPATE, FOUND WHILE TESTING: a bear Savage Sweep is much wider than three. Ursus's own blow mauls the enemies beside its target (its kit since Batch 30), so a Savage Sweep run by the bear touches up to three targets and their neighbours, while the same card run by the wolf or the eagle hits exactly three. Nothing is wrong — it is the beast's own rule composing with a new card — but it is a real reason to pick which companion runs it, and it is now in master.html so a player is not surprised by it.

FIVE OF THE NINE DELIBERATELY CARRY NO special (the Batch BT Arcane Bolt pattern), and it is recorded because it looks like an omission. _resolve sends any ability holding a special down _resolve_special, which means hand-rolling the blow and losing the whole attack pipeline with it: crits, armor, resists, Break, the parry roll, the Focus engine and every talent rider that reads a strike. Crossfire is defined by a crit, Calibrating Shot and Trophy Shot by the Focus engine, Hunt scales the raw damage and Loaded Shot rides a landed hit — all five need that pipeline, so they key on their display name at the ordinary rider sites. test_batch_bv asserts the split both ways round, because either half getting it wrong is silent.

BREAK DAMAGE WAS ASSIGNED DELIBERATELY RATHER THAN BY OMISSION (the standing rule since BO): the five that strike carry it in line with their siblings — Crossfire 10, Calibrating Shot 8, Trophy Shot 12, Loaded Shot 8, Hunt 10 — and the four that land no blow carry none, because Break from an ability that never hits is Break from nowhere.

UNIT-SIDE COST: ONE CALLBACK AND ONE COUNTER FOR NINE ABILITIES, plus one field on the status dictionary. Everything with a duration is a STATUS (bloodbond, ghostpack, crossfire), which is BQ's standard — statuses expire by themselves and cannot leak past a battle. bloodbond_cb is the Vow of Suffering shape aimed at the other side of the bond; prep_pending is a counter rather than a bool because that is what makes Preparation's delay exact. The new full_turns entry on each status is a LEDGER, not a duration, and it pins at -1 for a permanent status and never climbs out — without that, a later timed application would let Loaded Shot write a real number onto Permafrost or a Perfected Toxin and quietly un-permanent it.

NINE SUITES MOVED FOR THE MASTER.HTML STAMP AND FOUR MORE FOR THE POOL TOTALS, AND THE SECOND GROUP IS THE ONE WORTH KNOWING ABOUT. The stamp gate is duplicated across nine suites and all nine move together every time the timestamp is bumped — ordinary, predicted, and growing by one a batch. The other four (test_batch_bo, bp, bq, br) hard-code the draft totals, and three of them built their "an offer fills SHORT rather than padding" check by refusing a hero's whole CLASS pool and leaving him on his two SPEC cards. Taking the Hunter pools to five stopped that construction producing a thin pool: those checks began passing three and quietly stopped measuring what they were written to measure. Repaired by making the refusal reach the spec pool too, and in one case by moving it onto a Berserker — whose pool is genuinely still two. When the Warrior third lands that one has to move again, and the forced move is the honest signal that tranche 2 is finished.

THE BATCH BT INDENTATION FAULT HAPPENED TWICE HERE AND THE SUITE CAUGHT BOTH. BT recorded that a one-tab error can make an effect fire only for holders of an unrelated ability; in this batch Crossfire's splash landed inside if attacker.through_and_through > 0 (so it would have worked only for a Sharpshooter holding Through and Through) and Loaded Shot's refresh landed inside if attacker.has_status("venom_coat") (so it would have worked only under Venom Coating). Neither crashed, neither logged, and both were found only because the checks drove the effect directly and asserted the exact number rather than that the cast returned. The lesson stands and is now twice-proven: a hook added to an existing block must be checked against the block it landed in, not the block it was aimed at.

2026-08-14 — Batch BU: tranche 2, the Cleric nine

LEAD WITH THE TWO CROSS-SPEC CARDS, because they are the first draft abilities whose value is measured on somebody else's sheet. RECANT (Holy) hands an ally back 30% of their maximum Rage or Mana — it is a different ability in every party, worth most beside the hero whose engine is the party's engine. ANOINTING (Occultist) makes every ally's attacks apply Ruin for three turns, so three heroes who have never contributed to the meter that is his entire spec start feeding it. Every card before these paid off on the sheet of the hero who cast it.

Nine spec draft abilities, three per Cleric spec. The Cleric pools go from two to five, joining the Mage. The Hunter and Warrior thirds of tranche 2 are owed next, and tranche 3 after them. The draft holds 66 of a target ~96. Nothing else ships: no talent node, no magnitude, no existing ability changed, and no save version moves (still v10).

HOLY — what else can be reversed. Her pool was Second Wind (undo recent damage) and Rite of Return (prevent a death): both reactive, both defensive, and she had no offence at all.

  • Recant — 25 Mana, 2.0, 4cd, one ally. Restores 30% of their maximum resource (Perfect: 40%). Axis: reversal of expenditure. The primary resource only — Resonance, Mercy, Faith and Focus are spec meters and are untouched, because handing an Arcanist his compounding curve back for 25 Mana would make one card worth more than the passive it feeds.
  • Shared Grief — 20 Mana, 2.0, 4cd, self. She takes 25% of her maximum health as damage and gains 3 Mercy (Perfect: 4); it can never take her below 1. Axis: martyrdom as an economy — her meter has only ever filled by letting the party be hurt. A flat 3 rather than a scaling count, deliberately: the first draft paid per wounded ally, which in any real fight is her whole meter for one cast.
  • Reprisal — 25 Mana, 2.5, 3cd, one enemy, 6 BD. Damage equal to 50% of the healing she has landed in the last two turns (Perfect: 75%). Axis: her healing becomes a weapon — her only offence, and the answer to the turn where nobody is hurt. Overheal does not count, or Sanctum's 60% spill would pay twice.

DEVOUT — what else can he place. His pool was Vow of Suffering and Aegis Reversal, both acting on shields already out; he could not put Faith on anyone directly, and nothing in the game read faith_peak except the held-value multiplier Batch BI wrote.

  • Ordination — 25 Mana, 2.0, 4cd. Grants 3 Faith (Perfect: 4) to the ally holding the least. Axis: starting the engine without waiting to be hit. The lowest-Faith targeting is a design decision and is not player-chosen: Faith caps at five, so three granted to an ally on four throws two away, and aiming at the floor points the card at the ally Communion can never roll for.
  • Fortified Spirit — 25 Mana, 2.0, 4cd, one ally. Their maximum health rises by 30% of the Devout's (Perfect: 40%), healing them for the same, and the loan decays by a tenth of his maximum each of their turns. Axis: lending the number his whole kit is built on. Current health clamps under the shrinking maximum, so it rewards healing them inside the window.
  • Reliquary — 30 Mana, 2.5, 5cd, all allies. Each ally is healed for 2.5% of the Devout's maximum per point of their PEAK Faith (Perfect: 3.5%). Axis: cashing an accumulation nothing else reads. An ally who released down to zero is still paid in full for what they carried — which is the whole reason Batch BI introduced the field.

OCCULTIST — what else can be done to a mark. His pool was Blight the Well and Covenant of Ash, both about where corruption lands. The mark itself was a number and nothing but a number: everything read its depth, nothing moved it, fed it or traded it.

  • Suffering — 25 Mana, 2.5, 4cd, one enemy, 8 BD. 20% of Attack, healing him for all of the damage dealt, and for 4 turns the enemy gains 2 Ruin at the start of each of its turns (Perfect: 3). Axis: corruption that works on the enemy's clock. One cast buys eight stacks without another turn spent. The heal does not route through the Ruin lifesteal, which is capped at 40%.
  • Transference — 20 Mana, 2.0, 3cd, one enemy. Moves every Ruin stack from the deepest-marked other enemy onto this one (Perfect: plus 2). Axis: corruption relocated rather than spent. Nothing detonates in transit — Cryoclasm's precedent exactly.
  • Anointing — 30 Mana, 2.5, 5cd. For 3 turns (Perfect: 4) every ally's attacks apply 1 Ruin, counting hits, not casts. Axis: the party marks for him. Flagged as possibly the strongest card in the tranche and shipped to be watched rather than pre-tuned — Batch AY had to double Ruin generation because trash detonations measured 0.00, and four heroes marking instead of one could overshoot the other way. The designer has taken that call knowingly.

THREE PREMISES IN THE BRIEF WERE STALE AND ARE CORRECTED TOWARD THE CODE (the AR §6 / AX §7 / BD §3 / AY §1 / BS / BT precedent):

  • Recant is not the first thing to restore another hero's resource. War Stomp refuels every ally 10% (20% perfect), Cold Storage (Cryomancer, Deep Freeze row 8) drips the party a share per held prison, and Rally's perfect hands its target 15%. What is true is the smaller and more useful claim: nothing was a deep single-target restore, and nothing existed whose entire payload was the restore.
  • There are only TWO primary resources, not three. The brief has Recant reading "Rage, Mana and Focus alike"; resource_name is Rage for the Warrior and Mana for the other three classes, and Focus is a SECOND resource (Batch AZ) — a spec meter, i.e. exactly what the card must not restore. The card's own text says so.
  • The Apostle release loop Ordination's targeting was justified by cannot happen. The brief argues that under Apostle a release consumes no stacks, so granting 3 to an ally on 4 would release them, leave them at 5, and re-release on every later gain. Batch BG moved Apostle onto the HELD half and Batch BH deleted Binding Oath's remnant, so a release resets an ally to zero unconditionally and nothing parks anyone at five. The targeting rule is right and ships as specified; its stated reason was two batches stale, and the real ones are the cap at five and Communion's blind spot. Binding Oath's synergy line was corrected the same way — it swears the Devout a stack now, it does not keep three through a release.

ONE NAME ADJACENCY, REPORTED AND NOT RESOLVED (BR §1). SUFFERING sits inside VOW OF SUFFERING, the Devout's own tranche-1 card — same class, both in the draft, and one name a strict substring of the other. Nothing breaks: pool_ability is keyed on the whole display_name, the two strings differ, and the two share no special; test_batch_bu pins all three facts. Renaming either is the designer's call and one string. The other eight names are clean against every ability, talent node, status and rune in the game.

BREAK DAMAGE ASSIGNED DELIBERATELY, NOT BY OMISSION (the BO/BP/BQ/BR/BT rule). The brief names no figure, so all nine are this batch's. Suffering 8, level with Stoke and Arcane Bolt and below the free Shadowrend's 16 — a card that supplements the free basic must not out-Break it. Reprisal 6, level with Kindled Mind and well below the free Smite's 16, because a card that already converts her whole throughput into damage must not also out-Break her basic. The other seven carry none — they land no blow.

ONE UNIT-SIDE FIELD FOR NINE ABILITIES, and it is a ledger rather than a duration. heal_by_turn is the mirror of the dmg_by_turn Second Wind already rides: bounded to two keys, written by _book_healing at _stat_heal — BC's one credit door, so every heal in the game reaches it and one added later cannot forget. It books LANDED healing, subtracting the recipient's last_overheal, and it sits above the sim guard because an ability that only worked inside the sim would be an ability no player could use. The sim's contribution columns are byte-unchanged. Everything else with a duration is a status.

FORTIFIED SPIRIT IS FORCED TO EXPIRE AT THE VICTORY SYNC RATHER THAN GAINING A FOURTH FIELD THERE, and the choice is the whole landmine. A mid-battle max_hp change leaks through sync_victory_state — the arithmetic with a ~127,000 max-HP runaway in its history and the reason rot was dropped from Batch AQ. The effect is explicitly temporary, so there is nothing to preserve and no question a fourth sign would answer; unwinding the loan first leaves tenacity_hp_gained, conviction_hp_gained and rot_hp_lost untouched and still comparable with every batch that reasoned about them.

ONE GLOSSARY ENTRY ADDED (86 → 87): Lent Maximum Health. Fortified Spirit is the one thing in the batch a player meets with no explanation anywhere else — a maximum that rises and then decays, with current health clamping under it on the way down. The entry says both halves. Everything else the nine do rides a mechanic the player already meets (Faith, Ruin, Mercy, a resource bar), so nothing else needed one.

VERIFIED — and per the standing instruction that means the code landed and works. NO SWEEPS, NO BANDS, NO BALANCE MEASUREMENT WAS RUN, and none should be quoted from this batch. check_parse 0 · check_flow 0 · new test_batch_bu.gd 476/0, with all eight clauses §6 named as able to silently do nothing driven live and built so a broken implementation still fails. Ten negative controls were applied to product code and reverted, and classes.gd, battle.gd and unit.gd all came back byte-identical by hash.

2026-08-14 — Batch BT: tranche 2, the Mage nine

THE RULE FIRST, because it outlives the content: FROM THIS TRANCHE ON, EVERY ABILITY NAMES WHAT IT BUILDS WITH. Tranche 1 asked what gap a card filled, which was the right question against an empty pool. At five per spec the question becomes whether a build can be assembled — so each of the nine below names the talent node, the other card or the capstone it is meant to be played beside, and that line ships in classes.gd beside the definition and in master.html's draft table where a player reads it. A card that appears in nobody's build plan is a card that fills a slot, and it is now the thing a new ability is checked against.

Nine spec draft abilities, three per Mage spec. The Mage pools go from two to five; everybody else is still at two. The Cleric, Hunter and Warrior thirds of tranche 2 are owed next, and tranche 3 after them. The draft holds 57 of a target ~96. Nothing else ships: no talent node, no magnitude, no existing ability changed, no save version moves (still v10), and no unit-side field was added for any of the nine — everything with a duration is a status, on Batch BQ's standard.

PYROMANCER — hold the bank, multiply it, cash it for something else. His spine after Batch BS is deferred damage: he is weak until he cashes in, and with the drain deleted holding fire no longer costs Mana. What it costs is turns, and these three are the three things a bank can do.

  • Slow Burn — 15 Mana, 1.5, 4cd, no target. For 3 turns (Perfect: 4) the Burn on every enemy stops counting down. It still burns for its full damage; it just never gets shorter. Builds with Powder Keg, Sea of Flame and Cataclysm, all of which want the field lit longer; strongest straight after Firestorm. The marker rides enemies not yet alight, so casting it before the fire is a real line of play — which is why it is the one card here with no usable-gate, stated as a decision rather than left as an omission.
  • Stoke — 20 Mana, 2.0, 3cd, one enemy, 8 BD. 25% of Attack, and a Burning target's remaining turns are DOUBLED (Perfect: tripled). A multiplier, deliberately, not an addition: Backdraft already adds a flat +2 to every burning enemy, so an additive Stoke would have been Backdraft aimed at one body — the Deadfall/Snare Trap duplication Batch BD found. Builds with Detonation (250% of the bank, 325% under Focused Flame), Slow Burn, and Total Commitment.
  • Funeral Pyre — 25 Mana, 2.5, 4cd, one enemy. Consumes all Burn on the target for a shield worth 150% of the damage that Burn would still have dealt (Perfect: 200%), and Overburn refunds every turn consumed. The only card in his kit that converts fire into survival. Builds with the whole Inferno lane, every node of which is a percentage — a shield is the flat term that lane never gets.

SLOW BURN AND STOKE ARE THE STRONGEST PAIRING IN THE TRANCHE AND SHIP DELIBERATELY. Stall the field, double one stack, detonate. That is what a deferred-damage spec should be able to build toward, and it costs two cards, two casts and two turns to set up. Watch it in play; it was not pre-tuned.

CRYOMANCER — get a hold, pay before the hold, survive without one. His pool's two existing cards both assume he already holds something — Winter's Toll collects interest on a hold, Rimebinding copies one — so against a boss, which resists Frozen until Broken, both were dead cards for most of the fight.

  • Flash Freeze — 30 Mana, 3.0, 5cd, one enemy. Freezes outright whatever the stacks. The boss carve-out was verified before the card's text was written, and the text says what the code does: a boss still resists until Broken, and a held boss shrugs the ice off after ONE turn — so this buys a turn against one, not a lockdown. Its perfect takes an unbroken boss (still one turn), through the same call-site-visible force argument Pommel Strike's and Snare Trap's perfects already buy. Builds with Cold Snap and Second Prison. Expensive and slow on purpose — the emergency, not the opener.
  • Killing Frost — 20 Mana, 2.0, 3cd, all enemies, 6 BD. Every Chilled enemy takes 20% of Attack and gains 2 more stacks (Perfect: 3). Builds with Hypothermia, Frigid Grip, Winter's Depth and Bitter Cold; best after Blizzard or Eternal Winter. The stacks go on one application at a time, because that is the branch where four stacks flash-freeze — writing the pile directly would skip it and quietly make the card's last line false.
  • Hoarfrost Armor — 20 Mana, 2.0, 4cd, self. For 3 turns (Perfect: 4) he takes 25% less damage, and anything that strikes him gains 2 stacks of Chilled. The only thing in his kit that builds stacks without spending a turn applying them. Builds with Frigid Grip, Winter's Depth and Splintering Shards.

ARCANIST — the first thing in the DRAFT that spends, and the brief's premise was corrected toward the code. §4 says his kit has no consumer at all; Stabilize has been one since Batch AT, and it is recorded there as the deliberate exception to "nothing removes Resonance". What is true is that his draft pool had none. The two spenders are different shapes and that difference is the design: Stabilize is a boss-pool pick that VENTS to a floor of 2 for Mana and a ward; Arcane Bolt is a drafted card that HALVES for damage, so the curve keeps compounding from where it lands instead of restarting.

  • Arcane Bolt — 30 Mana, 2.5, 4cd, one enemy, 8 BD. 15% of Attack per Resonance stack (Perfect: 20%), then his stacks are halved. At 6 that is 90% of Attack; at 16 it is 240% and he lands on 8. The halving is AFTER the blow, so it is paid on the meter he walked in with. Builds with Cascade, Harmonics and Critical Mass, which make the climb back cheaper — and the Entropy lane cares twice, because halving the stacks also halves the compounding damage-taken penalty.
  • Inner Arcane — 15 Mana, 1.0, 3cd, self. Banks Resonance equal to the enemies still alive, doubled below half health (Perfect: one more). Builds with On the Edge and Backlash: an Entropy build gets paid three ways for being nearly dead.
  • Arcane Echo — 25 Mana, 2.0, 4cd, one enemy, 6 BD. 20% of Attack, and for 3 turns (Perfect: 4) every damaging HIT he lands anywhere repeats at 30% against this target. Per Batch BR §1 the echo counts HITS, not casts — a three-bolt Barrage echoes three times — and the card says so outright, because a player will assume otherwise. It reads the damage the hit actually dealt, so the echo carries his full multiplier. One mark at a time. Builds with Barrage Master and Suppressing Fire; it is also the only reason he has to focus one target.

BREAK DAMAGE ASSIGNED DELIBERATELY, NOT BY OMISSION (the BO/BP/BQ/BR rule, applied up front). The brief names no figure, so all nine are this batch's: Stoke 8 and Arcane Bolt 8, level with Cinderfall and below Fireball's 15 — a card that supplements the free basic must not out-Break it; Killing Frost 6 and Arcane Echo 6, level with Kindled Mind, and Killing Frost below Cinderfall's 8 because it only reaches Chilled enemies. The other five carry none — they land no blow, and Break from an ability that never strikes is Break from nowhere. Arcane Bolt's Break is FLAT rather than per-stack, deliberately: a per-stack Break term is Arcane Cannon's and Magi's Wrath's axis, and duplicating it here would have been the squaring trap Batch AT exists around, arriving through the Break door.

TWO FINDINGS, REPORTED AND NOT RE-TUNED.

  • FLASH FREEZE IS DOMINATED BY GLACIAL PRISON ON EVERY NUMBER — the node is 25 Mana, 2.5, 4cd for the same outright freeze, against this card's 30, 3.0 and 5cd. The distinction is the acquisition channel, and it is real: Glacial Prison is a bought cell in one lane of the tree, so a Winter or Thaw Cryomancer can never have it, while this is drafted by any of them. Its perfect is what stops it being dominated outright — no node buys the unbroken boss. Closest two cards since Batch BR found Cleave nearly War Stomp; recorded here rather than discovered later.
  • KILLING FROST COLLIDES WITH A LIVE CRYOMANCER TALENT NODE (cr_freezing, Thaw row 2). Same spec, so a Cryomancer holding the node can draft the card — the Iron Will shape rather than Batch BP's Precision Strike. It is a label collision only: a node's name is not an ability name, nothing resolves it, the node's counter is a stat field and the card's half is a handler, and the two touch nothing in common. Shipped as specified and flagged, on the BR §1 rule; renaming either is the designer's call and one string. Worth knowing that the brief renamed this card from Cold Snap to dodge a node collision and landed on that node's lane-mate. Hoarfrost Armor is an adjacency rather than a collision: hoarfrost is a severity-1 battle modifier, so the card's status is rimeguard precisely to keep two chips from reading the same word. The other six names are clean against the whole roster.

THE SHIELD USES THE EXISTING DOOR, AS §2 INSTRUCTED. Funeral Pyre goes through the barrier status Magic Barrier and Divine Shield already share, so the absorb, the Warded Robes rider and the prevented ledger all come free — and deliberately NOT as a divine shield, for Magic Barrier's own reason: Faith is the Devout's engine, and a Mage's ward feeding Conviction would be a spec mechanic leaking out through a Mage card. It is the fifth consumer of the ONE refund door, which is Batch AR's rule still working; test_batch_ar's pinned call-site count goes 4 → 5.

ONE FUNCTION GAINED AN ARGUMENT AND NOTHING ELSE MOVED: _hold_freeze takes an optional force, defaulted off, so its two older callers are byte-identical in behaviour and the boss carve-out sits exactly where it was. Exactly one caller arms it — a perfect Flash Freeze — and a forced boss is still timed, so the perfect buys the turn earlier, never a longer one.

THE BOT WAS NOT GIVEN A ROTATION, ON PURPOSE. All nine ride Batch BO's wrapper, which substitutes a usable drafted ability only when the real rotation came back with the free basic — so no existing rotation is re-weighted and no measurement taken before this batch stops being comparable. What did change is three targeting refinements inside that wrapper, each closing a case where the default mark would make a card read as inert: Stoke and Funeral Pyre aim at the deepest burning stack rather than at the lowest-health enemy (which is very often carrying no fire), and Arcane Echo aims at the highest-health enemy, because a three-turn mark laid on the enemy about to die spends its whole window on a corpse — Rally's problem arriving through a different door.

THREE LOG-HONESTY FIXES, ALL FOUND BY WATCHING A SMOKE RUN. Flash Freeze's perfect announced "the ice takes an UNBROKEN boss" against a raider; Inner Arcane printed the Resonance it asked for rather than what landed, so a hero who cannot hold the meter read "+2 Resonance (now 0)" (the Blessed Barrier precedent, Batch BC); and Stoke printed "1 turns".

VERIFIED — and per the standing instruction that means the code landed and works. NO SWEEPS, NO BANDS, NO BALANCE MEASUREMENT WAS RUN, and none should be quoted from this batch. check_parse 0 · check_flow 0 · NEW test_batch_bt.gd. All seven clauses §6 named as able to silently do nothing are driven live and asserted against the state they change, and seven are built so a broken implementation still fails: Slow Burn's turn count and the victim's health are read across the same ticks (a frozen status passes one and fails the other); Stoke is measured at 3 turns AND at 7, where doubling and any flat addition diverge by construction, plus the empty case; Funeral Pyre's shield is sized against a burn whose turns (5) and tick (8) are deliberately different numbers, so a turns-only or tick-only read lands nowhere near 60; Flash Freeze is driven at an unbroken boss, a Broken one and an unbroken one on a perfect, with the one-turn span asserted each time; Killing Frost is cast on a field holding a chilled half and an unchilled half at once; Arcane Bolt is driven at two stack counts AND at one stack, where a halve-first implementation floors to nothing; and Arcane Echo's echo count is compared as an identity between a single-strike probe and a three-hit volley, which is what tells three per volley from three per cast.

FIFTEEN NEGATIVE CONTROLS, each applied to product code and reverted (classes.gd, battle.gd and unit.gd all came back byte-identical by hash): Slow Burn freezing the whole status rather than its clock trips 2; Slow Burn inert trips 2; Stoke adding rather than multiplying trips 3; Funeral Pyre reading turns and ignoring the tick trips 4; the Pyre skipping the refund trips 1; Flash Freeze forcing on every cast trips 2; Killing Frost hitting every enemy trips 5; Killing Frost writing the pile directly trips 1; Hoarfrost Armor chilling the wrong body trips 1; Arcane Bolt halving before the blow trips 3; the echo firing once per cast trips 1; the echo ignoring whose mark it is trips 1; Inner Arcane counting the dead trips 3; Inner Arcane with no health clause trips 2; a spec card leaking into a class pool trips 2. None passed — the first battery of controls in several batches where that is true.

FULL BATTERY GREEN, ZERO FAILURES ANYWHERE. ah 5500, ah_battle 65, ai 2217, aj 418, ak 528, al 560, ar 735, as 396, at 470, au 336, av 324, aw 350, ax 339, ay 484, az 519, ba 690, bb 172, bc 91, bd 69, be 34, bf 78, bg 47, bh 233, bi 88, bj 67, bl 88, bm 1891, bn 77, bo 570, bp 268, bq 738, br 1441, bs 262, bt 454, runes 2973, rune_battle 97. an reads 3615 and bk 129, both inside their documented run-to-run drift. Four suites were re-pointed in place with the reason in each file and four of the re-points are inversions — test_batch_br's three "this pool is still two deep, tranches 2 and 3 are owed" became "five for the Mage three, two for the other nine"; the spec-pool totals in bo, bp, bq and br go 24 → 33; ar's and bs's _overburn_refund call-site counts go 4 → 5; ax's force := false count goes 1 → 2 with a companion check that _hold_freeze threads the argument rather than deciding by name. The master.html stamp gate is duplicated eight times now — ah, bb, bn, bo, bp, bq, br and bs — and all eight moved together.

LIVE AUTOPLAY CLEAN, 0 SCRIPT ERROR, ALL NINE FIRING IN ORDINARY FIGHTS — "Slow Burn — Burn stops counting down on 3 enemies for 3 turns (it still burns)"; "Stoke: Orc Raider's Burn goes 2 turns to 4" followed by "4 turns to 8" on the next cast, which is the combo compounding in plain sight; "Funeral Pyre — 2 turns of Burn consumed from Orc Chief (worth 12), a shield of 24 [PERFECT]" immediately followed by "Overburn: 2 turns of Burn consumed refunds 2 Mana"; and "Arcane Echo: Orc Chief answers Quick Shot for 11". A --run 6 was walked ONLY to exercise RunSim's draft path: 8.17 offers/run, 24.50 cards shown, taken 8.17 — exactly three cards an offer, never short, which is the deepened Mage pools measured end to end. Its Matrix row is not a difficulty reading and none is quoted. The designer had no run in flight and none was created; profile.json and relics.json are byte-identical by hash.

2026-08-14 — Batch BS: Overburn loses the drain, and Inferno becomes a lane

THE DIAGNOSIS FIRST, because it is the reusable half and the fix is only its consequence: an entire column of the Pyromancer's tree existed to mitigate an unfair passive. Seven of the eight INFERNO nodes read Overburn's Mana drain — Fire Walker reduced it, Invigorating Ashes offset it, Immolate doubled it, Kiln-Forged floored it, Ash Lung paid him for outrunning it, Cauterise billed it to health, and Forge Body threw the paid bill at an enemy. Only Heat Shimmer did not, and it read the drain's own counterpart, the bonus cap. A lane whose every node acts on one term is one node with eight prices — the shape Batch BC diagnosed and Batch BH proved — and it is worse when the term is a punishment, because then the lane is not a build, it is a payment plan. The drain is deleted and the lane is re-authored around a new thesis. This is a design change, not a tuning pass: no magnitude anywhere else in the game moves, and no save version moves (still v10).

§2 — Overburn, without the bill

Two clauses now, not three. BONUS — +2% damage per remaining Burn turn on the enemy team, capped at +40%, unchanged. REFUND — 1 Mana per turn of Burn he consumes, unchanged, and it keeps its property-of-the-passive status so anything later taught to eat Burn inherits it. DRAIN — deleted.

DELETED, NOT ZEROED, and that is the rule that stops a later batch reviving a mechanic by flipping a constant. _overburn_drain, _overburn_tick and the call from _player_turn are gone from the source; test_batch_bs asserts their NON-EXISTENCE rather than that they return zero. _drain_burn_turns went with them and _total_burn_turns is the single denominator again — the second one existed only because Ember Debt exempted an enemy from a bill that no longer exists. BattleUnit.ember_debt went with the exemption it served.

THE SPINE SURVIVES THE REMOVAL, and this is recorded rather than assumed lost. His damage is still DEFERRED: Burn ticks are small, Detonation pays 250% of the bank it empties, and turns spent lighting fires are turns not spent killing. The commitment is that he is weak until he cashes in — that was always the interesting half, and the drain was a punishment bolted onto it. Batch AR's "the reward caps and the cost does not" asymmetry dies with the cost; CLAUDE.md's Overburn block asserted it as load-bearing and was rewritten rather than amended, because a half-edited block is worse than either version.

ONE CLAUSE IS DROPPED RATHER THAN REHOMED, AND IT IS SAID PLAINLY: the damage cap can no longer be raised by anything. _overburn_capped is deleted too. Heat Shimmer (+20 cap points), Immolate and Cauterise-under-20-Mana were its only three lifters and all three were INFERNO clauses this batch re-authored. Raising the payoff's ceiling is Detonation's subject, not Inferno's, and every Detonation node is already authored — so rehoming it would have meant re-speccing a node outside this batch's scope. A later batch wanting a cap-raise authors one deliberately, which is the whole point of deleting the function instead of leaving it always-true. The 6% global Burn tick constant is still not touched, for Batch AR's reason: it is shared with enemies, runes and every other burn source in the game.

§3 — INFERNO: the fire that shields you

"How much heat you can stand" is meaningless with no heat. The new thesis is the one thing the Pyromancer most lacks and the one his theme most obviously supports: armoured in his own element — 135 HP, 85 Constitution, and until now nothing at all.

ALL EIGHT IDS SURVIVE AND RE-SPEC IN PLACE, EACH AT ITS OWN ROW. No new ids, none deleted, no save version move — a player holding a row-5 pick still holds a row-5 pick.

RowNodeEffect
1Ember ShroudWhile any enemy is Burning, he takes 8% less damage. Flat, on from turn one — the lane's opening statement, and the only one of the eight that does not scale with anything.
2Ashen Skin+25% fire resistance, and Burn ticks he applied heal him for 10% of the tick.
3Heat HazeEnemies attacking him while Burning have a 20% chance to miss.
4ImmolateRe-specced. 15 Mana, 1.5 int, 2cd, self: for 3 turns anything that strikes him is set Burning 3 turns and he takes 20% less damage (Perfect: a 4th turn).
5BackblastOnce per battle, on dropping below 40% health: every enemy is set Burning 4 turns and he heals 15% of maximum.
6Kiln-ForgedHe cannot be reduced below 1 health by any single hit while three or more enemies are Burning.
7Ash LungPer burning enemy he takes 4% less damage and deals 4% more. Uncapped.
8Forge BodyDamage taken is reduced 1% per remaining Burn turn on the field, capped at 50% — and the damage prevented is dealt to a random burning enemy as fire.

THE LANE'S SHAPE, PER BATCH BM'S RULE. Rows 1–7 are seven different KINDS of protection — flat mitigation, resistance and sustain, evasion, retaliation, an emergency, a death-refusal, and a scaling that pays both ways. Row 8 reads the accumulated quantity the lane built and converts it into offence, which is exactly the row-8 shape BM specified. No node is a larger magnitude of another, which is what the old lane failed.

Three notes that are decisions, not flavour.

  • Heat Shimmer's cap-raise left the lane and was DROPPED — see §2. The id (py_firebrand) stays at its row and becomes Heat Haze.
  • Immolate keeps its id and its ability slot and loses the drain clause entirely. Its "no damage cap" half goes too, since the cap is no longer this lane's business. What remains is retaliation plus mitigation, both pointing the same way — Batch AR's own standard for that node, read against a lane that now defends.
  • Forge Body reads burn TURNS, not burning enemies, and the difference is the node. Four enemies at four turns each is 16, and Firestorm alone puts 12–16 on the field in one cast. The 50% cap needs about fifty burn-turns and will rarely be reached. The designer has weighed the stack and it ships as written: at a realistic 20 burn-turns across four enemies, Ember Shroud, Ash Lung and Forge Body multiply to roughly 38% reduction on a 135 HP hero. Recorded so the arithmetic is on the page rather than in a conversation.

Batch AR's "no defensive option anywhere in kit or tree" assertion is RE-POINTED IN PLACE WITH ITS REASON, not deleted — an inversion, on the AV/BR precedent for a suite recording a decision a later batch reverses. It was correct while commitment meant no escape hatch and a punishing drain priced every fire; with the tax gone the absence stopped being the spec and started being a hole in the sheet. What the check asks now is that his defence is EARNED in the tree (his opening kit is still all fire) and that every mitigating node is in INFERNO rather than scattered.

§4 — Ember Debt, and the rest of the fallout

EMBER DEBT WAS RE-AUTHORED, NOT REPLACED. It was entirely a drain exemption — 8 turns of Burn on an enemy the bill ignored — and with no bill it was free Burn with no clause at all, which is not an ability. Its new axis is PAID BEFORE IT BURNS: 20 Mana, 2.0 int, 4cd, one enemy, 8 turns of Burn (Perfect: 12), and Overburn refunds every one of those turns immediately, as though he had already consumed them. The fire still burns its full term. Every other Burn payout in the kit EMPTIES the bank (Detonation eats one stack, Wildfire and Cinderfall skim the field); this is the deepest single-target Burn he can lay and the only card the passive pays for without consuming anything. It goes through _overburn_refund, the ONE door, which is Batch AR's own rule working: a fourth consumer arrived and inherited the refund — Crucible doubles it too — with no second implementation. test_batch_ar's call-site count is re-pointed 3 → 4, which is BO's pinned count doing its job rather than decaying.

THE RUNE AUDIT CAME BACK CLEAN, and it is recorded as a fact rather than a note, because a rune riding a counter that changed meaning fails silently — no error, no log line. No Pyromancer or Mage rune wrote any of the seven deleted fields, so nothing needed re-pointing and nothing was flagged for re-authoring. The four spec runes ride accelerant_ranks, conflagration_ranks, molten_ranks, supernova_ranks, blast_radius_ranks, rune_cinder_ember, rune_resist_pierce and the inert pyromaniac_ranks; every one of those counters was verified to still have a live read site.

  • The Rune of the White Flame's middle clause is still asserted INERT (Batch AR §4). This batch gave it no home — Overburn still has no per-burning-enemy step, and inventing one is the guess AR forbade — so the assertion is left unchanged rather than quietly re-pointed.
  • The Rune of the Blast Radius and the Rune of the Long Burn still read live fields — confirmed rather than assumed. They are rune-only content whose nodes are already gone, and their read sites survive.

THE AUTOPLAY MAGE POLICY KNEW A RULE THAT NOW REFERENCES NOTHING — "consume Burn when the drain exceeds Mana regeneration" (Batch AR §5). Replaced rather than merely dropped, or the bot would hold fire forever and never cash it: detonate the largest stack when it is worth more than waiting (four burn-turns on the ripest stack), and Wildfire when Detonation is cooling. Immolate is a defensive card now, so the bot arms it when there is something to retaliate against rather than when the field is already alight. Instrument honesty, not tuning.

Verified

Per the standing instruction, that means the code landed and works. No sweeps, no bands, no balance measurement was run, and none should be quoted from this batch.

check_parse 0 · check_flow 0 · run-harness gates 1/2/3 PASS · new test_batch_bs.gd 262/0, stable 3/3.

THIRTEEN NEGATIVE CONTROLS, each applied to product code and reverted (battle.gd, unit.gd and talents.gd all came back byte-identical by hash, and the suite to 261/0 after the last): the drain RESTORED as a turn-start bill trips 2; Forge Body reading burning BODIES instead of burn TURNS trips 1; Kiln-Forged with no board gate trips 1; Backblast without its once-per-battle flag trips 2; Ashen Skin healing on any burn tick rather than the one he applied trips 1; Heat Haze reading the defender's fire instead of the attacker's trips 2; Ash Lung paying only the dealt half trips 1; Ember Debt not paying the refund up front trips 3; Immolate lifting the damage cap again trips 1; Forge Body's throw not restoring the damage frame trips 1.

THREE OF THEM PASSED ON THE FIRST RUN, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE REASON TO RUN THEM. Each found a check that could not fail, and each was repaired rather than worked around:

  • Restoring the drain tripped NOTHING. The function-absence greps cannot see a bill written straight into _player_turn, which is precisely what a later batch reviving the mechanic would write. Three source-level assertions were added (the turn body still drips the regen, nothing in it takes resource away, and no CODE in it reads the passive) — with comments stripped first, because this batch's own tombstone in that function names the deleted bill on purpose and a bare grep would have failed against working code.
  • Deleting Backblast's once-per-battle flag tripped NOTHING, because the first fire leaves him at 45% health — ABOVE the 40% line — so the THRESHOLD was refusing the second call and the flag was never the thing under test. He is dropped back under the line first now, and the flag is the only thing that can refuse it.
  • Disabling Ash Lung's TAKEN half tripped NOTHING, and the diagnosis is worth keeping. The check compared one blow against one blow with a bare "<" — and with the term disabled the second blow still read SMALLER (34 against 32), because the ±10% variance roll drifts once the board differs and the two blows stop consuming the RNG identically. It was passing by luck, which is worse than failing. The term is now amplified for the measurement (20 points rather than the shipped 4, with the shipped value asserted separately off the payload) and the assertion is a RATIO with open ground between signal (~0.38) and noise (~0.94).

FULL BATTERY GREEN, every suite at 0 failures. Four counts moved and every one is explained: test_batch_ar 914 → 735 (its asymmetry loop shed its cost half — sixty-one iterations asserting a climbing drain), test_batch_bm 1890 → 1891 (its row-8 field loop walks one more field, because Ashen Skin honestly writes two), test_batch_bo 505 → 504 and test_rune_battle 96 → 97. Nothing else moved by one; an and bk sit inside their documented run-to-run drift. The designer had no run in flight and none was created — profile.json, relics.json and trees.json are all byte-identical by hash after the battery, and no --run was walked, because this batch measures nothing.

SEEN ONCE, NOT REPRODUCED: test_batch_br read 1441/1 on one battery pass and 1441/0 on four runs since; the failing check was not captured. Recorded rather than claimed fixed or diagnosed — nothing here is on the Warrior or Hunter class-card path.

FOUR SUITES RE-POINTED IN PLACE with the reason in each file, and five of the re-points are INVERSIONS — the honest treatment when a batch reverses a decision an older suite was guarding. test_batch_ar carries most of it (the asymmetry loop, the drain section, the chip, the Immolate check, _no_defence and the passive-desc check all invert; the refund call-site count goes 3 → 4) and reads 735/0, was 914 — the drop is the asymmetry loop shedding its cost half, sixty-one iterations that asserted a climbing drain beside a flat bonus. test_batch_bj's Cauterise line re-points onto Kiln-Forged's board gate; test_rune_battle's "the chip reads the LIVE drain" inverts to "the chip must not advertise a term that no longer exists" (97/0, was 96).

AND ONE WAS FOUND BY A COUNT RATHER THAN BY A FAILURE. test_batch_bo read 495/0 where Batch BR recorded 505: its Ember Debt section touches foes[0].ember_debt, which this batch deleted, so the section threw a SCRIPT ERROR, aborted its own function, and the suite still printed "0 failures" — the trap Batch BC documented. Nothing failed; ten checks simply stopped existing. Re-pointed onto the re-authored card and back to 504/0, one short of 505 because the two-denominator pair honestly collapses into one check. Read the counts, not only the failures.

LIVE AUTOPLAY CLEAN, 0 SCRIPT ERROR, with the re-specs reading correctly in ordinary fights: "Pyromancer: Immolate — -20% damage taken and attackers ignite (3 turns)", "Ember Debt — Orc Raider burns 8 turns, and Overburn pays the debt up front" immediately followed by "Overburn: 8 turns of Burn consumed refunds 8 Mana", and "Talent: Backblast — Pyromancer sets 5 enemies alight (4 turns) and takes back 20". Forge Body is visible reading burn TURNS rather than bodies, its throw climbing 1 → 2 → 4 → 5 across one fight as the field filled. No Overburn drain line appears anywhere — there is none to print. Kiln-Forged did not fire in any smoke and that is the branch being rare rather than a gap: it needs an actually lethal blow while three or more enemies burn. The suite drives it directly, which is where a rare branch belongs. NOTE the smoke's own artefact: DOD_SIM_ABILITIES applies its list to EVERY hero, so the Devout and the Beastmaster cast Ember Debt in the log — and only the Pyromancer's cast pays the refund, which is the passive's own guard working in plain sight.

2026-08-14 — Batch BR: the Hunter and Warrior class pools

Twelve class-wide abilities, six per class, closing the seam Batch BO opened and Batch BQ half-filled. All four CLASS_DRAFT_POOLS are populated now, so every hero's one-in-four class card draws a real entry and no class rolls an empty pool. The draft goes from 36 to 48 against a target of about 96 — tranches 2 and 3 are still owed, spec pools remain two deep, and an offer worn down by the no-return ledger will still fill short. Nothing else ships: no talent node, no magnitude, no existing ability changed, and no save version moves (still v10).

Two standing rules, set once

1. Charges and the effects that ride them count HITS, not casts. A multi-hit ability spends one charge per hit and fires its on-hit effects per hit. Aimed Volley is three shots; under Arcane Arrows it spends three of five charges and forks three times. Magic Missiles and Called Volley behave the same way. This is a real power increase for multi-hit abilities and it is deliberate — it is what makes a multi-hit kit and a charge bank a build rather than a coincidence.

Applied retroactively, and here is what actually changed. The rule was audited across every charge bank in the game:

  • Mirror Image (Batch BQ) now spends one image per hit of a multi-hit attack. The gate is multi_hits alone, which is what keeps the card's own promise true: a multi-hit ability is repeated strikes on ONE target, i.e. single-target, while an area attack, a random scatter and a chosen pair are not and still spend nothing. REPORTED: this changes nothing in play today. No enemy in enemies.json carries a multi_hits or random_hits ability, and heroes do not attack the Mage — so it is the rule made TRUE ahead of its use rather than a behaviour change, and the day an enemy gains a multi-hit blow it is already right. Asserted, not assumed: test_batch_br greps the roster for both fields.
  • Interpose's shield_charges, Waiting Guard's banked_guards and Feint's feint_guards ALREADY counted hits — verified at their sites rather than assumed. The block branch and the parry roll both sit inside the strike loop, so a three-hit blow already spent three. No change; the suite pins the positions.
  • ONE PLACE THE RULE WAS DELIBERATELY NOT APPLIED, and it is reported rather than buried: SPRAY OF ARROWS. Batch AZ gates it ab.multi_hits == 0 and ab.random_hits == 0 by design — spray is how many extra ENEMIES a single shot finds, and a multi-hit ability already finds its target three times. Firing it per hit would triple a shipped talent's magnitude, which is a balance change this batch's standing testing scope forbids measuring. The gate is pinned as a finding so a later batch has to read the reasoning first. The same shape applies to the Survivalist's post-loop on-hit package (Coated Blades, Venom Coating): it fires once per cast, and moving it inside the loop would both triple its output and change whether a MISSED or BLOCKED strike still applies it — a second, unasked change riding along.

2. A name is swept against the whole roster — every ability, talent node, status and rune — before it is authored. The Warrior recovery card was authored as Second Wind, which Holy already holds (tranche 1, Batch BO), and is Battle Trance here. That one was a true duplicate and had to be renamed: the resolver is keyed on display_name, so two abilities sharing one would make pool_ability answer the wrong question.

TWO MORE COLLISIONS FOUND, BOTH REPORTED AND BOTH SHIPPED AS SPECIFIED (the Batch BO Second Wind / Batch BP Precision Strike / Batch AV Shared Vigil precedent — a node's name is not an ability name, nothing resolves it, and renaming either is the designer's call and one string):

  • RALLY is also a Warden talent node (Banner row 2, +30% healing received for 2 turns), and there is a live rally status carrying that label. The ability needs no status of its own — it hands over a turn and is gone — so nothing can overwrite anything.
  • IRON WILL is also a Warden talent node (Threat row 3, −4%/rank damage taken per debuff) AND a live status with that exact label. This is the worst collision the project has had: same class, same spec reachable, so a Warden holding the node can draft the card. Nothing breaks, and the ability's own status is ironclad with its own chip precisely so a Warden holding both never sees two chips reading the same word.

The twelve, with their axes

CLASS_DRAFT_POOLS is 24 entries now (was 12); the defs live in Classes.draft_ability beside BO's, BP's and BQ's, resolved at the top of pool_ability as before. A drafted class ability lands in bm_abilities exactly as a spec one does, so battle spawn, the hero sheet, the rune filter and upgrade pairing pick it up with no new plumbing.

HUNTER — three spines that are all conditional (the Beastmaster needs a beast standing, the Sharpshooter needs to not have switched, the Survivalist needs statuses already on the board), in a class with no healing whatsoever and the thinnest defensive kit in the game:

  • Field Dressing (20 Mana, 2.0, 3cd, self) — heals 18% of maximum health and removes one harmful effect. The only self-heal a Hunter can get. The cleanse rides dispel_one_debuff, the existing On the Mend door, so "one harmful effect" means what it already means everywhere else.
  • Camouflage (20 Mana, 1.5, 4cd, self) — for 2 turns enemies are 70% less likely to target him. Buying time rather than absorbing damage.
  • Aimed Volley (20 Mana, 2.5, 3cd, one enemy) — three shots at 12% of Attack each, 8 Break damage a shot. The reliable strike, and being multi-hit is what makes it play with a charge bank.
  • Bola (15 Mana, 1.5, 3cd, one enemy) — Slowed and Crippled for 3 turns. Two statuses on one card, and nothing else at all.
  • Hunter's Mark (15 Mana, 1.5, 4cd, one enemy) — for 4 turns the whole party deals 15% more damage to it. Focus fire made explicit — the only party-wide amplifier the class has.
  • Arcane Arrows (25 Mana, 2.0, 5cd, self) — 5 charges: each of his next five HITS also strikes an additional random enemy for half that hit's damage. Banked, not timed, on the Interpose / Mirror Image precedent.

WARRIOR — three spines that are all melee, all Rage-driven and all reactive (the Berserker needs to be hurt, the Swordmaster needs a stance and a Break window, the Warden needs to be attacked), none of which can make something happen on turn one:

  • Battle Trance (15 Rage, 1.5, 4cd, self) — for 3 turns, at the start of each of his turns he heals 3% of maximum health plus half of all damage taken since his last turn. Recovery that scales with the beating.
  • Rally (15 Rage, 1.5, 4cd, one ally) — that ally acts NEXT, jumping to the front of the initiative order. Giving away tempo — the only ability in the game that hands an ally a turn.
  • Charge (20 Rage, 1.0, 3cd, one enemy) — 25% of Attack, 20 Break damage, and the target is Dazed for 1 turn; builds 30 Rage. Reaching something now. REPRICED BY THE DESIGNER IMMEDIATELY AFTER THE BATCH SHIPPED — see below.
  • Cleave (25 Rage, 2.5, 3cd, three chosen enemies) — 15% of Attack and 15 Break damage each. Breadth from a narrow class.
  • Warcry (20 Rage, 2.0, 5cd, party) — the whole party deals 20% more damage for 3 turns. The party buff the class lacks.
  • Iron Will (20 Rage, 1.5, 4cd, self) — for 3 turns he cannot be Stunned, Frozen, Dazed or Broken, and takes 15% less damage. Refusing to be stopped.

Iron Will's Break half is a CAP, not an immunity — decided, not left open

While it holds, his Break meter fills to 99 and no further. Pressure still accumulates and simply cannot cross, so the moment the trance ends he is sitting one hit from Broken. That makes it a delay rather than a negation, which is the better object: the enemy's three turns of Break work are not erased, they are deferred onto the turn he stops being immune.

The clamp is in unit.take_hit, BELOW the meter write and ABOVE the threshold, and that position is the whole decision. It is NOT implemented as "the meter cannot fill" (zeroing pressure_add up in the reducer block with Bulwark and Immovable) and NOT as "Broken is refused" (a guard on the broken = true line). Both read identically in a short test and neither leaves him at 99 afterwards — the first throws the pressure away, the second lets the meter sit at 100 and break on the very next point. test_batch_br asserts the 99, not the absence of Broken.

BROKEN IS DELIBERATELY NOT IN THE STATUS-REFUSAL LIST in _apply_status, which names exactly three ids (stunned, frozen, dazed). Broken is a Break-METER state written inside take_hit, not a status anyone applies; adding it to that list would look like the same rule and would quietly replace the delay with a negation. And force does not bypass the refusal: that argument is the boss carve-out bought by two perfects, and a hero who spent 20 Rage on three turns of not losing a turn is a different promise.

Three findings, reported and not re-tuned

CAMOUFLAGE AND GHILLIE SUIT STACK, AND THE CARD IS CLOSE TO A DEAD DRAW FOR A SURVIVALIST WHO ALREADY HOLDS THE NODE. §2 flagged the adjacency and asked which way it would resolve. They are combined as independent chances in one function, _evade_chance — 1 − (0.35 × 0.30) = 89.5% for a hero holding both, against 65% for the node alone. Neither silently overwrites the other and neither is summed past 100%. ONE combined roll rather than one each, because two sequential re-picks would let the second undo the first's choice. Measured: the card adds 24.5 points to a Ghillie build, against 70 to anyone else. Shipped as specified.

WARCRY OUT-SIZES BATTLE SHOUT ON ITS HEADLINE NUMBER, which fails §4's "class abilities are weaker than spec abilities" in the direction that matters. Warcry is +20% damage party-wide for 3 turns; Battle Shout — a Berserker SPEC-pool ability — is 8% base for 2 turns, 12%/3 or 18%/4 with its node. The term that keeps the spec card's ceiling above the class card's is the one Warcry has no answer to: Battle Shout adds 1% per 20 points of Bleed on the enemy party at cast time, so in the build it belongs to (a Bloodletting Berserker) it runs well past 20%. Shipped as specified per §4's instruction to confirm and REPORT; test_batch_br pins both numbers so a reprice has to read the reasoning first. The lever is one of Warcry's two numbers and it is the designer's.

CLEAVE IS NEARLY WAR STOMP, AND IT IS THE LESSER OF THE TWO — which is the direction §4 requires, but the adjacency is worth naming. Same 15% of Attack, same 15 Break damage, three targets each. War Stomp (a Warden spec-pool entry, also in CLASS_POOLS["warrior"]) costs 20 Rage against Cleave's 25 and refuels every ally on top. The distinction is CHOSEN three against RANDOM three — Cleave is choose_three, the stomp is random_hits: 3 and can land twice on one body. That is a real difference and the acquisition channels differ too (the stomp is a boss/spec pick, Cleave is drafted at elites, merchants and events), but this is the closest two cards in the draft have come since Batch BD found Deadfall duplicating Snare Trap, and it is recorded rather than discovered later.

Two decisions the brief did not make, and one correction toward the code

AIMED VOLLEY'S 25 BREAK DAMAGE IS A TOTAL, AND IT SHIPS AS 8 A SHOT — 24 across the volley. §4 names "Aimed Volley (25)" without the word "each" while naming "Cleave (15 each)" with it, so 25 is the card's figure rather than the shot's. pressure is applied PER HIT by the engine and 25 does not divide by three, so 8 a shot is the nearest integer split; it also lands level with Triple Shot's 8 a shot and 24 across three, which is the sibling the comparison was made against. Reading it as 25 per shot would have given a class card 75 Break against a Sharpshooter spec card's 24. The one-point deviation is stated rather than rounded away silently.

CHARGE WAS ASSIGNED 10 BREAK DAMAGE AND NO RAGE GENERATION, AND THE DESIGNER REPRICED IT TO 20 AND 30 IMMEDIATELY AFTER THE BATCH SHIPPED. BOTH THE ORIGINAL ARGUMENT AND WHAT THE REPRICE CHANGES ARE RECORDED, because the argument is what a later reader would otherwise re-derive. The batch's own assignment was BQ's general rule — the floor for a class ability is the FREE BASIC, not the spec kit — applied the other way round: Charge costs 20 Rage for 25% of Attack against Strike's free 23%, so what it bought was the 1.0 initiative (the fastest arrival in the kit, and a Warrior's only answer to a caster winding up) and the Daze, and it must not also win on Break. The other five Warrior cards carry no resource_gain, which is the cleanest statement of "weaker than spec work" a Rage class can be given: every Warrior spec ability builds 10-15 Rage while it spends, and those five spend without building.
