Leech		Blood Communion — Every point of Break damage the Occultist deals heals the lowest-health hero for 20% of its value.THE PARTY DRINKS FROM SOMETHING OTHER THAN HIS MARKS — his Break, which the Ruin lane spends rows building and which has never fed anybody.
Beastmaster
devotion		Kindred — At 8 or more Loyalty the beast's arrival effect fires again at the start of each of the Beastmaster's turns.LOYALTY BUYS A KIND RATHER THAN A QUANTITY — past a depth the arrival effect stops being something the beast DID and becomes something it DOES.
Beastmaster
pack		Instinctive Rotation — Swapping a companion no longer costs the Beastmaster his turn — he acts again immediately.ROTATION STOPS COSTING WHAT IT COSTS. BJ measured 0.05 swaps a trash battle; Wild Rotation had already removed the cooldown, so THE COST WAS NEVER THE COOLDOWN — it was the turn.
Beastmaster
handler		Ghost Pack — While no beast stands, the Beastmaster's attacks still draw a companion strike at 60% power.THE BEAST'S ABSENCE BECOMES AN ASSET — Vengeance inherits a dead beast's boon; this inherits its STRIKE.
Sharpshooter
Precision		Continuous Aim — Focus pays without a threshold: every point grants +0.6% damage, on top of everything the conversion already buys.THE CONVERSION POINT STOPS BEING A THRESHOLD — every point of Focus pays, above the split or below it.
Sharpshooter
Penetration		Sunder Shot — The armor the Sharpshooter ignores is also Constitution he ignores: a target resists his Break damage 100% less for each point of his armor penetration.ARMOR AND BREAK STOP BEING SEPARATE PROBLEMS — the armor he ignores is Constitution he ignores.
Sharpshooter
Tempo		Metronome — When Focus is spent it falls to 50% of what it was rather than to zero.THE METER SURVIVES WHAT CLEARS IT — Overkill covers the kill; this covers the PAYOUT.
Survivalist
Venom		Cocktail — At the start of a Poisoned enemy's turn its Poison gains a stack for every 2 OTHER statuses it carries.BREADTH BECOMES SELF-PROPAGATING WITHIN ONE ENEMY. BA's contagion reservation holds absolutely: nothing spreads between enemies or from a corpse.
Survivalist
Snares		Set and Forget — A sprung trap re-arms itself at the start of the Survivalist's next turn.REMOVES A CONSTRAINT — a trap is spent the moment it works, so the lane pays a turn for every spring it wants.
Survivalist
Guerilla		Practised Hands — Every ability the Survivalist uses takes 1 turn off all his OTHER cooldowns.THE COUNT ITSELF BECOMES THE WEAPON — Improvised pays for his first two actions; this pays for every one after them.

THE THREE WARRIOR LANE TRIPLES, read and reported for the next batch: Berserker — Bloodletting / Fury / Warpath. Swordmaster — Blade / Poise / Breaker. Warden — Plate / Threat / Banner.

ONE DEVIATION FROM A STATED DIRECTION, reported rather than glossed. The Cryomancer's Winter direction asked for accumulation that becomes self-sustaining without his casts. Eternal Winter, the Winter capstone, already occupies exactly that ground (every enemy gains a stack each of his turns) — the same collision the brief itself carved out for the Arcanist's Entropy against Perfect Conversion. A second consideration made it a decision rather than a preference: the open _hold_release/_hold_freeze recursion is reached by freezing more often, and a Winter node that raised freeze frequency would have widened a live crash. Winter's Depth converts Chilled into Break resistance instead — a currency the lane has never been able to buy.

§3 — The capstone shelf moves to row 9

Row 9 holds the three existing capstones per spec, unchanged in effect, every id in place, no re-specs and no magnitude changes. It sits behind difficulty 2, so a new player cannot take a capstone and reaching one is the meta arc's landmark. This also retires a long-standing measurement artefact: capstones stop being something the harness force-learns in row 8 as a matter of course.

§5 — The difficulty ladder

Vertical progression with nothing absorbing it means the game only gets easier. Three rungs, and the ladder is what rows 4–9 are for. Every scaling number is provisional and is the designer's.

RungEnemiesNamed twistOpens
1 — Wanderer×0.70nonetalent rows 1–3
2 — Warden×1.00the severity floor rises: the guaranteed mild bargain option may be severity 3talent rows 4–6
3 — Ruin×1.30the floor rises again (severity 4) and fixed encounters carry a modifier — the mini-boss and every boss, which today never dotalent rows 7–9

RUNG 1 IS TUNED BELOW THE PRESENT BALANCE, DELIBERATELY, AND THAT IS THE THING MOST LIKELY TO BE GOT WRONG. BK measured 39.3 / 43.3 / 21.3% completion with a full 8-node build; difficulty 1 is played with zero talents. So rung 1 is a new, easier tuning — it reuses Batch Y's existing ×0.70 rather than inventing a number — and rung 2 IS the present balance, byte for byte, so every BK row still describes it.

Run.difficulty was REUSED, not shadowed, and the check is recorded rather than assumed: it was already a String var, already saved with the run, already chosen at the draft and already armable from DOD_SIM_DIFFICULTY, and it already folded into exactly one multiplier at zone_base_mult. Every one of those is what the ladder needs, so what changed is the TABLE it keys into. Batch Y's two ids still resolve — "standard" maps to rung 2, the rung it was tuned at — so every old script and Matrix row still reads.

§6 — The fourth boss, and the reward table rewired

A run is 49 encounters now: 3 zones × 16, and then THE END BOSS as a seventeenth slot on the final zone's board. BK settled on 48. It ships as a placeholder — The Hollow Crown, statted as a hard boss, no bespoke mechanics. It is FIXED, not drawn from a pool, so it can be learned, and it gains stats AND mechanics with difficulty: a fourth ability at rung 2, a fifth at rung 3. That is a filter over authored content (Enemies.config(kind, rung)), not a second kit, and every other enemy in the roster reads identically at every rung. It awards a relic, always, and no talent points. Beating it is what opens the meta tree's row tiers.

IN-RUN TALENT POINTS ARE DELETED, NOT ZEROED, per the standing rule. Gone with them: Run.award_talent_points, Run.award_spec_point, member["talent_points"], member["talent_flex"], Talents.can_learn, Talents.purse_for, Talents.points_spent, MAX_PER_ROW, the hero sheet's spend path, the map card's points badge and the sim's earned/spent/banked report line. Run.tally never had a talent counter — checked rather than assumed; the brief expected one.

EVERY HISTORICAL FIGURE THOSE SITES PRODUCED IS SUPERSEDED, BK's 10.9 / 10.8 / 6.0 talent points per hero per run included. They measured a per-run purse that no longer exists. Do not compare any post-BM number against them.

Three consequences the brief did not enumerate, each re-specced rather than left with a dead hook. The two relics granting start_talent_points — Waystone Shard and Horn of the First War — bought a purse that no longer exists; both keep their id, tier and "you start ahead" role with a different opening currency. The events verb talent_points went with its handler, and the five effect instances across Blood Altar, Ancient Training Grounds and Echo of the Warden are re-pointed at attack_pct, the closest live effect in kind on the same target selectors. Magnitudes there are provisional.

§7 — What it measures, and NOTHING WAS TUNED AGAINST ANY OF IT

n=50 a row, balanced route, default first-lane builds (BG's standing confound applies). DOD_SIM_ROWS is new and says how many of the nine rows the equipped loadout fills, which is what makes "the build that rung expects" measurable at all.

Rowrungrows equippedcompletionsdepth (of 49)ratio@z1t8
THE GATE NUMBER — a fresh save1012%32.46 ±1.361.28
a second-run player1352%43.44 ±1.071.26
the build rung 2 expects260%31.82 ±1.560.91
the build rung 3 expects3918%34.38 ±1.890.67
EIGHT picks, matched rung288%34.78 ±1.500.88
NINE picks, matched rung2968%45.18 ±0.930.90

THE GATE NUMBER IS 12%. That is difficulty 1 with a party carrying no talents at all — the state a fresh save is in, and the gate the entire meta layer sits behind. It is reported and not corrected; whether 12% is the right number for a first run is a designer's call, and the lever is rung 1's multiplier, which is one float in one place. The second row is what a player faces once their first completion has bought them rows 1–3: 12% → 52% for three points. The meta layer is doing something.

THE NINE-PICK ROW IS THE FINDING OF THE BATCH, AND IT IS BIGGER THAN §7 EXPECTED. Eight picks and nine picks at the SAME rung read 8% and 68%, depth 34.78 and 45.18. The ninth pick is the CAPSTONE (row 9 is the shelf), so what this measures is that a capstone is worth more than the eight lane nodes beneath it put together — and depth, the low-variance metric, says the same thing at a tenth of the noise. That is the number §7 asked for ("nine picks is stronger than anything the AJ–BA pricing ever assumed, and it is now reachable"), answered, and it was not tuned against. Two honest caveats travel with it: the harness's build is a PURE LANE, so its capstone is the one its seven rows were authored to feed — a real player's mixed build will read lower; and this is one measurement of one construction, not a re-pricing brief.

DOES THE LADDER ABSORB THE TREE? NOT YET, AND THE SHAPE OF THE MISS IS LEGIBLE. The three rung-matched rows read 52% / 0% / 18%, which is not monotone in either direction. Read against the ratio column — 1.26 / 0.91 / 0.67, clean and monotone — the ladder is unambiguously steeper at every rung, so the non-monotone completions are the TREE moving, not the ladder failing to. Specifically: rung 2 with rows 1–6 is the floor of the whole table at 0%, and rung 3 with the full nine beats it at 18% on a harder rung. Rows 7–9 are where the tree's power lives, and rung 2 asks for a build that stops one row short of them. Whether the fix is the rung's multiplier, the tier boundary, or the capstone's price is a design decision with three plausible answers and no measurement yet distinguishing them. NOTHING WAS CHANGED. At n=50 the resolvable difference on completions is roughly ±14 points, which is BK's own standing caution — never publish a three-row completions verdict off n=50 — and it is why the sentences above lean on depth and ratio rather than on the percentages.

BH's fifteen-point leave-one-out grid on a compounding row 8 was NOT run, and that is a gap rather than a finding. The lane it is owed on is named in the code: Harmonic Convergence (Arcanist, Resonance row 8), because AT §3 measured that build rate beats per-stack value QUADRATICALLY on a triangular curve, and a node whose whole subject is the build rate is the most dangerous shape in the batch. The harness is one DOD_SIM_TALENTS string with the id withheld.

§9 — Tests

NEW test_batch_bm.gd, 1890 checks / 0 failures. Structure (every tree 9×3 plus 3 capstones, every id present and disjoint across the roster, valid condition references, and no row-8 node duplicating a stat field above it in its own lane); earning; spending; gating; persistence; and the six negative controls the brief names, each built as broken state and proven to be rejected — a purchased cell auto-equipping, points transferring between specs, a per-spec row tier, an in-run talent award, equipped talents changing mid-run, and a row-8 node that is a row-4 node with a bigger number.

Regression: SIX SUITES RE-POINTED IN PLACE with the reason in each file (test_batch_ai — whose gating, elite-crack and economy sections described the purse BM deleted and are REPLACED by test_batch_bm's rather than half-repaired — plus aj, bc, bg, bh and bi). VERIFIED: check_parse 0 · 13 scenes 0 SCRIPT ERROR · a full --run walks clean and reaches the new end boss.

2026-08-12 — Batch BL: enemy intent, and a recap that says what killed you

Two features, both extensions of machinery that already existed rather than new systems. No difficulty tuning in either: BK set new baselines two batches ago, §1 was expected to move them, and this batch measures and reports rather than correcting.

The headline — "I was at 40 and it hit for 52"

When a hero falls, the run summary now names what struck last, from whom, for how much, and what their health was before it landed — for every hero who fell, for the whole run and again for the final battle alone. "I died" becomes a sentence a player can act on. That last number is the difference between a run that felt unfair and a run that taught the player something.

§1 — Enemy intent

Every enemy shows what it is about to do. An enemy picks its next action the moment its turn ends — every enemy picks once before the first turn — and that stored choice is displayed until it resolves. Two surfaces: an icon under the enemy's portrait in the turn bar (on its next turn's slot only) and an icon plus a written line on its nameplate, so a player watching the battlefield never has to read the bar.

Seven categories, and they are data-driven rather than a table of names — an enemy added to enemies.json classifies itself. Strike (single-target damage), Sweep (hits the party or several of it), Crush (more Break damage than health damage), Afflict (applies a status, and names it), Bolster (buffs, wards or cleanses its own side), Mend (heals), Wind-up (a charged blow with its turns remaining).

This retroactively upgrades twelve spines. The Cryomancer's hold, the Sharpshooter's patience, the Warden's threat, Break itself, Stun, Freeze and the three madness statuses all exist to deny an enemy its turn — and the payoff of every one of them used to be an event with no visible content. Freezing an Ash Hurler mid-charge always read as a win because Batch V made the wind-up say its name; freezing an Orc Brute a turn before its Overhead Crush read as nothing, and was worth about the same. The difference was a label.

A declared action can be denied, and that is the point. Three re-validation cases, in a fixed order: the target is gone → the enemy re-targets within the same ability (the plan holds, only the victim changes); the ability cannot be paid for → it falls back to the basic attack and the log says so, because a silent substitution is the intent system lying; the enemy cannot act at all (Stunned, Frozen, Broken, held) → the declaration is discarded, not banked. Banking it would mean a stun delayed the blow instead of deleting it, which would take the payoff back out of the twelve spines. Hysteria, Bewitch and Psychosis take the turn the same way.

Nothing about enemy behaviour changed — only when the choice is made. The selection logic was lifted out of the turn handler unaltered. What did move is that state-sensitive policies now read the board a turn earlier: the whole enemy support suite (Healing Wave under 40%, Regenerate, Cleansing Rite, Shielding, Wild Growth, Dark Vigil), the wounded-hero and taunt picks, the Savage Presence and Ghillie Suit rolls, and — most sensitively — the Broken-hero exploit, since a Break window is short and a declaration made before one opens will not take it. Reported, changed nowhere.

The wind-up is now the same mechanism rather than a second one. Batch V's charge was already a declared action landing on a later turn; it stores its blow in the same single place every other declaration does, and the charging status survives only as the chip and the cancel hook. A charging enemy declares once, not twice, and a cancelled charge counts as exactly one denial.

NO PREDICTED DAMAGE NUMBER IS SHOWN, and that is the batch's main finding rather than an omission. The brief required any number to come from the same code the real hit runs, dry. That code cannot be: the strike path is ~680 lines that mutate state as they go and write to three separate ledgers, and its very first line rolls damage variance — with a critical rolled inside it — so the same call with identical inputs returns a different number. A preview worked out a second way is a tooltip with a shorter fuse than the thirty-eight Batch BJ found drifted. The category and the ability's own name are shown instead, read straight off the declaration.

§2 — The recap

Batch Z's run summary reported damage share per hero. It could not say how, and it said nothing at all about damage taken. Three new breakdowns, each reported for the whole run and again for the final battle alone — the run totals say what the build was doing, and only the final-battle block says what happened in that last fight:

  • Damage dealt, by ability — per hero, their top five with totals. The split always sums to the flat per-hero total beside it.
  • Damage taken, by source — per hero, the total and their top five sources, named as enemy kind / ability. Attribution is by kind, so three Shieldmasters aggregate into one row rather than three. Indirect damage is included and attributed to whoever caused it: status ticks to the unit that applied them; Blood Price, Dark Pact, Cauterise, the Overburn drain and recoil marked as the hero's own. A hero killed by his own Overburn drain reads as killed by his own Overburn drain.
  • The killing blow — the headline above.

All of it goes into the same line list the Copy summary button serialises, so the clipboard version stays complete — a recap on screen but not in the paste is half a feature. And none of it is defeat-only: a completed run wants to know which ability carried it as much as a wipe wants to know what ended it.

The save version rises to 9, tolerantly — unlike BK's refusal, an older save loads and simply starts the new counters mid-run at zero, because these are counters rather than structure. Each per-hero map is bounded at 24 rows, with anything past that folded into a single "(other)" row, so no future ability draft can quietly grow the save file; the per-hero totals stay exact whatever the bound does to the rows.

A real hole closed on the way past. Hero damage-over-time ticks — a Pyromancer's Burn, a Survivalist's Poison — were credited only in the simulation harness, so in real play two specs whose whole lane is damage-over-time were missing from the summary's damage share entirely. Simulation totals are untouched (that path already counted them), so no baseline moves.

What was measured

The three re-validation rates, over 150 complete runs (50 per route policy, 50,799 declarations):

  • Re-targeted: 3.2 / 3.5 / 5.9% (greedy / balanced / cautious). Fine, and expected — the intent the player planned against held; only the victim changed.
  • Fell back to the basic attack: 0.0% in all three. This is the number that matters: §1's rule is that a common fallback means the declaration is happening too early, and the answer would be to declare later rather than to widen the fallback. It is not happening.
  • Discarded: 16.1 / 15.1 / 11.2% — almost entirely the Cryomancer's holds (3,014 / 2,598 / 1,365) with Break the small remainder (103 / 94 / 162), and zero plain stuns or freezes, because in this party the Cryomancer's freeze always becomes a hold. One in six declared enemy actions never happens, and that is the twelve spines being paid.
  • Hijacked by madness: 0.0%, and this is a hole in the measurement rather than a result: the standard test party carries no Occultist, so Hysteria, Bewitch and Psychosis were never applied. The counter exists and is asserted in the suite; it has not been exercised at scale.

Completions across the three route policies, against BK's 39.3 / 43.3 / 21.3 for greedy / balanced / cautious: greedy 52%, balanced 46% (±13.8 pts at n=50) and cautious 20% (±11.1). All three inside their bands — but greedy is +12.7 points and sits near the edge of its own, so it is reported as a signal rather than as noise. The bot does not plan, so it cannot benefit from seeing an intent; if this is real, the mechanism has to be the one behavioural shift §1 knowingly introduced — enemies now choose a turn earlier, and the most state-sensitive choice they make is the Broken-hero exploit. An enemy that declared before a Break window opened will not take it, which is a small unearned favour to the party. Named as the hypothesis, measured, and corrected nowhere: BK's baselines are two batches old, and tuning against a number in the batch that moves it is how four consecutive Devout batches went wrong.

Two things deliberately not built

Hidden intent — an enemy whose intent is concealed, making information itself a resource the player fights for. A good mechanic and a different one; it belongs after the baseline is legible. An AI rewrite — the selection rules are untouched by construction. Both recorded as not built rather than missed.

2026-08-12 — Batch BK: the branching map

The largest structural change since Batch AN. A run is a generated map again — 3 zones × 16 slots, three rows wide — with two new node types, re-specced events, and the first save-breaking change since AN. No difficulty tuning: every number below is a new baseline, and tuning against a baseline in the batch that creates it is how the last four Devout batches went wrong.

The headline — 12.9 real decisions a run, against Batch BG's 0

Batch BG measured route agency on Batch AN's line and reported 0% of 2,764 steps offering a real choice. On the same definition — two or more nodes reachable and not all of them the same type — the generated map reads 41% of steps, 10.8–13.1 a run (n=150 per policy), out of the 42 branching steps a full clear walks. Reachable nodes per step goes from 1.00 to 1.61.

The mechanism is not a rule. Edges reach the same row or an adjacent one, they never cross, and 59% of nodes have a single exit — so a step down a row leaves the corridor above unreachable for a measured 2.23 columns. Nothing is sealed and nothing is announced. On the map screen a node this route can no longer reach is drawn nearly out, which is the only way a player sees what the last step cost.

§1 — What a zone is now

3 zones × 16 slots = 48 encounters (was 36). Slots 1–7 branch three wide, slot 8 is the mini-boss and every path converges on it, slots 9–15 branch again, slot 16 is the boss. Any of column 1's three nodes may be entered, and the mini-boss opens the whole of column 9 the same way, so each half of a zone begins with a free pick and then narrows. 14 branching columns × 3 rows = 42 positions; pruning removes two or three, so a real zone holds ~39.7 nodes and its columns are 2 or 3 wide (84% are 3; none is ever 1).

Copies per zone: 6 elites, 6 blacksmiths, 5 merchants, 5 events, fights filling the rest (~17.8). An unsteered route walks 6.25 fights, 2.24 elites, 2.04 blacksmiths, 1.73 merchants and 1.74 events — measured over 1,500 generated zones, and dead on the arithmetic the design was specified from.

NO NODE TYPE IS GUARANTEED ON A ROUTE. Route past every blacksmith and you get no blacksmith that zone: it happens in 7.7% of zones under an unsteered walk, and a zero-elite route happens in 6.1%. That is the design — it is what makes the trade route a choice rather than a milestone, and it is what makes skipping every elite an underpower strategy rather than a safe one.

The brief says 49 encounters. It is 48: 3 × 16, by the same construction that made the old number 36 (3 × 12), since the third zone's boss is the end boss.

§2 — Generation, and why there is no retry loop in it

The edge graph is generated first; types are assigned to the positions that survive. Every node has 1–3 edges in and out (measured out-degree 59/37/4% for one/two/three), every edge lands on the same row or an adjacent one, no two edges cross, and nodes with no way in or out are removed to a fixpoint — but never below two per column, because a one-node column is a step with no decision on it.

Nothing is rolled and thrown back. Every legal edge assignment for a column is enumerated and one is drawn weighted; assignments that would strand a column are never in the bag. Elite columns come from the standard non-adjacent-subset bijection, so an illegal spread cannot be named, let alone rejected. This is Batch AN's own severity-floor argument reused: a retry budget works until the table's shape changes, and then it fails invisibly.

Type constraints: no elite in column 1; no two elites in adjacent columns (the columns run 1–14 straight through the mini-boss, so elite → mini-boss → elite is barred too); at most one of each non-fight type per column; and every entry node reaches at least one elite and at least one trade node — 0 failures in 4,500 entry nodes.

§3 — The Blacksmith: the gold sink the economy has been missing since AO

Batch BJ measured 63% of every run's gold going unspent, with 13.6 shop-rune refusals a run for no free slot against 6.8 for the gold reserve: slots were the binding constraint, not prices, so no amount of extra shop stock could ever have fixed it. A blacksmith consumes no slot.

It sells the ability upgrade Batch AP already built — three pairings on the counter, one hero each, drawn through AP's own eligibility filter so Honed is never offered on a heal. Gold only, no HP cost and no second currency, because the economy needed a pure sink. One purchase ends the visit: it is not a shop you clear. Price scales with the zone: 150 / 225 / 300, set from BJ's measured numbers rather than by guess (1,505 dead gold a run at 36 encounters scales to ~2,050 at 48; six visits at these prices is 1,350).

AP's once-per-run rule does not apply here — that rule governs the mini-boss pick pool, and a blacksmith is not that pool. A player may buy the same upgrade type more than once in a run on different abilities; the never-twice-on-one-ability rule still holds. The subtle half of that, which would have shipped unnoticed: a bought upgrade goes into the same list the mini-boss award writes (that is what makes it land, wear its ◆ and hover identically), and that list is what the once-per-run rule reads — so without a flag, buying Honed in zone 1 would silently have deleted Honed from that hero's mini-boss offers for the rest of the run. The flag has exactly one reader.

§3 — The Merchant becomes a node you can see coming

The Peddler used to appear after a won fight, 40% of the time with a four-fight drought floor. It is a map node now — five a zone — so a shop is something a player routes toward with gold in hand rather than something that happens to them. Same stock. The 40% roll, the 25% event roll, the drought counter and the post-fight queue are all deleted, not left unreachable. The one survivor is the severity-4 bargain's "a merchant follows the fight", kept because it is bought rather than rolled.

§4 — Events become a trading floor

Events were gifts, and a gift node has nothing to think about. They are resource conversions now, in three kinds that share one icon and one colour. The player knows an event is there; they do not know which kind, because a visibly-bad node is a wall with extra steps rather than a gamble — the map screen cannot read an event's kind at all.

  • Tradeoff, 60% — two or three conversions of one resource into another, at a rate taken or declined. Health for a rune, gold for maximum health, health for talent points, gold for a relic. Declining is always one of the buttons. Eight authored: every existing tradeoff gained a second, deeper conversion beside its first.
  • Boon, 25% — a straight benefit, no cost. Eight authored.
  • Bane, 15% — a straight detriment, one button. Four new: Ashfall (12% of maximum health), the Cutpurse (a quarter of the purse), Spoiled Stores (two potions), Wormrot (−5% maximum health for the run). No bane can kill a hero or take anyone below 1 HP — a bane you could not have avoided is only fair if it costs a resource, never a run.

The kind is drawn first, at 60/25/15, and the weight inside that kind picks the entry, so authoring a ninth boon will not make boons commoner. 20 events ship, against ~5 met per run. One new effect verb, rune_grant, for §4's own worked example.

Reported, not built: §4 also suggests trading a held rune for two of lower rarity. That needs a rune to be surrendered, and nothing in this project has ever removed a rune from a hero — the equipped flag has one writer and the pouch only grows. Building a removal path for one event would put a second, untested writer beside the one careful one.

§5 — The map screen, rebuilt

A three-row lattice with drawn edges, reading left to right inside a clipped viewport with a horizontal scrollbar, so the whole zone can be read before the first step. The four hero cards moved to the left edge, stacked, and the right two thirds of the screen belongs to the map. Four node states, and the fourth is the new one: cleared is dim green with its edges lit gold, a node the party may step onto wears its full type colour and is larger, the road ahead recedes grey, and a node this route can no longer reach is drawn nearly out. The scroll position is held across redraws and centres on the party's own column when the screen opens.

The card lost 74 pixels of height in the move, so an owed ability, upgrade or rune pick is now a CHOOSE button opening a three-choice overlay — one implementation for all three, where there were three near-identical inline rows, and each choice shows its full description under it rather than behind a hover.

The tier ramp was rescaled and the enemy stat ladder was not, and the two decisions are different on purpose. The budget ramp now spans 16 slots with both ends held exactly where Batch AN left them (slot 1 opens 3–5, slot 15 tops at 8–10, boss band untouched at 10–12); keeping the old slope would have run slot 15 into the boss's own band, which is a structural break rather than a preference. The enemy ladder's +2% attack / +2.5% HP is a rate, not a curve with endpoints, so it simply runs four more steps — slot 16 reads ×1.40 HP / ×1.32 attack against the old slot-12 top of ×1.30 / ×1.24, and the party's own +2%-a-win ladder gained the same four steps.

Bargains per run became variable, as intended: they gate on elites and mini-bosses, and elites are routed to rather than placed. Measured 8.4 a run greedy, 8.1 balanced, 3.8 cautious (n=150).

Reported, not taken: §5 asks for the hero sheet to show "current talents (not changeable mid-run)". Talents are still spent in-run — the meta layer that locks them before a run does not exist yet — and making the sheet read-only would have deleted the entire in-run talent economy, which is not a change this batch was asked for.

§6 — Save v8, and pre-v8 saves are refused

A v7 save holds a flat 12-slot line and a single slot index. There is no honest place for that on a lattice: the migration would have to invent a row the party never stood in and a set of edges they never had, on a map generated after the fact. Refused and cleared, which is the call Batch AN made about pre-v7 saves and Batch AI made about ranked talent purchases. The brief calls this the first save-breaking change in the project's history; it is the second.

§7 — The new baselines, and the question the map exists to ask

Every figure here is a new baseline. 48 encounters is a third more than 36, so Batch BG's 52.3% completion figure is not comparable and is not a regression when a number below it appears. Three route policies, default first-lane builds, n=150 pooled per policy:

RouteCompletionsDepth (of 48)Elites walked Talent points / heroGold earnedBlacksmith gold
greedy (elite first)39.3% ±7.830.70 ±1.376.47 10.91963931 (47%)
balanced43.3% ±7.932.11 ±1.356.00 10.81930904 (47%)
cautious (elite last)21.3% ±6.626.24 ±1.222.03 6.01226508 (41%)

Does the greedy route win? No — and it does not lose either. Greedy and balanced are indistinguishable at 0.70σ on completions and 0.73σ on depth. Both beat cautious decisively: +18.0 points at 3.46σ and +22.0 points at 4.19σ respectively, and +4.5 / +5.9 slots of depth. So the elites are not underpaid — ducking every one of them is unambiguously the worst way to play, which is exactly what §1 said a zero-elite route should be. What the batch cannot yet say is whether hunting them pays, because the two policies that tolerate elites turned out to be only 8% apart in elite exposure (6.47 walked against 6.00): the axis is flat where they sit, and resolving its top end wants either a much larger n or a policy pair further apart than these two. That is named as a next step rather than closed.
One caution on reading the table: gold, talent points and bargains all scale with depth, so cautious earning less of everything is mostly a survival consequence rather than an economy finding. Per slot actually walked, the three read 0.36 / 0.34 / 0.23 talent points and 0.21 / 0.19 / 0.08 elites — there the elite economy is doing plainly what it was built to do.
And one on the instrument itself, because it caught us: at n=50 these same three policies read 30% / 50% / 18%, which would have been written up as "balanced wins by 20 points". At n=150 it is 39% / 43% / 21%. The n=50 spread was noise, and the report's own resolution block said so before the number was believed.

The economy, re-measured with the merchant's new frequency and the blacksmith both live. Unspent gold at run end falls from Batch BJ's 63% to 25–28%. The blacksmith alone converts 41–47% of all income; the route walks 6.5–7.9 of them a run and buys 2.4–4.1, the rest refused for want of gold — which is a sink doing exactly what a sink is for.

Tests

test_batch_bk: ~130 checks (the count drifts by one or two run to run, because two of its loops iterate over columns whose width the generator decides) — 1,000 generated maps asserting every degree, adjacency, crossing, orphan, pruning-floor, copy-count, elite-placement and entry-reachability invariant; reachability contiguity; the blacksmith's offer, price, purchase and pool independence; the three event kinds, their weights and their declinability. Plus a suite-level check that all eight sections reached their last line, because a GDScript error mid-function aborts that function and the suite still prints "0 failures".

Seven negative controls, each applied to the product code and reverted (the suite came back to 0 failures after the last): crossing edges permitted trips 2, an AN-style key-route guarantee trips 3, a restored post-fight merchant roll trips 4, a purchase consuming a mini-boss pick trips 1, has_upgrade counting bought entries trips 2, a bane allowed below 1 HP trips 1, and the map screen leaking an event's kind trips 1.

One of those seven is worth reporting on its own, because the attempt failed interestingly. Rigging a route guaranteed to hit a blacksmith was tried twice and caught both times — but by the graph and copy-count invariants rather than by the route check written for it. The reason is structural: with a floor of two nodes per column, one of each non-fight type per column, and no orphans, no node type can be made unavoidable at a single column at all. A guarantee has to be a whole-route repair pass, which is exactly what Batch AN's _ensure_key_route was and exactly what this generator does not have. The route check is therefore a measurement rather than a trip-wire, so it ships paired with its own companion: a route that tries to duck every blacksmith is sometimes forced onto one anyway, which is what proves the avoid-walk is doing real work rather than always returning zero.

Re-pointed rather than deleted: test_batch_an's line section and its scheduling section (both now pin the absence of what AN built), test_batch_ah's mini-boss unavoidability (a reachability walk again rather than a fact about an authored array), test_batch_ai's and test_run_harness's talent arithmetic (a range now, because the purse is a function of the route). Full battery green.

2026-08-11 — Batch BJ: the full audit

No save version moves (still v7). No magnitude moves, no node re-specced, no system redesigned. What shipped: deletions of unambiguous dead code, one deduplication, one sim-harness speedup, and every tooltip, rune text, and glossary entry corrected toward the code where the two disagreed.

§2 — The tooltip audit, leading, because the count is the headline

Thirty-eight discrepancies, against the four found by accident in twenty batches: 13 talent tooltips, 7 ability descriptions, 6 rune texts, 11 glossary entries, and one block of authored enemy text no screen displays. All text is fixed toward the code. Two were not text problems but MISSING MECHANICS, reported rather than silently implemented:

Unbroken Watch's tooltip lied outright — it promised +2 Loyalty on a no-damage turn while the code reads the field as a gate and pays a fixed +1; the magnitude was never read anywhere. The tooltip says +1 now (behavior unchanged); paying more is a design decision. Reality Fracture's perfect ("An Arcanist also banks 1 Resonance") has no implementation at all — and never could have been seen: the ability is vault inventory only reachable through the retired class draw. Pinned inert, the White Flame pattern.

The rest in brief: three Beastmaster tooltips described baselines that never existed ("rather than halving it" — nothing halves; "rather than per two" — no per-two exists; "rather than for a few turns" — a dead boon simply ends); Sacred Covenant triggers on ANY shield saving a life, not only Divine Shield, and now says so; Consecrated Ground's tooltip finally states the Faith drip that is two thirds of all Faith the lane generates; Call of the Wild claimed 15% strikes for beasts that actually make their own full strikes; two Hunter texts read as flat damage where the number is a percent of Attack; four "new ability" nodes now state what they grant when the ability is already owned; five rune texts understated their own payloads; eleven glossary entries still taught systems deleted batches ago (ceil(N/3) talent pricing, 1/2/3 points per fight, rests, rune slots that grow by zone, 10-tier maps with routing, three-spec trophies). One spelling: Ricochet.

§3a — The signature-payoff table: how often the fantasy actually happens

Every sim report now ends with this table. Rotated full runs, n=100, default first-lane builds, per battle:

The question this table was built to answer: boss-only payoffs are TWO SPECS, not a pattern. Ten of twelve signatures fire in ordinary fights. The Occultist is boss-tilted by explicit design (5.4x). The Beastmaster's swap — the verb his capstone lane is named for — is nearly absent everywhere (0.05/battle): the bot's swap rule needs both slots full and a 1.25x better boon. Reported for the design review, not tuned.

§3c — The run economy, finally visible

Default party, n=100, completions 53% (inside BG's band): gold earned 2386 / spent 881 / dead 1505 per run — 63% of income is never spent. Items used 10.7/run against 8.6 carried unused at the end; merchants met 9.8/run; bargains 6.6/run at severity 3.67. The answer to AO's open question: income is not the constraint and neither are prices. The binding refusals are SLOTS — 13.6 shop runes refused per run for "no free slot" against 6.8 for the 40g reserve. The economy generates roughly 2.7x what the bot can find worth buying (with the standing caveat that sim acquisition floors a bot's policy, not a human's).

§1 — Dead code deleted, the sync deduplicated, the sim 5.3x faster

Deleted, each pinned absent by test: five unit fields whose writer or reader died in old re-authors (below_half_last, was_frozen, companion_hp_bonus, infusion_ranks, chain_reaction_ranks), six functions with zero callers (relic_active, slot_type, owed_rune_picks, modifier_name, owed_upgrade_picks, Runes.generate_spec), the never-applied "bleed" status registry row, and the last rest-machinery survivors in code (a tally key with no writer, a summary line that could only print 0, a report row for a slot type the line cannot deal). Kept deliberately, each now asserted present: the AY half-growth rule, CLASS_POOLS, the whole ability vault, the rune-only counter families, and the two dead rest_heal_add relic hooks (re-speccing a relic is design).

The victory sync is one implementation now — BattleUnit.sync_victory_state, called by both the real victory branch and the harness; the two copies whose comments begged each other not to drift are gone. The sim harness is 5.3x faster: headless Godot was sleeping two thirds of every sim's wall clock between frames; sim.sh passes --fixed-fps now and nothing the sim computes changed (100 battles: 88.5s → 16.7s, identical stats). A 100-run measurement is ~8 minutes.

2026-08-10 — Batch BI: the two Faith axes stop fighting each other

No save version moves (still v7); every id survives. §1 is a change to what the resource IS — the held half of Conviction reads the highest Faith you have held this battle rather than what you hold right now. §2 is a decomposition of where Faith actually comes from, with one rate change shipped beside it.

§2 — Where the Faith comes from, leading, because it is the deliverable

BH left releases at 0.4 a battle and nothing in the project could say which source was dry. Every Devout sim row now prints the whole table. Full eight-node lane, all four built, n=200, BC/BE's lineup — Faith gained per battle by source:

sourceFaith/battleshareits denominator
Consecrated Ground drip9.166% ground up on 54–56% of hero turn-starts (9.1 of 16.6)
Divine Shield absorbs3.223% 1.5 absorbed hits a battle, at 2.05 Faith each
Binding Oath0.705%ally releases (0.73/battle)
Communion0.685%ally releases
Sacred Covenant0.03—lethal saves
opening rune0.00—not in this build
total13.8 of which 3.4 lands on the Devout's own non-releasing meter

THE BRIEF GUESSED THE GROUND, AND THE GUESS IS RIGHT ABOUT WHICH SOURCE MATTERS AND WRONG ABOUT WHY. It expected the ground to be the dry one — a 3-turn cooldown against a 2-turn duration. The ground is up 55% of the time, close to that guess, but it is 66% of all Faith and the only thing keeping the lane alive. The genuinely dry source is absorbs, at 1.5 absorbed hits a battle.

AND THE REAL BOTTLENECK IS NEITHER RATE. About 10.4 Faith a battle reaches allies, split across THREE of them — 3.5 each — against a release threshold of 5, in a fight that gives an ally roughly four turns. The average ally cannot fill the meter in an average fight. 0.73 releases × 5 = 3.65 Faith actually reaching a payout: 35% of what lands, the rest stranded as partial stacks when the battle ends.

§2 — The rate change, and the next lever named with its number

Conviction pays 2 Faith per absorbed hit, up from 1. §2 said outright it expected this to be insufficient, and it is: releases went 0.42–0.49 → 0.68–0.76, a factor of ~1.6 against the 1.7 predicted, on a stated target of two to four releases a battle.

THE NEXT LEVER, WITH THE ARITHMETIC, AND NOTHING FURTHER WAS TAKEN: the ground's base drip 1 → 2. It adds ~9.1 Faith a battle, ~6.8 of it to allies, taking an ally from 3.5 to ~5.7 — across the threshold — for an estimated 2.0–2.5 releases a battle. The alternative, recorded with its number so the choice is visible: the release threshold 5 → 3, which costs no new Faith at all (~2.6–3.0 releases) but changes the cap and the threshold, today the same number. Both are design decisions. Neither was taken — and the drip is the term BH deliberately removed a node from, so raising the base is a decision that has to be made knowingly rather than as a repair.

§1 — What changed, and why it was a design fault rather than a magnitude

The lane's two axes were fed by one meter and they wanted opposite things: releases want the meter EMPTY, held-stack value wants it FULL. So the second axis BG and BH added to break the lane's compounding was not independent of the release engine — it was antagonistic to it. That is why BH's leave-one-out grid came back flat, and why Communion (which only rolls on a release) and Binding Oath (which is paid by releases) both read dormant.

A stack's held value now reads the PEAK — the highest Faith that carrier has held this battle — rather than the current count. A release resets the count and leaves the peak standing, so it is pure upside instead of a cost paid against the lane's other half. The peak is per-battle and is zeroed when the next fight opens.

The magnitudes came down, because peak-reading multiplies their effective value. 3% and +2% were priced against a current count that averages low; read against a peak that ratchets to five and stays there, the same numbers pay roughly double for the rest of every fight. Now 2% mitigation and +1.5% damage per peak stack.

Fervor and Apostle are ADDITIVE with each other, not multiplicative. Both double the held value; multiplied they reach ×4, which is the compounding fault this whole arc exists to remove, rebuilt on a new axis. Base 1×, Fervor +1×, Apostle +1× — so both together are ×3 and never ×4, and both tooltips now say TRIPLE. At ×3 on a peak of five that is 30% mitigation and +22.5% damage on every ally while the ground holds — a capstone plus a row-6 node, and it is meant to be strong.

The Faith chip now survives a release, sitting at F0 and stating the peak beside the count. A live benefit with no chip on the bar is a lie, and the peak is still paying.

§3 — Measured, every row replicated

BH recorded that this row is no longer a ±2-point instrument, so every cell is a median of repeated n=200 reads. All four built, BC/BE's lineup. Read the lane against its distance from the ungeared floor, never as an absolute.

all four builtd+h+p% per readmedianreleases/battle healing/battle
FAITH + Apostle15 / 14 / 15 / 1515%0.68–0.76 42–48
− Apostle15 / 15 / 1515%0.71–0.75 46–48
− Communion15 / 15 / 1415% 0.65–0.7240–45
− Binding Oath15 / 13 / 1313% 0.57–0.7636–47
ungeared (the floor)12 / 11 / 1111% 0.60–0.7518–22

THE LANE IS WORTH FOUR POINTS OVER THE FLOOR, AGAINST BH'S TWO. Its healing more than doubles (19 → 46) over the same floor.

NO IGNITION. BH warned that §2 raises the very term that feeds the growth loop (release → bigger maximum → bigger shield → more absorbs → more Faith), which is positive feedback with a threshold. Across four full-lane reads the spread is 14–15 and across three ungeared reads 11–12; nothing came back ten points above its neighbours. 200/200 wins every row, 0 SCRIPT ERROR.

Communion and Binding Oath were reported and NOT touched, on §3's instruction — and only one of the two is still dormant. Communion is, exactly as §3 predicted: withholding it moves the headline by nothing, and the source table says why outright — it is worth 0.68 Faith a battle against the ground's 9.1, because it only rolls on a release and there are 0.7 of those. Binding Oath is the one cell this instrument cannot resolve, and it is reported as unresolved rather than claimed either way: its reads are 15 / 13 / 13 against the full row's 14 / 15 / 15 / 15 — the ranges overlap, and BH's standing warning is that this row is no longer a ±2-point instrument. What the cell actually tracks is releases (its single read at 0.76 releases scored 15, the same as a full row at the same rate).

The mechanism that would explain a real effect, offered as a hypothesis with its evidence rather than as a finding: §1 gave the node somewhere to go. The Devout's own Faith ratchets a peak now, so the ~0.70 stacks a battle it swears him pay him mitigation for the rest of the fight instead of sitting on a meter that never releases — and the numbers move the right way (Faith onto his own meter 3.4 with the node, 2.6 without; absorbed hits 3.1–3.4 against 2.6–3.3, which is the documented loop of less damage taken → more shields cast → more absorbs). It would take more replicates than this batch ran to say so, and no lever should be aimed at this node on the strength of three reads.

Apostle reads zero on the headline (15% with, 15% without) — the capstone's value is held mitigation and damage that lands on ALLIES and raises the denominator, which is the shape BG already recorded; read it in the ally rows, not in his contribution share.

LIVE AUTOPLAY: the signature payoff is back in ordinary fights. Three releases in one battle, with Binding Oath and Communion both firing — against BH's five consecutive autoplay battles with no release at all. 0 SCRIPT ERROR.

2026-08-10 — Batch BH: four more ability upgrades, and the Faith lane gets a second axis

Two things that do not interact. §1 is additive content — the generic ability-upgrade pool goes from four to eight. §2 is two re-specs that finish the job BG started on the Devout's Faith lane. No save version moves (still v7); every id survives.

§2 — The before and after, leading, because it is the number anyone comes back for

THE ROW DID NOT SETTLE BELOW ITS SIBLINGS. IT FELL TO ROUGHLY WHERE HIS WEAKEST LANE SITS. §2's brief predicted a fall below ZEAL and said in advance that it must be reported rather than chased. It was, and nothing was taken. All rows n=200, BC/BE's lineup (berserker, cryomancer, inquisitor, beastmaster), measured in ONE session on ONE instrument, with the pre-BH rows re-run on an unmodified-HEAD copy rather than quoted.

All four builtd+h+p%healing/battlereleases/battle healing per releaseDevout growth/battle
FAITH + Apostle — HEAD (BG)39%3314.78 6228.6 HP (+13.6%)
FAITH + Apostle — BH13% / 14%26 / 31 0.42 / 0.49542.9 HP (+1.4%)
