The HEAD rows reproduce Batch BG's exactly (BG published FAITH 38, ZEAL 32, BULWARK 12; these read 39, 32, 12), which is what makes the comparison a measurement rather than two sessions being quoted at each other.

One-hero, the other construction: FAITH + Apostle 63% | 918 healing | 12.80 releases | 65 per release on HEAD, against 25% | 95 | 1.50 | 55 on BH; − Apostle reads 24% | 100 | 1.59 | 55.

THE BAR, AND IT IS MISSED IN THE OTHER DIRECTION NOW: FAITH 13% | ZEAL 31% | BULWARK 12%. The lane was 1.19× ZEAL at BG and is 0.44× now — it has gone from twice its siblings to level with the weakest of them. REPORTED AND NOT CORRECTED, on §2's own explicit instruction. The node to raise if the designer wants it raised is Blessed are the Faithful, the lane's only magnitude node; that is a decision, not a repair, and this batch took nothing.

ZEAL AND BULWARK ARE THE CONTROL AND THEY DO NOT MOVE (32→31 and 12→12). That was worth measuring rather than assuming: unlike BE's and BF's levers, this batch changed a rule about the RESOURCE and not only two nodes, so the two lanes that take no Faith node at all were no longer byte-identical by construction. They read the same anyway.

§2 — What actually changed, and the premise that turned out to be stale

Fervor (row 6) moved onto Conviction's held half. Its old effect deepened Consecrated Ground's Faith drip from 1 to 2 per ally per turn, which is a release-frequency multiplier wearing a different coat. It now reads: while Consecrated Ground holds, every ally standing on it counts each stack of Faith DOUBLE — 6% mitigation and +4% damage dealt instead of 3% and +2%. It grants no extra Faith at all. It stacks with Apostle for quadruple while the ground is up, which is a real build rather than a coincidence: both nodes now want the party carrying stacks rather than cycling them. The base drip of 1 is Batch AW §2's and is untouched.

Binding Oath (row 7) moved onto the Devout's own Faith. Its old effect left a remnant of 3 standing after a release — which means the NEXT release costs two stacks instead of five, i.e. the lane's third frequency multiplier. It now reads: whenever an ally's Faith releases, the Devout gains a stack of Faith himself. His own Faith never releases — it holds, paying him the same mitigation and damage it pays them.

THE BRIEF'S REASON FOR THAT NODE WAS STALE, AND CORRECTING IT TOWARD THE CODE MADE THE CHANGE BIGGER THAN THE BRIEF THOUGHT. It says the Devout “has no meter of his own anywhere in his kit”. He has had one since Batch AW §2: Consecrated Ground drips onto its own caster and _gain_faith has never excluded him, so he has been building Faith like anybody else — and releasing at five. That release healed, grew his own maximum and rolled Communion, so it was a frequency source nobody had ever counted. Closing it is part of what §2 asked for (its own named negative control is “his own stacks never releasing”) rather than a side effect of the node, and it is why this is written up as a change to what the resource is.

Communion stays as the lane's single frequency node, at 15, untouched; Sacred Covenant keeps its +2 Faith on a lethal save — one small frequency term in a lane is not the fault, three multiplying ones were.

§2 — The shape test, and an instrument finding that came with it

§2's real criterion is not the share. It is that releases per battle fall while healing per release stays flat, and that no single node dominates a leave-one-out grid.

Both halves of the first test pass, decisively. Releases/battle 4.78 → 0.42–0.49 all four, 12.80 → 1.50 one-hero; healing per release 62 → 54 and 65 → 55. The small fall in healing-per-release is explained rather than waved at: on HEAD the average included the Devout's OWN releases, and his maximum health had been grown by every prior release, so his payouts were the largest in the sample. Only allies release now.

THE INSTRUMENT FINDING, AND IT MATTERS MORE THAN THE GRID: THE ALL-FOUR ROW'S REPLICATE SPREAD IS NOT BG's ±2 POINTS ONCE THE FREQUENCY ENGINE IS DAMPED. The first read of the − Binding Oath cell came back at 24% and 2.06 releases/battle, twice the full row — and it does not reproduce: three subsequent reads of the same build give 12%, 13% and 13%. The cause is legible, and it is the loop Batch AW documented as intended: a release grows the Devout's maximum, a bigger maximum means a bigger Divine Shield, a bigger shield absorbs more hits, and absorbs are what build Faith. That loop is positive feedback with an ignition threshold, and BH parks the lane just under it — so a handful of battles in 200 that happen to ignite drag the mean by ten points. HEAD sat far above the threshold and was stable; the pre-BE lane sat far above it too. Read every BH Faith row as ±1–2 points at the bottom of its range and capable of a runaway, and replicate anything load-bearing.

With that established, here is the leave-one-out grid, every cell replicated because of it. All four built, n=200 per read, three or four reads per cell:

Withheldd+h+p% (each read)medianreleases/battle vs the full lane
— (the full lane)13, 14, 13, 1313% 0.41–0.49—
Communion14, 14, 1314%0.40–0.48 +1
Fervor13, 13, 1313%0.45–0.49 0
Binding Oath24, 12, 13, 1413% 0.36–0.470

NOTHING MOVES THE HEADLINE BY MORE THAN ONE POINT. BC's grid had Communion at THIRTY-THREE. On §2's stated test — nothing exceeding about fifteen — the lane is a lane.

AND THE TEST IS PASSED TRIVIALLY, WHICH HAS TO BE SAID OR THE GRID IS MISREAD. An ungeared Devout beside the same three built allies reads 11% (n=200, 14 healing/battle). So the entire eight-node FAITH lane is now worth about two points of contribution over taking no lane at all, and no node inside a lane worth two points can possibly move a headline by fifteen. Read a leave-one-out grid against the lane's own distance from the ungeared floor, never as an absolute — that caveat is recorded in CLAUDE.md beside the rule itself.

One consequence worth flagging rather than fixing: the lane's one surviving frequency node is now nearly dormant too. Communion rolls only ON a release, and at 0.4 releases a battle it almost never rolls — hence its 1 point. Binding Oath has the same problem by construction: its payer is ally releases, and §2 removed most of them, so the node that was re-specced to give the Devout a meter is fed by the term the same batch damped. Both are reported and neither is touched.

§1 — The upgrade pool goes from four to eight

The mini-boss reward has offered three upgrades from a placeholder pool of four since Batch AN. Four more are authored here, deliberately stopping at eight: a hero draws three a run, so a pool much past eight stops being felt as a reward.

UpgradeEffectOffered only when
WeightedBreak damage doubled.it deals Break damage
WidenedStrikes one additional target, or lands one additional hit. it has random or repeated hits
PiercingIgnores half the target's armor, added to anything it already pierced.it deals damage and does not already pierce everything
CertainIts status application always lands.it applies a status or Bleed on a chance, and that chance is not already a certainty

THE EXTENDED FALLBACK ORDER IS A COMPATIBILITY SURFACE, NOT A PREFERENCE. Batch AU §1 made this pool the generic fallback for any talent node whose ability grant collides with a copy the hero already owns, walking UPGRADE_PRIORITY in order. The four new ids are appended — Honed, Quickened, Effortless, Swift, then Weighted, Widened, Piercing, Certain — so a node that granted Honed yesterday grants Honed today. Inserting anywhere else would silently re-point every live fallback in the game.

THREE CORRECTIONS TOWARD THE CODE, because the brief named fields the game does not have or does not use.

  • The brief's bd is pressure. Break damage on an Ability has always lived in that field; there is no bd. Weighted doubles pressure.
  • Widened does not accept aoe, and that is AP §3's own rule. An area attack already strikes every living enemy, so there is no additional target to add — offering Widened on Blizzard would read as a reward and do nothing, which is exactly the dud the eligibility filter exists to prevent.
  • No hero ability in the game sets status_chance below 1.0. The only writers are enemy kits in data/enemies.json, which no upgrade can reach, so on its authored eligibility Certain would have been a pool entry that could never be offered. The reliability axis the hero roster actually has is bleed_chance (Hack and Slash and Wildstrikes both roll 0.5, and the Relentless talent exists to buy precisely this), so Certain covers both fields.

AND ONE REPLACEMENT, ON THE BRIEF'S OWN INSTRUCTION. Sure — “the Perfect window on its skill check is doubled” — WAS NOT WRITTEN. §1 required the window to be verified as a readable value at its site first, and it is not: PERFECT_HALF is a bare script constant in battle.gd, read in exactly two places — _grade_skill_check(), which takes no arguments and cannot see which ability is being cast, and a perfect-zone rectangle built once during UI setup. It is a fixed fraction shared by every ability and every hero. Worse, the bot never runs the timing bar at all (it rolls a grade off hardcoded probabilities), so a widened window would be invisible to every instrument this project owns and its worth could never be reported. PIERCING takes the slot: armor is an axis nothing else in the pool touches, and “the number lands against less” composes with Honed instead of duplicating it.

THE ELIGIBILITY DISTRIBUTION, measured across all 92 abilities Classes.pool_ability resolves (the four it does not are the class core attacks, the known Batch AP consequence). Abilities qualifying for each count: 2 → 8 abilities, 3 → 46, 4 → 1, 5 → 6, 6 → 23, 7 → 7, 8 → 1 (Hack and Slash is the only ability in the game eligible for all eight). Per upgrade, how many abilities it can land on: Swift 92, Quickened 88, Effortless 84, Honed 37, Piercing 37, Weighted 35, Widened 8, Certain 2.

CERTAIN REACHES TWO ABILITIES IN THE WHOLE GAME — Hack and Slash and Wildstrikes, both the Berserker's — so for eleven of the twelve specs it is simply dropped from the offer rather than paired with a dud. Reported, not forced. Widening it means authoring partial status chances onto hero abilities, which is a design decision and a larger batch than this one. The axis is built and the roster can grow into it.

One consequence stated so it is not read as a bug: the offer draws three from eight rather than three from four, so any given upgrade appears about half as often. A hero can finish a run having been offered a pool that never included the one their build wanted. That is correct for a roguelike.

Implementation notes worth keeping

  • Two fields were DELETED WITH THEIR READ SITES rather than re-pointed in place, because both nodes changed what they MEAN and not only what they pay — the harder failure, and the BA plague_bearer precedent. Gone: fervor_step (the +N on the ground's drip) and oath_ranks (the release remnant). Three replace them: fervor (a gate, shaped exactly like apostle), oath_faith and oath_opening.
  • One multiplier, TWO gates, one read site. _faith_stack_mult(devout, holder) takes the HOLDER as well as the Devout, because Fervor reads the carrier's ground and not the Devout's — reading his would double everybody's stacks whenever he stood on his own ground. .apostle is still read in exactly one place and the test asserts the count.
  • THE RUNE OF THE BINDING OATH COULD NOT PAY WHAT IT PAID, AND IT IS RE-POINTED RATHER THAN DELETED (the AZ Deep Sight / AY Deep Bond / AX Hollow Chalice precedent). Its first clause bought the release remnant the re-spec deletes, so it keeps the relationship it always had — Faith that persists — through the only door left open: oath_opening, the Devout opening each battle already holding a stack of his own. Its second clause (faithful_step 5) is byte-untouched and its description was rewritten rather than left lying. This is a dead-clause repair, not the rune magnitude pass the designer closed in Batch AF.
  • _swear_opening_oath() is its own function called from the battle-start block, for the standing reason: _run_battle cannot be driven headlessly, so a clause left inline could only ever be checked by a grep (the AW _ground_faith_tick / BA _perfected_toxin_tick / BD _deadfall_tick precedent).
  • Batch AY §9's half-growth rule is now unreachable twice over. Apostle was the only thing that ever parked an ally at five (BG), and Binding Oath's remnant was the last writer of a non-zero remnant (BH), so a release always consumes all five. The rule and its argument stay inside _conviction_growth, where test_batch_ay drives them directly.

Live autoplay, and the finding that matters most for feel

0 SCRIPT ERROR, and Fervor reads correctly in a real fight: “Consecrated Ground kindles Berserker (+1 Faith) — and his Faith is worth double here (Fervor)” on every ally, every turn. The drip is a flat 1 with the node learned, which is the whole of the re-spec visible in one log line.

AND IN FIVE CONSECUTIVE AUTOPLAY BATTLES THE FAITH NEVER RELEASED ONCE. That is not a bug and it is exactly what 0.4 releases a battle means, but it is worth stating in the plainest terms available: the Devout's signature payoff has largely left ordinary fights. It is the same shape as Batch AX's finding about Ruin — a mechanic measured at zero in trash — and the same instruction applies: reporting the number was the job; the decision is the designer's. Binding Oath's own log line was therefore never exercised by the smoke either; test_batch_bh drives it directly instead, which is where a rare branch belongs.

Verified

check_parse 0, check_flow 0 (6 screens), 11 scenes 0 SCRIPT ERROR, run-harness gates 1/2/3 PASS. NEW test_batch_bh.gd 230/0.

SEVEN NEGATIVE CONTROLS RUN, each applied to the code and reverted (the suite came back to 230/0 after the last): Fervor still dripping stacks trips 2; the Devout's own Faith reaching five and releasing trips 5; a new upgrade bypassing AP's eligibility filter trips 4; Fervor reading the Devout's ground instead of the holder's trips 4; Binding Oath's remnant restored trips 2; the four new upgrades inserted AHEAD of the old four in the priority list trips 9; and Weighted landing on damage rather than pressure trips 2.

Six suites were RE-POINTED IN PLACE with the reason in each file, and three of the re-points are INVERSIONS rather than edits — which is the honest treatment when a batch reverses a rule an older batch was guarding. test_batch_aw's Fervor check asked “does the node deepen the drip” and now asks whether it leaves the drip alone; test_batch_ax's §7 finding (“the ground already pays 2”) became history and the site now pins the flat 1; test_batch_bg's “Binding Oath still keeps its 3” became “a release resets to zero, Binding Oath or no”. test_batch_an's pool-of-four literal and test_batch_au's four-entry priority list moved to the pool's real size and to the ORIGINAL FOUR still leading it. One check was sharpened rather than re-pointed: test_batch_aw's dead-counter sweep used a bare contains, which trips on a COMMENT naming a deleted field — and naming them is exactly how this project asks a later batch not to re-add one — so it now looks for a read, a declaration and a rune key instead.

Regression, every suite at its Batch BG count with zero drift except the four re-pointed here: ah 5587 (stamp gate bumped BG→BH in both files that hold it), ah_battle 63, ai 2036, an 6053 (+1), aj 403, ak 527, al 559, ar 887, as 387, at 461, au 340 (was 335), av 315, aw 340 (was 338), ax 329, ay 457 (was 455), az 489, ba 647, bb 172, bc 91, bd 69, be 34, bf 78, bg 45, test_runes 2973, test_rune_battle 96 — all 0 failures.

2026-08-09 — Batch BG: the run harness reads difficulty, and Apostle changes axis

Two things that cannot contaminate each other. §1 is a measurement and ships nothing — it replaces BF's sweep as the difficulty reference, because the sweep's enemies are unscaled and a run's are not. §2 is one capstone re-specced, and it ran first so §1 measured the fixed spec. No save version moves (still v7).

§1 — The run harness reads difficulty (leading, because this supersedes the sweep table as the reference anyone comes back for)

./sim.sh --run 100, all three route policies, two build levels. Unlike the sweep, this carries tier stat scaling, the zone slot multiplier, HP and mana between fights, talent points earned and spent, elite runes and boss trophies. Nothing was tuned. The proposal at the end is marked as a proposal.

Three premises corrected toward the code before a single number is read.

  • The three route policies are one walk, and have been since Batch AN. The report's own line reads “Reachable nodes per step: 1.00 — steps offering a real choice: 0% (0 of 2764)”, and every node type reads taken == offered (fight 18.6/18.6, elite 4.6/4.6, boss 2.1/2.1). A line map has no route decision, so the “band across policies” is three samples of ONE configuration. That is not a loss — it is a free triplicate, and it is where the noise floor below comes from.
  • Rests do not exist (Batch AN). “Rests taken versus offered” reads 0.0/0.0 and always will. Recovery is the per-slot heal and the bot's potions.
  • The build policy is a confound and it is smaller than it looks. DOD_SIM_BUILDS defaults to each tree's FIRST lane — and for three of the four specs in the default party that first lane is already the lane its own batch measured as the standout (Berserker Bloodletting, Beastmaster devotion). The default band's real deficit is the Devout on BULWARK, the lane BF measured at 14%, plus the Cryomancer on Winter. So the two bands differ in two heroes, not four, and the gap between them must be read that way.

DEFAULT BUILDS — first lane per tree. The floor: what an indifferent build achieves. n=100 per policy.

routecompletions95% banddepth of 36wipe median slotpoints/hero/rungold unspent
greedy48%±9.6 pts24.31 ± 1.30z1 t99.21410
default60%±9.6 pts27.64 ± 1.13z2 t0.510.41572
cautious49%±9.6 pts24.23 ± 1.25z1 t109.21293

The spread across the three IS the noise, since the three are the same walk — at n=100 completions cannot resolve anything under 19 points, and the rows differ by 12. Read depth reached, not completions.

Run economy (default route): gold earned 2496, spent 924, 1572 left unspent; items used 11.0/run against 8.9 carried unused; merchants 10.6/run, events 5.8/run, bargains 7.0/run at severity 3.67; runes acquired 3.51/hero/run with 94% of slots filled; 8.0 talent nodes owned entering a boss, with points spent to within 0.1 of points earned.

NAMED BUILDS — each hero on the lane its own batch measured strongest. The ceiling: what a player who reads their tree achieves. n=100 per policy.

routecompletionsdepth of 36wipe median slotpoints/hero/rungold unspent
greedy58%27.00 ± 1.28z1 t910.21589
default55%25.81 ± 1.29z1 t99.71485
cautious57%27.67 ± 1.26z1 t910.41687

SUBSTITUTION, REPORTED RATHER THAN BURIED. The strongest-lane control set BC and BE built puts the Cryomancer on Thaw, and Thaw row 6 (Honed Shards) reaches the still-open recursion between _hold_release and _hold_freeze that BF §3 found — measured here at 15 stack overflows in four runs and ~1.1 MB of backtrace, against zero on Deep Freeze at the same n. A band measured through a stack overflow is not a band, so the Cryomancer runs Deep Freeze. Until that crash is fixed, no run band can contain a Thaw Cryomancer, which means one of twelve specs cannot have its best lane measured by the project's best instrument. (Standalone lane rows are unaffected: zero events across ten n=200 rows on the fixed lineup.)

AND THE QUESTION NOBODY HAD ASKED: how much does building well actually matter in a full run? Because the three route rows are provably the same configuration, they pool to n=300 a band:

bandcompletions (n=300)95% banddepth of 36
default builds52.3%±5.7 pts25.39 ± 0.71
named builds56.7%±5.6 pts26.83 ± 0.73
gap+4.3 pts (1.07σ)—+1.43 tiers (1.40σ)

Neither is significant. Moving the Devout off his worst lane onto his best, and the Cryomancer off Winter onto Deep Freeze, buys about four points of completions and a tier and a half of depth — and at n=300 a band that cannot be told from zero. Read it with the caveat that makes it honest: the two bands differ in TWO heroes, not four, because the default first lane already is the standout lane for the Berserker and the Beastmaster. So this is not “building well is worth nothing”; it is “these two lane swaps, on this bot, are worth about four points.”

Per-tier win rate is 91–100% at every tier of every zone (default route, default builds: z1 t8 96%, z1 boss 91%, z2 boss 100%, z3 boss 100%; named builds z1 boss 97%, z2 boss 95%, z3 boss 98%), at 0.3–1.1 deaths per fight and 70–89% party health entering.

The Devout's FAITH lane, measured in a real run for the first time: 37% d+h+p% over 2581 battles, against the 38% the standalone all-four lane row gives — the two instruments agree, which is the first time a lane row has been cross-checked against progression. Healing 456/battle at 125 per release over 3.13 releases. Caveat on one field: the “max observed” Conviction growth reads 1071–6130 across the named rows, which is a stalemate artefact — 2–3 stalemates per band run to a forced end and the growth clause accumulates for hundreds of rounds. Read the mean (26.9 HP/battle, +9.4% of base), never the max.

THE RECONCILIATION WITH BF'S SWEEP, WHICH IS WHAT THE SECTION WAS COMMISSIONED FOR. The 15-point gap is not an artefact of measuring without tier scaling. A real scaled fight is not measurably harder than its budget row — 91–100% scaled here against 93–100% unscaled in BF's sweep. What kills runs is compounding, not scaling: roughly 24 fights a run at ~95% each is 0.9524 ≈ 29%, and the measured completion rate is 48–60%. The per-fight number and the per-run number are both right and they answer different questions.

The measured power ladder is D-shaped inside every zone and its floor drops zone by zone (party-vs-warband ratio, default route): z1 1.49 → 0.83 at its boss, z2 1.23 → 0.69, z3 1.04 → 0.53. Wipes cluster where the ratio first crosses under 1 — z1 t8–boss (19 of 40) and z2 t4–7 (10 of 40).

PROPOSAL — AND IT IS ONLY A PROPOSAL. Do not pull enemy health, attack or count. Every one of those moves the per-fight number, and the per-fight number is already inside the band the sweep says is 8–15 points too easy; pulling them would fix the run by breaking the fight. The lever the run data argues for is tier scaling's own slope, and the number that argues for it is the ratio floor: 0.83 / 0.69 / 0.53 at the three zone bosses. A ladder whose top sits at 0.53 while the fights on it are still won 100% of the time is a ladder whose shape is doing nothing — the party is nominally outgunned two to one at the zone-3 boss and beats it every time. Either the ratio model does not predict outcome, in which case stop steering by it, or the slope is real and something else is holding the win rate up. That is the question the next difficulty batch should answer before it moves a number.

§2 — Apostle moves off the frequency axis

The capstone no longer changes what a release consumes. Instead every stack of Faith an ally carries is worth double: 6% damage mitigation and +4% damage dealt per stack, up from 3% and +2%. Same id, same lane, same row, same payload field — no save migrates.

Why the axis and not the price. BF measured the old capstone at −8 one-hero and −2 all four: taking it lowered the engine it sat on, because parking allies at five made them invisible to Communion. A price cannot fix a sign. Apostle multiplied release frequency — the exact term BE and BF spent two batches taming — so a capstone whose whole effect is that term will keep inverting at any number you put on it.

Conviction has two halves: what a stack does WHILE HELD, and what happens when five of them RELEASE. All eight Faith nodes touch the release half. Nothing had ever touched the held half. That made it simultaneously the only untouched axis in the lane and an unmistakably capstone-sized one. And it makes the two nodes complementary instead of cancelling — Communion pays only for allies below five, Apostle now pays for allies carrying stacks, so both want the party sitting in the 1–4 band. The smell BF recorded is not reduced, it is inverted.

One of BF's own rows does not reproduce, and that had to be established before anything could be compared to it. Every row below is n=200 on BC/BE's lineup, taken in one session on one instrument, with the pre-BG rows re-run on an unmodified-HEAD copy rather than quoted. BF's all-four −Apostle cell (31%) reads 39% on unmodified HEAD today — an 8-point miss on the cell BF called a natural control and computed “Apostle is worth −2 all four” from. Its other rows do reproduce: one-hero FAITH 54→55, one-hero −Apostle 62→64, ZEAL 31→32, BULWARK 14→12, all inside n=200's ±1–2.

d+h+p% | healing/battle | releases/battle | healing per release | prev/b | Faith-stack prev/b:

rowHEAD (old Apostle)BG (new Apostle)
one-hero, FAITH all eight55% | 639 | 9.96 | 61 | 70 | 1461% | 857 | 11.98 | 64 | 79 | 25
one-hero, −Apostle (control)64% | 958 | 13.30 | 65 | 68 | 1263% | 930 | 12.91 | 65 | 71 | 12
all four built, FAITH all eight36% / 35% | 271/264 | 4.18/4.12 | 47/41 | 838% / 38% | 313/314 | 4.51/4.49 | 50/51 | 14
all four built, −Apostle (control)39% | 334 | 4.79 | 43 | 640% / 38% | 348/313 | 4.96/4.47 | 43/43 | 7/6

Two replicates apiece on the all-four rows: the n=200 spread there is about 2 points, which is the resolution every all-four number should be read at.

The control that proves the change is inert where it should be: the −Apostle rows do not move. Seven of the eight Devout builds never take this capstone and not one of them feels it.

The mechanism reads exactly as designed, in the term that IS the capstone. Faith-stack damage prevented per battle is 2.0–2.3× the −Apostle baseline in both constructions — all four 7/6 → 14/14, one-hero 12 → 25. The old capstone moved the same term by 1.3×, and it did it by parking rather than by doubling.

Apostle's worth, on one instrument: old −9 one-hero and −3.5 all four; new −2 and −1 — the latter inside the replicate spread, i.e. zero. It is still not positive ON THE SHARE, and that is the intended outcome rather than a failure. The mitigation books to him; the +4% damage lands on his allies and raises the denominator; and a tougher party absorbs fewer hits, so his own engine spins slower (releases 12.91 → 11.98 one-hero). Read the capstone in prev/b and the stack term, not in the share. His prevented per battle goes 43 → 50 all four and 71 → 79 one-hero; party damage per battle 616 → 627 (the old capstone took it 610 → 597 — the wrong sign); enemy damage landed per battle 168 → 162. BDprev/b is unmoved at 36 all four and 49 one-hero, which is Devoutness and is untouched.

The bar, re-measured on the BG tree in the same session: FAITH 38% | ZEAL 32% | BULWARK 12% — 1.19×, six points clear of ZEAL, against BF's stated 1.06×. Note that HEAD's own FAITH/ZEAL was already 1.11× before this batch (35.5 vs 32), so BG added about 2.5 points to a gap that was about 3.5. The brief said: if the row climbs past ZEAL by more than a couple of points, report and stop, and do not take a fourth lever on this lane. It did, and nothing further was taken.

One rule went quietly unreachable and the docs now say so. Batch AY's “a release that consumed NO stacks pays half growth” existed only to tax Apostle's stream. Apostle was the only thing that ever parked an ally at five, and Binding Oath's remnant is capped at four by construction — so no release can now reach it. The rule and its argument stay in the code, one line from being live again; master.html no longer claims it happens, and the test drives it at _conviction_growth instead of through a release.

Tests: new test_batch_bg.gd — 44 checks, including both rates measured end to end over 400 casts each (12% / 24% less damage taken at four stacks, +8% / +16% dealt) and the two negative controls the batch exists for: a release under Apostle still consumes the stacks, and the doubled values follow the CARRIER, not the Devout (an ally on four stacks is paid +16% with the Devout empty, and nothing at all when the four stacks are on the Devout instead). Communion still rolls 60% for a carrier at four, unchanged. Five assertions in test_batch_aw and test_batch_ay pinned the superseded capstone and were re-pointed rather than deleted.

2026-08-09 — Batch BF: the instrument learns to see Break, Communion's last lever, and the first honest difficulty read

Three things, in that order, because each one changes what the next measures. §1 is a measurement change with no gameplay in it. §2 is one condition on one node. §3 is a MEASUREMENT AND NOTHING WAS TUNED — it is the table the tuning batch will design against. No save version moves (still v7).

§3 — The first honest difficulty read (leading, because it is the thing anyone will come back for)

Every spec batch from AJ onward recorded no difficulty measurement belongs here — a single-spec re-author has no honest control row. There is nothing left to re-author, so that reason expired. ./sim.sh --sweep 400, n=400 per budget (Batch R used 200; at 400 the standard error on a 93% row is 1.3 points, so these rows cannot flap), run at two build levels because they are two different questions.

UNGEARED — no talents spent, the party as it enters zone 1. Read the LOW budgets off this row.

budgetbattleswin%roundsdeaths/battlefoesalive@r3
3400100%5.00.022.50.0
6400100%7.60.224.20.0
940097%10.00.595.40.1
1240093%12.30.965.90.3

ALL FOUR FORCE-LEARNED INTO FULL 8-NODE LANES — the party at zone 3, where 8 points is exactly a full path. Read the TOP budget off this row. (Same four lanes as Batch BE's all-four control set, so the construction is comparable with §2's rows.)

budgetbattleswin%roundsdeaths/battlefoesalive@r3
3400100%3.90.012.50.0
6400100%5.30.064.20.0
9400100%6.60.145.40.0
12400100%7.60.215.90.0

Both shares, per budget, at the top budget (damage% | Break-dealt% | d+h+p%). Ungeared: Beastmaster 33 | 10 | 26 · Berserker 27 | 44 | 22 · Cryomancer 36 | 41 | 29 · Devout 4 | 5 | 22. Built: Beastmaster 45 | 10 | 26 · Berserker 23 | 43 | 14 · Cryomancer 30 | 43 | 17 · Devout 2 | 4 | 43, with Break prevented 55/battle (all of it Devoutness).

Where it lands against Batch V's target, and what I would propose

THE CONTROL FIRST: the ungeared row reproduces Batch R almost exactly (R measured 100/100/98/93.5 at 200 per budget; this reads 100/100/97/93 at 400). Nothing in this batch, and nothing in the twelve spec batches since, has moved the composition curve. That is what makes the rest of the table worth reading.

Batch V pinned the target at ~85% on top-band (budget 10-12) encounters. The sweep lands above it at both build levels: 93% ungeared and 100% built — 8 and 15 points high, and the built party did not lose a single one of 400 top-band fights. The low budgets are not a finding: 100% at budgets 3 and 6 is what a first-zone encounter is supposed to read.

THE CAVEAT THAT KEEPS THIS HONEST, AND IT IS LARGE. Sweep enemies are UNSCALED and both build levels ran against the same zone-1 roster, so the build axis is clean but the enemy axis is missing: a real tier-8+ zone-3 fight carries tier scaling and the zone multiplier on top of its budget. These win rates are an upper bound on the party's side, not a verdict on the game. What the table honestly says is that composition alone does not produce an 85% top band.

THE LEVER I WOULD PULL: ENEMY COUNT AT THE TOP OF THE BUDGET LADDER. The number that argues for it is that budget 12 fields 5.9 enemies against budget 9's 5.4 — a 9% increase in bodies for a 33% increase in budget. The ladder's top step is being spent almost entirely on per-enemy quality, so the top band is currently the same fight with tougher individuals. Beside it: alive@r3 is 0.0 at every budget on the built row and 0.0-0.3 ungeared, i.e. the field is empty entering round 3 at every budget on both build levels (Batch S found this and it has not moved). Enemy count is the only one of the four levers that moves both numbers: it raises incoming actions per round — and attrition is the sensitive dial, deaths/battle swinging 0.02 → 0.96 across the ungeared ladder, a 48x change while win% moves 7 points — and it puts a field on the board for the round the AoE specs are balanced around. Expected to move: win% down at budgets 9-12 and barely at all at 3-6, deaths/battle up at the top, alive@r3 off the floor for the first time, and the damage shares toward the AoE specs, which is diagnostic rather than a side effect.

The three I would NOT pull, and why. Enemy attack moves deaths/battle directly but leaves alive@r3 at 0.0 — a lower win rate without fixing the empty field, and harder in the least interesting way. Enemy health lengthens fights that already run 12.3 rounds ungeared at budget 12, pushing toward stalemates (one already appeared in this sweep). The power-budget curve changes where a budget appears on the road rather than how hard that budget is — a run-shape lever, and it belongs to a --run measurement, not a sweep.

NOTHING WAS TUNED. This is the table the next batch designs against, and it is stated as a proposal so that it is obvious nothing here was acted on.

And the sweep found a crash

THE BUILT ROW SURFACED AN INFINITE RECURSION BETWEEN _hold_release AND _hold_freeze, REACHABLE IN NORMAL PLAY BY A CRYOMANCER WHO HAS TAKEN HONED SHARDS. It is PRE-EXISTING and not this batch's — reproduced on unmodified HEAD at matched n with the identical count, 23 events in 400 budget-12 battles on both builds. It is not fixed here, because this batch measures rather than tunes and the repair wants its own tests. It is now the first item in the open threads.

The mechanism is deterministic, not a race. A release erases its target from the hold list, sets its Chilled to 1, and then Honed Shards adds 3 — exactly the 4 that flash-freezes. So every release re-freezes its own target. The hold limit is 1 for a Thaw-lane Cryomancer, so freezing a second enemy evicts the first, whose release re-freezes it, which evicts the second — and the two ping-pong until the engine's stack limit. The code comment beside Honed Shards already warns it "can re-freeze the enemy it just thawed"; what was never noticed is that the eviction loop turns that into a cycle. Trigger, in one line: Honed Shards learned, hold limit 1, two enemies held at once — about 6% of budget-12 battles with a full Thaw lane, and never at budgets 3/6/9, where the field is rarely big enough for a second hold.

It does not invalidate the built row (all 23 affected battles were wins, and the row reads 400/400 with them excluded too), but the contamination favours the party — a permanently held enemy stops acting — so that is a second reason to read the built top band as an upper bound.

§1 — The contribution metric could not see Break, and every Break build was measured by it

The share has been (damage + healing + damage prevented) over the party's total of the same three since Batch W. Break points were in neither the numerator nor the denominator, dealt or prevented — while BD/b sat printed in its own column right beside it, which made the omission look handled. The Warden's entire Threat lane pays in Break (Batch AL took his Break/battle from 104 to 320); the Occultist's Broken Will and Entropy exist to grind a boss's meter; Batch BD gave a single deadfall 270 Break points. None of it reached a contribution number.

  • TWO NEW COLUMNS, AND DELIBERATELY NOT ONE NEW SHARE. BD% is a hero's Break dealt over the party's, off its own pool. BDprev/b is Break refused or reduced. Break is NOT folded into the old share and must not be — that needs an exchange rate between a Break point and a hit point (one? a tenth?) that nobody can defend, and inventing it would bury a guess inside every measurement taken afterwards, including §3's.
  • THE OLD COLUMN IS NOW LABELLED FOR WHAT IT IS: d+h+p%, with a legend under the table saying outright that it is not a share of the party's work. It was called contrib%, and at least one number in this project's history was read the second way. It did not move by a point in this batch — that is the control that makes the new columns readable and keeps every pre-BF row comparable with every post-BF one.
  • THE AUDIT FOUND FIVE MORE HOLES BESIDE THE ONE THE BRIEF NAMED. Six effects reduce or refuse incoming Break — Devoutness, Bulwark of Fortitude, Hold the Line, Ward, Immovable, Bracing — and exactly one of the six was booked anywhere before this batch. Bulwark gives the party three turns of taking NO Break damage and was counted nowhere in the game.
  • ONE DOOR. All six now book through _prev_bd: the per-hero total and the named term are written by the same call, so the parts can never disagree with the total. A bd_ term prefix is the routing rule, so a seventh reducer added later names itself into the right ledger or does not book at all. Measured live: Bulwark refuses 182 of 200 raw Break points on one hit and books all 182 to its caster.
  • TWO DELIBERATE EXCLUSIONS, AND THE REASONS DIFFER. Base Constitution is not booked (a stat block is not a contribution — the same rule that keeps base armor and resists out of the prevented column; Bracing is booked, because a stance a talent bought is exactly what the damage side already counts). The run modifiers Muffled and Deadened are not booked (nobody in the party did that). Two more are adjacent and are not in the column at all: Rallying Shout and Battered Not Broken remove banked Break, which is Break healing rather than Break refused.
  • WHAT THIS MEANS FOR READING ANY REPORT: a build that pays in Break reads low in d+h+p% by construction. Read it in BD% and BDprev/b, and never call such a build weak off the d+h+p column alone.

§2 — Communion spreads to allies who are still building

Communion no longer rolls for an ally already at five Faith. One condition, on the gate that already existed. The remaining coupling Batch BE shipped with was that Communion reads the recipient's current stacks while Apostle parks them at 5 — so parked allies rolled 75%, and every advance on one was itself a release.

  • Framed as an inclusion, because that is what the mechanic is: fervor spreads to allies who are still building it. An ally sitting at five is not building — they are at the payout. So the node reaches the three who need it and cannot manufacture a release out of one that already happened.
  • THE CLIFF IS STATED IN THE TOOLTIP: 60% at four stacks, 0% at five. An ability whose chance rises with stacks and then vanishes at the top reads as a bug otherwise. Measured over 1200 driven releases apiece: 58.8% at four, 0.0% at five (it was 75%).
  • IT LANDED INSIDE THE BRACKET BE PRE-MEASURED FOR IT, which is why this lever was chosen over the alternative — nothing else on the table had its outcome measured in advance. Bracket 48-73% one-hero / 25-45% all four; measured 54% and 33%.

n=200 per row, Batch BC's lineup, both constructions. Contribution | healing/battle | releases/battle | healing per release:

RowBatch BE (before)Batch BF (after)
one-hero, FAITH all eight73% | 1555 | 22.77 | 6554% | 618 | 9.64 | 61
one-hero, −Apostle (control)63% | 910 | 12.70 | 6562% | 913 | 12.70 | 65
all four built, FAITH45% | 429 | 6.55 | 6233% | 244 | 3.81 | 61
all four built, −Apostlenot measured31% | 318 | 4.58 | 62

The −Apostle row is a natural control and it reproduces to the hundredth — releases 12.70 → 12.70 — because without Apostle an ally never sits at five and the condition can never fire. Healing per release has not moved in any row across either batch: it was a frequency problem and both levers bought frequency.

THE BAR IS MET ON THE ROW THAT COUNTS. All four built, FAITH 33% against ZEAL 31% / BULWARK 14% — a 1.06x gap, from 1.8x before Batch BE and 1.45x after it. (ZEAL and BULWARK were not re-run and did not need to be: a build without Communion makes no draw at all, so those rows are byte-identical by construction.)

TWO HONEST COSTS, CARRIED FORWARD RATHER THAN DROPPED. The cliff, above. And the sign on Apostle flipped: the capstone was worth +7 points at Communion 40 and +10 at 15, and is now worth −8 one-hero (54 vs 62) and −2 all four (33 vs 31). Taking it now lowers the Faith engine, because parking allies at five makes them invisible to Communion. Two nodes in one lane that partly cancel each other is a smell. Recorded, not solved — if the tree wants restructuring later, this is the note to start from, and the honest fix is probably to make one of the two something else rather than to re-price either.

2026-08-09 — Batch BD: Deadfall becomes a placed hazard

One ability. No tree is touched, no magnitude in the tree moves, no other spec is involved, no save version moves (still v7).

THE FINDING FIRST, BECAUSE THE RE-SPEC IS ONLY ITS CONSEQUENCE. Read side by side, Deadfall and Snare Trap were the same ability — and had been for fourteen batches:

Snare Trap (base kit)Deadfall (trophy pool)
Cost20 Mana20 Mana
Initiative2.02.0
Cooldown33
Victimone you choosethe next enemy to act
EffectStun 1, Poison 435% Atk nature, Stun 1
Perfectthe stun beats an unbroken bossyou name the victim

Same cost, same speed, same cooldown, both traps, both against the same trap cap, both a 1-turn stun springing at the victim's turn start. The single distinction was that Deadfall does not let you pick — and its perfect handed that back. A perfect Deadfall was a Snare Trap that traded poison for damage. And the trade ran against his own spine: Trapper pays +8% per different status on the target, Snare Trap lands two (three with Quick Rigging), Deadfall landed one. It cost a zone-boss pick for an ability that was harder to aim and fed his passive less.

  • §1 DEADFALL, RE-SPECCED. 25 Mana, 2.0 initiative, 5 turn cooldown, untargeted. Arm a deadfall in the path: the next enemy to act takes 20% of Attack as nature damage and is Stunned for 1 turn. The trap then lies dormant for 2 turns, re-arms itself, and springs again — three times in all. Perfect: four.
    Untargeted is the design now rather than the drawback, so the perfect no longer names the victim — that clause is precisely what collapsed the two abilities, and it would have survived any re-spec that did not delete it deliberately. A snare catches one animal you picked; a deadfall sits in the trail and takes whoever walks it.
    Per-spring damage falls 35% → 20% because there are three of them: 60% total against a single ability's worth, with the real payoff in three turns of denial rather than in the numbers. Bosses shrug the stun unless Broken, by the ordinary rule — and note the synergy that falls out rather than being designed in: three attempts across a fight means a Snares build grinding a boss's Break gets its stun the moment the Break lands, without needing the perfect rig Snare Trap does.
  • THE SLOT RULE, AND THE NUMBER BEHIND IT. A deadfall occupies one trap slot for as long as it has springs left. That is the honest cost of persistence and it ties a trophy to a tree node: at the base cap of one, arming a deadfall locks out Snare Trap until it is spent, and Deadfall Network's three slots become genuinely valuable to him rather than a stacking bonus. It is also the clause most likely to make the ability feel bad, so the gate is instrumented at the refusal itself — trap_report_line() reports casts, springs, springs-per-cast and how often the cap actually refused a Snare Trap, split by whether a charged deadfall was the thing holding the slot.
    AND THE ANSWER IS NOT SUBTLE. Kit smoke, n=200 battles, lineup berserker/pyromancer/inquisitor/mystic with Deadfall granted:
rowDeadfall casts/battlesprings/battlesprings per cast Snare Trap casts/battleSnare Trap refused by the cap
base cap of one (no talents)1.062.242.10 1.43.03/battle — all 3.03 of them with a charged deadfall holding the slot
full SNARES lane (Deadfall Network, cap 3)1.032.422.35 2.60.00
  • READ THAT SECOND COLUMN AGAINST THE LAST ONE: at the base cap the deadfall blocks a Snare Trap cast ABOUT THREE TIMES A BATTLE, which is more often than Snare Trap is actually cast (1.4), and every single refusal is the deadfall's doing. Deadfall Network takes it to zero and nearly doubles his Snare Trap casts, 1.4 → 2.6. So the clause does exactly what it was expected to do, at a size worth knowing before deciding whether to keep it — the alternative (a deadfall costs no slot at all) is one condition at that same gate. Shipped holding a slot, as specified, with the number attached.
    A second figure worth having: springs per cast is 2.10, not 3. A smoke fight is over in seven rounds and the third charge often never lands, which also means the 270-Break Bone Breaker figure below is a full-deadfall ceiling rather than a typical fight. A run is where that number will mean something.
  • SURVIVALIST ROWS, for context and NOT as a difficulty reading (kit-mechanics ratios only; ./sim.sh carries no difficulty signal since Batch R): base cap 27% damage share (170/battle), Trapper breadth 1.25; full SNARES lane 28% (178), breadth 1.56. Both 40/40-equivalent clean, 0 SCRIPT ERROR.
  • §2 THE FIELD KEPT ITS NAME AND CHANGED ITS UNIT, which is the class of change that fails silently. deadfall_armed counted armed traps and now counts charges remaining on the one deadfall a cast places. Every read site moved with it: the spring decrements it, the chip renders it, and the trap cap counts a charged deadfall as exactly ONE occupant — left alone, a three-charge trap would have filled Deadfall Network's cap by itself, spending the node it is meant to make valuable. deadfall_dormant is new. deadfall_aims is DELETED, not left unreachable (the BA precedent): the perfect no longer names a target, so the array could never be non-empty, and the test asserts the field does not exist rather than that it is empty.
    The rest-and-spring rule was extracted into _deadfall_tick(), because _run_battle cannot be driven headlessly (the AR trap) and a rule left inside it can only ever be checked by a grep — its negative controls could never fail. Its position is asserted too: it runs above the stunned branch, so the stun a spring lands costs the victim the very turn it walked into the trap.
  • THREE TALENT NODES NOW PAY PER SPRING, AND THAT IS THREE TIMES WHAT THEY USED TO. MEASURED, not estimated: Bone Breaker pays 90 Break on each of three springs = 270 across a full deadfall — the largest number in the batch and quite possibly the most Break any single cast in the game produces; Cruel Devices multiplies each spring by 1.5 (81 damage across three at 100 Attack, against 30 for one 35% spring); and Caught Fast is re-applied on every spring, so five turns of unhealable becomes a rolling lock. None of the three is changed here. The figures are reported so the next decision has them.
  • THE CHIP HAS TO SAY MORE THAN IT DID. DF# showed how many traps were armed; it shows springs remaining and whether the trap is resting — DF3 armed and ready, DF2·2 for two springs left and two turns of rest, with the tooltip saying so in words. A player who cannot see that the deadfall is dormant cannot plan around it, and the whole value of the ability is that it is a thing sitting on the board.
  • §3 THE BOT ARMS IT EARLY NOW. Deadfall sat last in the rotation, which was right for a one-shot mine and wrong for a persistent investment: a trap with three charges wants arming while there are turns left to spend them. It moved to directly below Snare Trap, gated on there being no deadfall already out (the cap refuses a second at a cap of one, but Deadfall Network would not, and two deadfalls are not a thing).
    §3's own instruction to verify the rotation at its site found the brief's premise half stale: it describes the live order as snare → venom coat → hamstring → harvest → deadfall, and the code reads snare → hamstring → venom coat → harvest → hamstring again → deadfall — Batch BA put breadth before depth, so Hamstring appears twice and above Venom Coating. Corrected toward the code; the move itself is unaffected.
  • §4 AN OPEN THREAD, RECORDED AND NOT ACTED ON. Batch AH's curation rule checks that a CLASS-pool entry works for a sibling spec. Nothing has ever checked a SPEC pool for redundancy against its own base kit — which is why this duplication survived fourteen batches and was caught by a player reading two tooltips. A pass across all twelve spec pools, comparing each entry against its own kit, is worth doing and is not this batch: twelve pools, five to eight entries apiece, and any redundancy found is a design decision rather than a fix.
  • VERIFIED. check_parse 0, check_flow 0 (6 screens), 11 scenes 0 SCRIPT ERROR, run-harness gates 1/2/3 PASS. New test_batch_bd.gd 69 checks / 0 failures.
    Four negative controls, each applied to the code and reverted (the suite came back to 69/0 after the last): a spring not decrementing the charges trips 10; the dormancy not blocking a consecutive spring trips 5; the slot never freeing after the last charge trips 2; and the cap counting CHARGES as occupants — the unit change simply un-made — trips 2, which is the one worth having, because that is the failure the rename invites and nothing else in the game would have complained about it.
    Three test re-points, each with the reason in the file: test_batch_ah's perfect-text map reads "A fourth spring."; test_batch_ah_battle's Deadfall check was written to cover the human-only target picker this batch DELETES, so it now drives what survives underneath (does a perfect rig pay more?) and asserts the old field gone — 65 → 63 checks; and test_batch_ba's trap-cap check fills the cap with real occupants (a deadfall plus snares) because the unit of the field it drove has changed under it.
    Regression, everything else at its Batch BE count with zero drift — ah 5587, ai 2036, an 6052, aj 403, ak 527, al 559, ar 887, as 387, at 461, au 335, av 315, aw 338, ax 329, ay 455, az 489, ba 647, bb 172, bc 91, be 31, test_runes 2973, test_rune_battle 96, all 0 failures.
    Live autoplay clean, and it reads correctly in a real fight: "Deadfall armed — 4 springs, and it holds a trap slot until spent" / "a perfect rig: a fourth spring", then "springs on Orc Archer — 3 spring(s) left, resting", the stun costing the Archer that turn, and the trap coming back twice more across the fight.

2026-08-09 — Batch BE: Communion, 40 → 15

One number changes. Communion's chance falls from (40 × the recipient's Faith stacks)% to (15 × the recipient's stacks)%. Nothing else in the game moves — no other magnitude, no re-spec, no save version (still v7). The rest of this entry is the measurement that says whether it was enough, and the answer is partly.

THE BEFORE-AND-AFTER, WHICH IS THE DELIVERABLE. Every row n=200 battles, fixed lineup (berserker, cryomancer, inquisitor, beastmaster), identical flags either side. The “at 40” rows are Batch BC's grid, unchanged; the “at 15” rows are this batch. Two of them are CONTROLS that must not move — a build with no Communion in it cannot see this change — and both reproduce to within a point, which is what makes the rest of the table readable.

AND THE COMPARISON §3 ASKS FOR NEEDED ITS OTHER HALF BUILT, so it is built here and kept. BC's lane controls are one-hero (BULWARK 23%, ZEAL 42%); the all-four rows above had nothing to be read against. The Devout's other two lanes, all four heroes built, n=200, same lineup — a control set that did not previously exist and that the next batch should use rather than re-derive:

lane, all four builtcontributionhealing/battle releases/battlehealing per release
FAITH (at 15)45%4296.5562

BULWARK14%320.6025

* derived from BC's two reported figures; BC printed healing and releases for its all-four rows, not the quotient.

  • WHAT THE NUMBER BOUGHT. One-hero the row goes 80% → 73%, healing 2255 → 1555 (−31%) and releases 32.21 → 22.77 (−29%). All four built, it goes 55% → 45%, healing 695 → 429 and releases 10.54 → 6.55 (−38% each). Healing per release does not move — 67 → 65, 66 → 62 — which is the check that the lever landed on the axis it was aimed at. This was a frequency problem and the change bought frequency.
  • WHAT IT DID NOT BUY, said plainly. §3's bar was that FAITH should land near the Devout's other two lanes rather than at twice them. It does not. One-hero it reads 73% against ZEAL's 42% and BULWARK's 23%; all four built it reads 45% against ZEAL's 31% and BULWARK's 14%. The gap narrowed from 1.9× the next lane to 1.7× one-hero and from 1.8× to 1.45× all four. Both constructions agree: this is a real reduction and it is not the whole repair. (BC's one-hero lane controls were not re-run and did not need to be — neither lane contains Communion, the roll sits behind communion_ranks > 0, so a build without the node makes no draw at all and those rows are byte-identical by construction.) Per §3, this is reported and NOT chased with a second change.
  • §2's WORRY WAS RIGHT, AND THE MEASUREMENT TURNS IT ROUND IN A WAY NOBODY PREDICTED. Communion reads the recipient's CURRENT stacks, and Apostle parks allies at 5 rather than resetting them — so in an Apostle build the chance is not 15%, it is 75%, and every advance on a parked ally is itself a release. Three parked allies at 75% is ~2.25 expected advances per release, and above one the loop still sustains. It does: 73% is the Apostle row.
    The turn is in the −Apostle rows. AT 40, APOSTLE WAS WORTH 7 POINTS; AT 15 IT IS WORTH 10 (80→73 against 73→63), and the releases it buys go from +59% to +79%. The reprice made the capstone MORE valuable, not less, and the reason is exactly the saturation BC found: at 40, Binding Oath's three parked stacks already rolled 120% — a certainty — so Apostle's five were buying nothing but redundancy. At 15 the same three stacks roll 45% and five roll 75%, so Apostle is finally paying for something. Two nodes stopped being redundant back-ups for each other, which is a better tree than it was, and it is also why the row did not fall as far as the arithmetic suggests.
  • TWO CANDIDATE LEVERS EXIST FOR THAT INTERACTION AND NEITHER IS TAKEN. Recorded so the next batch starts from a decision rather than a blank page:
    • Communion reads stacks only below 5, so a parked ally is invisible to it — one condition at the existing gate. This is the one to take, for one reason above the others: its landing zone is already bracketed by a measured row. Under Apostle it makes Communion nearly inert (every ally sits at 5 permanently, so the node fires at most once per ally as they cross 4→5), which puts the Apostle build somewhere between the −Communion row and the full row — 48–73% one-hero, 25–45% all four. Nothing else on the table has its outcome pre-measured. It also attacks the coupling rather than the output: it does not care WHICH node parks the ally, and it leaves Communion whole in the seven builds that never take Apostle. Against it, honestly: 60% at four stacks and 0% at five is a cliff that has to be said out loud in the tooltip, and two nodes in one lane that cancel each other is a smell.
    • Communion fires at most once per battle turn — the _communion_chain guard already proves a limiter fits at that site. Rejected for now because its bite depends on fight length: it barely binds in a seven-round smoke and binds hard in a long boss fight, which is the wrong way round, and “a battle turn” has to be defined before it can be priced.
  • §4 — DEVOUTNESS'S ZERO IS THE INSTRUMENT, NOT THE NODE, AND THE BLIND SPOT IS BIGGER THAN THE DEVOUT. BC's grid read −Devoutness at 80% with slightly HIGHER healing than the full lane, for a node that removes 20% of the party's incoming Break damage. Determined at the sites rather than argued: the contribution share is (damage + healing + damage-prevented) over the same three summed across the party, and Break points appear in neither the numerator nor the denominator. The share's pool is built from exactly three keys — dmg_hero_, heal_hero_, prev_hero_ — so Break damage DEALT is outside it too, even though BD/b is printed as its own column right beside the share. faith_break_cut is banked (49 points a battle in the FAITH row) and read by the FAITH decomposition line, and it goes no further: _on_unit_credit branches it off and returns before the healing door.
    So the metric has a missing term, and the Devout is the least of it. The Warden's whole THREAT lane is Break — Batch AL measured it taking his BD/battle 104 → 320 and the party's Breaks/battle 1.02 → 2.35 — and none of that reaches a contribution share either. Every Break-focused build in the game is measured by an instrument that cannot see its output. Nothing is changed here; this is reported, and it is the item that gates a difficulty pass rather than the Devout repair is, because a difficulty pass designed against this metric would tune the game around damage and healing alone.
  • VERIFIED. check_parse 0, check_flow 0 (6 screens), 11 scenes 0 SCRIPT ERROR, run-harness gates 1/2/3 PASS, live autoplay clean. New test_batch_be.gd 31 checks / 0 failures, and the three rates in it are MEASURED over 1200 driven releases apiece rather than read off the formula: 1 stack 14.2% (want 15), 3 stacks 47.9% (want 45, was a certainty at 40), an Apostle-parked ally at 5 stacks 73.7% (want 75). §2's concern is therefore pinned as a number in a test rather than as prose. The _communion_chain guard is driven at the new value too, both ways: one release with three parked allies banks at most one release per hero, and the latch is a re-entrancy lock rather than a once-per-battle limiter (a second release still rolls).
    Four negative controls, each applied to the code and reverted (the suite came back to 31/0 byte-for-byte after the last): putting the payload back at 40 while the tooltip still says 15 trips 6 (including all three measured rates — 39.2%, 100%, 100%); putting the tooltip back at 40 while the payload still pays 15 trips 3; the chain latch never lowered (a once-per-battle limiter instead of a re-entrancy lock) trips 66; and the roll reading a constant 5 instead of the recipient's stacks trips 3 — which is the control that proves the rates are measured rather than re-derived, because the five-stack row still passes while the other two do not.
    Regression: every suite at its Batch BC count with zero drift — ah 5587, ah_battle 65, ai 2036, an 6052, aj 403, ak 527, al 559, ar 887, as 387, at 461, au 335, av 315, aw 338, ax 329, ay 455, az 489, ba 647, bb 172, bc 91, test_runes 2973, test_rune_battle 96, all 0 failures — with three re-points, each with the reason in the file: test_batch_aw's magnitude probe reads 15 (it is the file that priced the node at 40), test_batch_ah's master.html stamp gate moves BB → BE, and test_batch_bb's duplicate of that gate moves with it.
    ONE PRE-EXISTING BREAKAGE FOUND AND REPAIRED, and it is worth naming because of how it broke. test_batch_bb sliced the changelog on the bare phrase "Batch BB" to check that its own entry carried Batch BB's detonation numbers. Batch BC's entry then used that phrase in its regression line (“every suite at its Batch BB count”), which moved the first occurrence above BB's own heading — so from BC onward the slice was the tail of BC's entry and all four assertions failed for a reason that had nothing to do with what they were asking. Reproduced on unmodified HEAD before it was touched, so it is not this batch's; the anchor is the <h2> heading now, ending at the next one, which no later entry's prose can imitate. The four assertions are byte-unchanged — the question was always right, only the text it was asked of was wrong. Worth generalising: a doc assertion anchored on a phrase a later document can reproduce is a test that stops asking its question, silently.
    Live autoplay clean, and the change is visible in a real fight rather than only in the aggregate: a release under Apostle now spreads to two of the other three heroes on one turn and all three on the next. At 40 it reached all three every single time.

2026-08-09 — Batch BC: decomposing the Devout's FAITH row

This batch ships no gameplay change. No magnitude moves, no node is re-specced, no spec is touched, no save version moves (still v7). It exists to answer one question that two previous batches guessed at and missed — and the answer arrived in two parts, the second of which is worth more than the Devout repair it was commissioned for.

  • §0 THE INSTRUMENT FIRST, BECAUSE IT REFRAMES THE NUMBER. Every lane row this project has ever reported was measured with ONE hero fully built and three unbuilt. DOD_SIM_TALENTS is not a one-hero harness — it walks every hero in the party and force-learns whichever named ids appear in that hero's own spec tree. But all 288 node ids across the twelve trees are disjoint (verified, zero collisions), so a flag string naming one spec's lane builds exactly one hero. Nothing is broken and there is nothing to fix: the harness always built whoever was named, and only one spec was ever named. The repair is a flag string, not a code change.
    What that costs, measured rather than argued. The FAITH row re-run with all four heroes force-learned into their own full 8-node lanes: 80% contribution → 55–58% (55% with the other three on the lanes their own batches measured as standouts, 58% on the lanes they measured lowest — a tight bracket, so the result does not depend on which lanes were picked). Roughly a quarter of the 80% was methodology.
    And it inflates TWICE, which is the part worth keeping. A contribution share is a ratio, so three unbuilt allies shrink the denominator — that much was expected. They also feed the numerator: three weak allies take far more punishment, and the Devout's Faith comes from Divine Shield absorbing hits, so his engine spins faster beside heroes who are losing. Enemy damage/battle falls 207 → 74 when the party is built, and his releases fall with it, 32.2 → 11.1. A support measured beside unbuilt allies is measured in the conditions that suit him best.
    How to read the historical rows, then: they are honest A/B comparisons of one spec's lanes against each other, which is mostly what they were used for. They are not "this is how much of a real party's work this hero does". At least one of them was read that way.
  • §1/§2 THE ANSWER, AND IT IS THE FREQUENCY HALF. The FAITH row is large because it releases a great many times, not because it pays a lot per release. Across the whole lane the payout per release roughly triples (24 → 67 HP) while the number of releases goes up twenty-four-fold (1.33 → 32.2 a battle). Three of the eight nodes multiply frequency, one multiplies magnitude, and the three frequency nodes own the row.
    THE LEAVE-ONE-OUT GRID IS THE DELIVERABLE. Fixed lineup (berserker, cryomancer, inquisitor, beastmaster), n=200 battles for every row, one node withheld at a time, everything else identical:
rowcontributionhealing/battlereleases/battle healing per releasevs the full lane

− Blessed are the Faithful64%97330.8629−16 pts — the only magnitude term; frequency untouched
