  • COMMUNION IS THE ROW, and it is not close. Withholding one row-1 node takes the headline from 80% to 47% and the healing from 2255 to 444.
    THE MECHANISM IS LEGIBLE ONCE THE NUMBER POINTS AT IT, and it is worth more to a repair batch than the ranking is. Communion's chance is 40% × the other member's own Faith stacks — which reaches 100% AT THREE STACKS AND SATURATES THERE. It is written as a chance and is, in every build that matters, a certainty. TWO SEPARATE NODES PARK ALLIES ABOVE THE SATURATION POINT: Apostle keeps them at 5 (roll 200%) and Binding Oath keeps them at 3 (roll 120%). That is why the grid reads the way it does — withholding Apostle costs only 7 points, and withholding Binding Oath only 1, because each is a redundant back-up for the other's guarantee.
    PROVED RATHER THAN INFERRED, with a ninth row: withholding Communion AND Apostle together reads 48% | 465 healing | 6.93 releases, which is the Communion-only row (47% | 444 | 7.08) to within the noise band. Apostle contributes nothing measurable once Communion is gone — it is not an independent frequency multiplier, it is Communion's amplifier. They are one engine, and Communion is the half that multiplies.
  • THE RANKING SURVIVES §0'S CORRECTION, checked rather than assumed. Re-run under the all-four construction, withholding Communion reads 24% against the corrected lane's 55% — healing 695 → 137 and releases 10.54 → 2.21, i.e. −80% and −79%, against −80% and −78% one-hero. The two constructions disagree about the LEVEL and agree exactly about the CAUSE.
  • WHY THE LAST TWO FIXES UNDER-DELIVERED, with a number on it at last. Conviction's growth clause — the term Batch AY halved with precision — is 97 of the row's 2255 healing a battle, 4.3%. Halving 4.3% of something cannot move it, and no amount of care in the aiming would have changed that. The next lever the roadmap names (the growth's base step, 3% → 2%) is the same term again and is worth about 1.4% of the row. It should be expected to under-deliver for the same reason. Reported, not acted on: this batch changes nothing, and the repair is its own batch.
  • §1 THE DECOMPOSITION FOUND FIVE EFFECTS CREDITING NOBODY AT ALL, three of them healing and two of them the largest single heal in their own lane. Blessed Barrier's conversion and Afterglow's mend fire inside unit.gd, which cannot reach the sim ledger, and simply vanished; Healing Pulse and the Bulwark of Fortitude tick fire in battle.gd and were never booked; and Devoutness's Break reduction was counted nowhere in the game. All five are instrumented now, and the correction is visible in the control rows: the ZEAL lane reads 42% where Batch AW read 27%, because Healing Pulse alone is 219 of its 363 healing a battle, and BULWARK reads 23% against AW's 20%.
    The arithmetic is stated so the rows can be reconciled rather than trusted: take the newly-counted terms back out and ZEAL reads 144 healing against AW's 134 and BULWARK 50 against AW's 48, while the ungeared row reads 41 against AW's 40 and the FAITH row is untouched — a FAITH build learns none of the five nodes involved, which is why §2's grid is directly comparable with every historical FAITH row. BATCH AW'S DEVOUT BULWARK AND ZEAL ROWS ARE SUPERSEDED ON THE HEALING AND CONTRIBUTION COLUMNS; his FAITH and ungeared rows are not. Holy's and the Occultist's rows are untouched — none of the five effects is theirs.
  • ONE LOG-HONESTY FIX, FOUND BY INSTRUMENTING IT. Blessed Barrier discarded heal_amount's return, so its combat-log line and its floating number reported the heal it asked for rather than the one that landed — a heal into a full health bar read exactly like a heal into an empty one. It reports what landed now.
  • WHAT WAS ADDED IS INSTRUMENTATION AND NOTHING ELSE. One report line, faith_report_line(), shared by the standalone report and RunSim's exactly as ruin_report_line and its four siblings are, returning "" when no Devout stood. One booking function each for healing and for prevention, so the parts can never disagree with the total. One callback out of unit.gd for the three effects the battle scene cannot see. §2's leave-one-out needed no new harness flag at all — withholding a node is seven ids where there were eight, which DOD_SIM_TALENTS already does.
  • VERIFIED. check_parse 0, check_flow 0 (6 screens), 11 scenes 0 SCRIPT ERROR, run-harness gates 1/2/3 PASS, live autoplay clean. New test_batch_bc.gd 91 checks / 0 failures. Regression: every suite at its Batch BB count with zero drift, which is itself the check that matters for a batch that adds no gameplay and no pool entry — ah 5587, ah_battle 65, ai 2036, an 6052, aj 403, ak 527, al 559, ar 887, as 387, at 461, au 335, av 315, aw 338, ax 329, ay 455, az 489, ba 647, bb 172, test_runes 2973, test_rune_battle 96, all 0 failures.
    Five negative controls, each applied to the code and reverted (the suite came back to 91/0 after the last one): every barrier counting as a Divine Shield trips 2, folding Devoutness's Break points into damage-prevented trips 2, Blessed Barrier banking the heal it asked for trips 1, un-crediting Healing Pulse trips 2, and not banking the release count at the release trips 2.
    ONE CORRECTION TO AN EARLIER BATCH'S CLAIM, found in passing. Batch AR recorded test_rune_battle's standing defect as closed and stable; the check "pyromancer: rune_resist_pierce never fired" failed 1 run in 8 here and passed 4/4 on a stashed, unmodified HEAD, so it is not this batch. The residual cause is named so nobody re-diagnoses it: the check drives _resolve by hand and the file arms no_cover nowhere, so the forced hit still rolls the 5% miss — and a missed hit writes no resist line at all. AR fixed whether the line survives to be read; it did not fix whether the line is written. One line at the forced hit closes it. Reported, not taken — this batch changes nothing.
  • NOISE, STATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED, because this spec's numbers were flagged as sampling-noise-prone as far back as Batch W (heal/battle moving 10→14 and 25→13 in one measurement, opposite signs). The baseline row was run twice at n=200, nothing changed between them: 80% / 2255 healing / 32.21 releases against 79% / 2165 / 30.96 — about a point of contribution and 4% of the healing. SO THE BOTTOM THREE ROWS OF THE GRID ARE INSIDE THE BAND AND MUST NOT BE READ AS EFFECTS: Unwavering Faith, Sacred Covenant and Binding Oath move the headline by 1–2 points, which is what zero looks like at this sample size. Everything above them — Communion at −33, Blessed are the Faithful and Fervor at −16, Apostle at −7 — is far outside it. Binding Oath's 1 point is explained rather than dismissed: see the saturation note above.

2026-08-09 — Batch BB: clearing the deck

Six repairs carried over from the twelve spec batches — every one a thing that was found, recorded and deliberately left for later. No spec is re-authored, no magnitude is retuned, no tree is touched. Every id survives; no save version move (still v7).

  • §3 THE OCCULTIST'S DETONATION NUMBER, FINALLY PRINTED — and it leads because it is why the batch exists. ruin_report_line(), 40-run line, Ruin lane rows 1–7 at the standing threshold of 10: trash 0.07 detonations/battle (n=566, deepest mark 31) | boss 0.60 (n=25, deepest mark 18). Against AX's trash 0.00 (n=519, deepest 10) | boss 0.13 (n=23, deepest 12) and AY's trash 0.06 (n=774, deepest 20) | boss 0.35 (n=52, deepest 32). TRASH IS NOT ZERO, SO THE THREAD CLOSES. The condition for escalating to the first detonation at 5, every 10 after variant was "if trash is still zero"; it is not, and that variant stays unshipped. Nothing shipped off this measurement. Read the boss half as the real movement — 0.13 → 0.35 → 0.60 across three batches — and the trash half as flat but alive. Two caveats stated rather than buried: AY did measure both halves, but only into the project guide, so this is the first time the number has appeared in a changelog, which is what was actually being asked for; and this row was walked with Rot in the bargain pool (the bot took it 0.60/run), which AX's and AY's rows could not have been.
  • §1 THE PACK'S SWAP RULE IS BATCH Q'S AGAIN: the beast holding LESS LOYALTY is the one that goes, with a tie falling to the older so the rule is total rather than order-dependent. Batch AY shipped "replaces the older of the two", and that was a regression in the worst possible batch to make it in — AY is the batch that removed Loyalty's ceiling, and its own smoke measured a bond fifty stacks deep. The shipped rule could destroy a 50-stack partnership in favour of a fresh arrival, inside the spec whose whole spine is partnership depth. AY's stated reason does not survive the site: the newcomer is not on the field yet when the victim is chosen, so it can never be the one evicted. What the age rule really bought was the ability to rotate a deep bond OUT, and refusing that is the point. One implementation (_swap_victim) read by the summon AND the bot, so the beast the bot prices is the beast the summon frees; Batch Q's never two of the same kind clause is untouched, and it covers the swap clones as well as the summons. The capstone's own text and both swap tooltips now say what the code does.
  • §2 CREEPING DEATH WORKS UNDER PERFECTED TOXIN NOW. The capstone makes his poison permanent; the node refreshed a poison's duration; a poison with no clock has no duration to refresh — so Venom row 5 plus the Venom capstone owned a node that could never fire. You cannot extend forever, so you deepen instead: on a permanent poison the node ADDS A STACK, and on a clocked one it refreshes exactly as before. One clause, both nodes stay live, and it reads correctly from either text. Both halves read the full stamp the poison already carries rather than a constant of their own.
    The governor the refresh version never needed: the node fires on applying any status to a poisoned enemy, and since Batch BA a single Distillate cast lands poison, Exposed and Slowed together. Refreshing three times is refreshing once — but adding three stacks is not adding one. The stack clause is limited to once per enemy per turn; the refresh clause is deliberately left firing freely, because each half is bounded by what it does.
  • §4 CALL OF THE WILD'S BODILESS BLOWS ARE MEASURED. Batch AY confirmed that a beast's damage credits its hunter at one named site, which is what makes every Beastmaster number readable — and found that _ghost_hit, the spirit strike an absent kind makes, booked no damage at all. It is credited the same way now, through the same resolution, so the two sites cannot drift. There is no Break half to mirror: checked at the site rather than assumed, the ghost blow deals a hardcoded zero pressure. It is a trophy ability, so this changes no balance — it changes whether a Beastmaster who earned Call of the Wild is measured honestly.
  • §5 ROT IS BACK, AT SEVERITY 4: everyone's maximum health is halved, on both sides, for the fight and no longer. Batch AQ authored it, implemented it and dropped it during that batch, because the battle-end save sync writes each unit's maximum straight back onto the party member — so a one-fight bargain would have cost the party half its health for the rest of the run, with nothing crashing to announce it. AQ recorded the fix it needed; Batch AW then built exactly that pattern for the opposite sign. The severity-4 pool is four, which was AQ's stated target, and the pool is twenty.
    Three fields now meet at one line and their signs differ, so both syncs state the ordering: Tenacity's growth and Conviction's growth are subtracted (one-fight gains), Rot's reduction is added back (a one-fight loss), and all three stay separate — Tenacity's has a second reader in Unkillable's mend, which means "the pool he brought into the battle" and nothing else.
    Rot is NOT excluded from anything that reads maximum health, and that is the design rather than an oversight: Unkillable's mend, the below-half Mercy window, Conviction's growth and the Devout's whole kit are ratios, so they scale with the smaller pool exactly as every other both-parties modifier does. It is why Rot is severity 4. One correction, from checking rather than trusting: they are not all ratios. Tenacity adds a flat +15 maximum health per block, so under Rot it claws back a slightly larger share of a smaller pool. Nothing absurd, nowhere near the runaway that was feared — reported, not changed.
  • §6 ASHES OF AL'AR HAS A HOME: every Mage can EARN it. Batch AR removed the Pyromancer's self-revive along with every other defensive option in that spec, which was right for a spine built on having no escape hatch, and left the ability homeless. It is now 30 Mana, 2.5 initiative, no cooldown, self-cast: the next blow that would kill you this battle returns you at 25% of maximum health instead (Perfect: 40%), once per battle — in the Mage class pool, restoring it to twelve entries, and in all three Mage spec pools, which is where a zone boss actually draws from. AR's rule is untouched: the Pyromancer still has no defensive option anywhere in his kit or his tree. He can buy the escape hatch back only by spending a boss pick on it and a turn to arm it — a real choice against his own spine rather than a hole in it.

2026-08-09 — Batch BA: the Survivalist — attrition through craft

Last of the twelve. Every talent tree in the game is now purpose-authored. The spine was already half-written: attrition — breadth of affliction, not depth of one. Trapper's +8% damage per different status on the target is the cleanest statement of a spine in the game, and this is the one spec whose ceiling is correct and is deliberately kept — breadth is bounded by how many distinct debuffs exist, which is a design constant rather than a dial. Overburn, Loyalty, Focus, Resonance and Ruin all lost theirs; this one keeps its. Every one of his 24 ids survives and re-specs in place; no new ids, none deleted, no save version move (still v7). One spec only: the other eleven trees and enemy tuning are untouched.

  • §0 THE INSTRUMENT. Damage share is a valid read for him (Pressure archetype), and Batch W has him at 25/25/25/26 — the most stable spec in the game across every field size. One new number is printed, and for this spec it is the whole batch: the average count of distinct statuses standing on a target when he strikes it. That count is his damage multiplier, so it is the only figure that says whether the Venom re-specs below actually worked — a damage share moves for a dozen reasons, breadth moves for one. It is banked at the site that reads it, shared by the standalone report and the run report, and printed only when a Survivalist stood.
  • §1 THE CONTAGION SPACE IS RESERVED, AND HE WAS OCCUPYING IT. A future spec is planned whose fantasy is disease and virality, and four of his seven Venom nodes were that character's idea — one of them a capstone. Epidemic (every enemy permanently Poisoned — a pandemic), Plague Bearer (the rot leaps from enemy to enemy), Creeping Death (a poisoned corpse passes its stacks to the living) and the name Virulence (a pathogen term outright) are all re-specced off it.
    Poison itself is not reserved and stays entirely his. Poison is craft — curare, hemlock, a blade wiped on the right leaf. What goes is anything that spreads on its own. The distinction to hold: a hunter who knows which plant does what versus a plague that no longer needs him. The reservation is recorded as a standing design rule rather than as four edits, so a later batch cannot re-add "spreads to another enemy" innocently and spend the new spec's idea a second time. Snares and Guerilla are untouched by it — traps, denial and endurance were never disease.
  • §2 POISON BECOMES A CARRIER OF BREADTH. His Venom lane built depth while his passive paid for breadth: a poison build earned +8% where a five-affliction build earned +40%, so the lane named for his signature damage was fighting his own passive. Now every Venom node hangs a different affliction off the poison. Coated Blades carries Cripple, Distillate carries Exposed, and Slow Acting carries Slowed — the pun that node has left unclaimed since Batch 33. Measured: one application from a Distillate + Slow Acting build lands three distinct statuses, i.e. +24% off a single cast. Necrosis stays exactly as written, name included — tissue death from venom is what venomous bites actually do, it is craft rather than contagion, and it was already the only node in the lane pointed at breadth rather than depth.
  • §3 THE THREE LANES, RE-PRICED AT ROW PRICING (the Hunter's 3–4×, as the Beastmaster and Sharpshooter took — not the Cleric three's 4–5×). All three lane names and theses stand, re-aimed only in what Venom's nodes do: the affliction that ticks · the affliction that stops · the affliction that adds up.

  • Potent Toxins stays flat damage rather than becoming a percentage of Attack, deliberately. Converting it would change the poison-application site's units and every rune riding that counter with it, for a gain the reprice already delivers. Eight times the value, same units, no read-site change — which is exactly why the two runes paying into it needed no re-point at all.
  • Quartermaster is party-wide craft with nothing self-propagating — he oils his allies' blades. It is the closest thing to Plague Bearer's reach that stays out of the reserved space. The poison it applies is his: the tick reads his Attack and his Potent Toxins, the Venom carriers come with it, and the damage is credited to him rather than to whoever swung.
  • A live bug, found and fixed: Quick Rigging's cooldown clause had no implementation at all. The node's tooltip has promised "Snare Trap's cooldown is reduced by 1" since Batch 33 and the code carried only the Cripple half — nothing crashed, nothing logged, the tooltip simply lied. Same shape as Measured Rage in Batch AJ, through a different door.
  • §4 BOTH NAMED EXCLUSIVE PAIRS GO, AND THE LIST IS NOW EMPTY. Virulence ↔ Slow Acting had already dissolved on its own — they sit in Venom rows 3 and 4, so row exclusivity lets a player hold both, which under the new lane means Exposed and Slowed together (intended, and worth stating). Plague Bearer ↔ Deadfall Network went with Plague Bearer, and its replacement needs no pair: Quartermaster and three traps share row 7, so row exclusivity already enforces the choice. Nothing remains in the list — every pair ever authored has either dissolved under Batch AI's row exclusivity or is a same-row pair that row exclusivity handles correctly.
  • §5 HE OWES NO ABILITY FALLBACK, AND THAT COMPLETES THE TWELVE. His tree grants no abilities at all — Tripwire, Shrapnel Charge and Snare Trap are base kit, and Explosive Shot, Venom Coating, Hamstring, Deadfall and Harvest are all boss-trophy pool — so the collision Batch AU's rule exists for cannot arise. Asserted both ways, and recorded rather than left to a reader to wonder whether his shelf was skipped by accident.
  • §6 EVERY COUNTER IS ADDITIVE, AND THREE CHANGED MEANING RATHER THAN UNITS. Every magnitude now lives in the units its read site sums, so a node and a rune each pay what they advertise, alone and stacked. Plague Bearer and Epidemic no longer exist as fields at all (deleted with their read sites rather than renamed in place, so a later batch cannot write one), and Creeping Death left the death path entirely for the status-application path. The rune audit came back clean and is recorded rather than assumed: no Survivalist or Hunter rune ever rode any of the three, so nothing needed flagging for re-authoring. The four spec runes all still pay exactly what they paid — only the units moved.
  • §7 HARVEST NOW PAYS FOR WHAT IT ACTUALLY REMOVED. It counted the statuses on the target before its own purge, so a poison that cannot be cleansed survived the cleanse and was billed for anyway — a known quirk since Batch 33, minor while his breadth was narrow. This batch widens that breadth substantially, so the over-count would have grown with it. The count is measured across the purge now rather than predicted. Measured: against three standing statuses of which two are reapable, Harvest deals 19 where the old over-count paid ~30. That is a real reduction under Slow Acting and Perfected Toxin builds, and it is correct — it was charging the enemy for a status it did not take away.
  • §8 THE BOT: BREADTH BEFORE DEPTH. His damage is the count of distinct statuses, so the rotation prefers an ability that adds a status the target lacks over one that deepens a status it already has: Snare and Hamstring lead, and another poison application onto an already-poisoned mark waits below them. Harvest's threshold moved off four and reads the same yield the ability itself is paid on, so the bot and the ability can never disagree about what the button is worth. Instrument honesty, not tuning — no difficulty measurement belongs in this batch.
  • Kit smoke (fixed lineup, 40 battles a row, all rows 40/40 wins, no script errors). Survivalist damage share and Trapper breadth: ungeared 23% (149/battle), 1.51 statuses per strike; VENOM 28% (187), 1.94 — the carriers landing, and the most ever seen on one target rising from 5 to 7; SNARES 21% (137), 1.65; GUERILLA 29% (200), 1.53. Kit-mechanics ratios only; no difficulty signal.

2026-08-08 — Batch AZ: the Sharpshooter — patience

Second of the Hunter three. The spine is patience: his power is in not looking away. Focus already carried a real cost — earned by staying on one enemy, lost by switching — and the Master Document already named the tension the spec exists for, so this is a spine that fits plus the repricing every unauthored tree still needed. Every one of his 24 ids survives and re-specs in place; no new ids, none deleted, no save version move (still v7). One spec only: the other eleven trees and enemy tuning are untouched.

  • §0 THE INSTRUMENT, AND A STANDING INSTRUCTION CARRIED FORWARD. Damage share IS a valid read for him — he is a Nuker damage archetype, unlike the Cleric three — and Batch W has him mid-pack at 32–36%. Batch W also retired the old "Sharpshooter leads damage at 38%" flag as a stale number from a party that no longer exists, with the words never tune against it, and that instruction stands: if his rows come back high after this batch, that is reported, not acted on. One new number is printed, shared by the standalone report and RunSim's: the deepest Focus reached, paired with the critical multiplier it was paying at that depth. §1 removes the ceiling, so "how deep does his patience actually get" stopped being answerable from the design and has to be measured. It is banked at the gain site, because a switch, a kill and One Shot all wipe the meter.
  • §1 FOCUS HAS NO CEILING, AND ITS PAYOFF CONVERTS AT 100. One rule, one number, split in two: the first 100 points of Focus each grant +0.5% critical chance, exactly as before, and every point beyond 100 grants +0.5% of his critical multiplier instead. Lethal Aim's ×2 becomes ×2.5 at 200 Focus, ×3 at 300, and it never stops paying — patience buys certainty first, then force. Focus is otherwise unchanged: +20 on attacking the enemy he attacked last turn, cleared on switching, up to 50 retained on a kill.
    The conversion point is a fixed 100, deliberately, and not "whenever crit chance reaches 100%". The alternative was tried on paper and fails: with Steady Hands, Consistent Aim and Tunnel Vision a built marksman passes 100% chance at very low Focus, which would make the chance half of his own passive vestigial and make the conversion depend on what else he had stacked. A fixed threshold is legible in a tooltip — the first hundred is your aim, everything past it is your force — and it keeps those three nodes meaningful, because they are what buy reliable crits while Focus is still shallow. The rate past 100 is a best guess at a number, said plainly: if the tail comes back far past ×3–3.5 the lever is that rate, not the threshold.
    Two consumers read Focus as a quantity rather than a fraction, and both were checked at their sites. Coup de Grâce still consumes all Focus but reads at most 200 points of it (at 400 Focus its clause would be 400% of the target's missing health), and One Shot's "at maximum Focus" became a number: 200, since §1 left no maximum for it to name. Spray of Arrows is the one node that still hands the meter a ceiling, at 50, and that cap is what its breadth costs.
  • §2 UNWAVERING CHANGES SIDES. It was the escape hatch — switching targets halves your Focus instead of clearing it — which is a spec about not looking away selling a discount on looking away, from inside the spine's own lane. Re-specced to reward staying: each consecutive turn attacking the same enemy grants +10 additional Focus, rising by 10 each turn to a maximum of +50, and resetting the moment he switches. Patience that accelerates rather than patience he can skip. Spray of Arrows stays the honest way out — it caps Focus at 50 and costs him Tunnel Vision, which is a real opt-out priced against the spine rather than a softening of it.
  • §3 ALL 24 NODES RE-PRICED AT ROW PRICING (3–4×), and all three lane names and theses stand — Precision, Penetration and Tempo were never lying about their jobs. Old → new:
    PRECISION — Steady Hands +4% → +15% crit chance · Perfect Form +20 → +40 Focus on a crit · Deep Focus RE-SPECCED (was "the Focus cap rises from 100 to 150", which §1 left with no premise): the conversion point falls from 100 Focus to 60 · Executioner's Eye ×2.1 → ×2.5 · Consistent Aim rewritten: your critical multiplier is reduced by 0.5 — but you gain +60% (was +30%) crit chance · Unwavering RE-SPECCED, per §2 · Tunnel Vision ±50% → ±100%.
    PENETRATION — Armor Piercer 8% → 30% · Sundering Shot 15 → 45 Break damage · Bonecracker +12% → +40% · Opportunist's Aim doubles → triples Powershot's Break scaling (+6% a point) · Exposed Nerve gains a second clause: +15% damage to Exposed enemies · No Cover unchanged — a bypass, not a magnitude, and there is nothing there to reprice · Overkill gains a second clause: the carry keeps your Focus in full rather than dropping it to the usual 50.
    TEMPO — Fletcher's Speed +5 → +18 · Snap Shot 1 → the first two abilities · Muscle Memory +10 → +30 Focus a shot · Opening Volley 60 → 150 · Follow-Through −1 → −2 cooldown turns · Second Nature 2 → 4 covered attacks · Spray of Arrows 1 → 2 additional enemies.
    CAPSTONES — One Shot fires at 200 or more Focus · Through and Through unchanged · Rapid Fire 35% → 50%.
    Deep Focus is the cleanest re-spec available — same name, same lane, and it becomes a dial on the new mechanic rather than a dead ceiling raise. Consistent Aim's rewording is load-bearing, not cosmetic: it used to set the multiplier to 1.5, which was coherent only while it was exclusive with Executioner's Eye, and §4 shows that pair is dead. Written as −0.5 it composes — ×1.5 alone, ×2.5 with Executioner's Eye alone, ×2.0 with both. Opening Volley at 150 opens him past the conversion point, which is the Tempo lane's whole argument in one node: he arrives already converting rather than earning his way there. Overkill's second clause ties the Penetration lane to the spine — a kill-chain is the one time switching targets is not disloyalty, so it should not cost him the bond.
  • §4 ONE EXCLUSIVE PAIR DISSOLVES, ONE SURVIVES. Executioner's Eye ↔ Consistent Aim is dead: Batch 32 authored it as a fork, and Batch AI's row exclusivity destroyed it — they sit in Precision rows 4 and 5, so a player can hold both. The pair survived only as prose in the project guide, and that prose is corrected; §3's −0.5 rewording is what makes holding both coherent, since the two nodes previously contradicted each other outright. Tunnel Vision ↔ Spray of Arrows survives untouched, because both sit in row 7 and row exclusivity is already enforcing it correctly — stated so a later batch does not "fix" it.
  • §5 THE TROPHY-POOL COLLISION CANNOT ARISE, and it is recorded rather than left to a reader. His tree grants no abilities at all: Aimed Shot, Powershot and Hold Breath are base kit, and Quick Draw, Triple Shot, Coup de Grâce, Pinning Shot and Called Shot all come from the boss-trophy pool. Asserted both ways — no node carries a grant, and a fully-learned tree adds nothing to the action bar's own ability list.
  • §6 EVERY COUNTER IS ADDITIVE — each holds its own magnitude in the units its read site sums, so a node and a rune each pay their advertised number alone and stacked. Three were named for conversion; every other node whose magnitude is a number took the same treatment, reported rather than silently generalised. Four remain honest flags (No Cover, Overkill, Through and Through) because they buy a rule, not an amount.
    Rune audit, all four re-pointed. Three still pay exactly what they paid, only the units moved: the Narrow Gap, the Long Draw (still scarred, still −10 Speed) and the Level Aim. The Rune of the Deep Sight is the one that could not pay what it paid, and it is reported rather than hidden: "his Focus climbs to 150" has no equivalent value once there is no ceiling. Re-pointed, not deleted — it keeps the relationship, moving the conversion point 8 points sooner (a fifth of Deep Focus's own 40), and its description was rewritten to the new units rather than left lying. Its second clause, crits granting +20 Focus, is untouched. The collision the brief asked about, resolved and stated: the Long Draw's opening Focus and Opening Volley's 150 now sum to 210, which is the additive rule the whole tree uses, and the rune's text says "60 more" so neither claim is false. The three Hunter class-wide runes touch no Sharpshooter counter, asserted.
  • §7 THE BOT: it never switches targets while its mark lives. Focus is the spec, and a bot that retargets on convenience measures a spec nobody plays — so it gives up a target only when that target is dead. Checking the code rather than the description found why the fix belongs at the top of the branch: the Sharpshooter rotation already worked one mark, but the class-pool abilities he can earn — Shrapnel Charge, Snare Trap, Hamstring, Harvest, Deadfall — each aimed at whatever the generic picker chose, and would have quietly broken the meter they were priced against. Coup de Grâce's threshold moved off 80, which was most of a capped meter and is a fraction of an uncapped one: it now waits for the 200 the ability can actually read, off the same constant the damage site uses, so the two can never disagree about what it is priced for. Instrument honesty, not tuning — no difficulty measurement belongs in this batch.

2026-08-08 — Batch AY: the Beastmaster — partnership (and The Pack, built)

First of the Hunter three, and he goes first rather than last on Batch X's own precedent that the companion machinery "deserves a pass rather than being the tired third of three." The spine is partnership: his power lives in another body, and it is the only resource in the game that can die. The Hunter class's old three-way contrast — three meters in three places — is the axis the Cleric now uses, so it is retired for partnership / patience / attrition. Every one of his 24 ids survives and re-specs in place; no new ids, none deleted, no save version move (still v7). §8 and §9 carry the two open fixes from AX.

  • §0 THE INSTRUMENT, CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED. Unlike the Cleric three, damage share is a valid read for a Ramp damage archetype — but half his output comes from a body that is not him, so the batch verified the attribution instead of trusting it. IT CREDITS THE HUNTER. _companion_hit books dmg_hero_<pack_master> at one named site, so every Beastmaster number ever measured is readable and this batch's rows are comparable with Batch W's. Two new numbers are printed, shared by the standalone report and RunSim's: the deepest Loyalty reached (§2 removed its ceiling, so "how deep does it get" stopped being answerable from the design) and, under The Pack only, the share of hunter turns on which two beasts were genuinely standing.
  • §1 THE PACK IS BUILT — AND MOST OF IT WAS ALREADY THERE. The brief's premise that it still read "COMING SOON, deferred" was stale: that text survives only in Batch 30's changelog entry, and _beast_cap, the beasts array and the per-kind Loyalty dict were all already live. What this batch built is the five edge cases that would otherwise have been decided by accident. Both boons apply at FULL strength (Menagerie stays the half-strength node and now carries a third, absent beast's boon instead of a first's). Ursus's 100 + hunter_idx taunt encoding survives two beasts and still prefers the bear. Call of the Wild strikes the two live bodies, ghosts the absent one, and still never spends Lone Bond's one summon.
  • ONE SOUL NOW SPANS THREE BODIES, and it needed a field change to do it honestly. soul_partner was a single pointer, so a second summon silently overwrote it and the older beast kept a link the hunter no longer answered. It is soul_bond, an array holding every living member, written in one place — and a wound divides across all of them: 30 damage is 10/10/10.
  • THE SWAP REPLACES THE OLDER OF THE TWO, not the lower-Loyalty one — and that is a correction §2 forces rather than a preference. With an uncapped meter the beast you just called always holds the lower Loyalty, so the old rule would have evicted the newcomer immediately and made rotation impossible under the very capstone whose lane is named for it.
  • LONE BOND CLOSES THE PACK, and row exclusivity was NOT already handling it. Lone Bond is Devotion row 7 and The Pack is a row-8 capstone with no lane-purity requirement, so a player could hold both — "one beast per fight" plus "two beasts at once", the one combination that must be impossible. Rather than build picker-level exclusion machinery, it is resolved where the number is read (_beast_cap returns 1 under Lone Bond) and both node descriptions say so, so the door is visible before the point is spent.
  • §2 LOYALTY HAS NO CEILING, AND THE BOON IS A CURVE. It capped at 5 and the boon doubled there, so partnership plateaued and raising the ceiling was three different nodes' job — the identity was a node question instead of an identity. The boon is ×(1 + 0.20 × Loyalty), continuous: ×2 at five stacks — exactly today's doubling, so nothing a player has learned became wrong — ×3 at ten, ×5 at twenty, and it keeps climbing. _loyalty_cap returns a sentinel rather than a large number (a large number is a ceiling a later batch reaches by accident), and exactly one node still hands it a number: Wild Rotation caps at 3, and that cap IS its cost.
  • THE ONE CLAMP THE CURVE FORCED, and it is a guard rather than a magnitude. Ursus's Savage Presence is 1 − 0.10 × boon on damage taken, so an uncapped boon crosses zero and enemy attacks would start healing him (at the Absolute Devotion + Ancient Pact step, from Loyalty 13). The mitigation is bounded at 75%; the curve is not bounded, because the curve is the identity.
  • §3 ALL 24 NODES RE-PRICED AT ROW PRICING, 3–4×, and the lane theses sharpened without renaming: DEVOTION is partnership in depth, THE PACK is partnership in breadth, HANDLER is what he does when the partnership is not the answer. THREE NODES ARE RE-SPECCED RATHER THAN REPRICED, because §2 took their premise: Absolute Devotion was a ceiling dial and is now the lane's thesis (the boon's step 20% → 35% a stack); Ancient Pact tripled at a threshold and now doubles the step again (both taken, +70% a stack against an unhealable beast — terrifying and fragile in equal measure); Lone Bond's payoff was a higher ceiling and is now arrival at 6 Loyalty and double gain thereafter. Vengeance carries the dead beast's boon for the rest of the battle rather than a status's duration.
  • §4 STEADFAST BOND ↔ VENGEANCE: THERE WAS NOTHING LEFT TO DISSOLVE. Batch 30 authored it as a fork and it survives only in that batch's changelog entry — not in the tree data, not in CLAUDE.md's prose list, not in master.html. Recorded rather than actioned. LONE BOND ↔ WILD ROTATION SURVIVES UNTOUCHED because both sit in row 7 and row exclusivity enforces it correctly; stated here so a later batch does not "fix" it.
  • §5 THE TROPHY-POOL COLLISION CANNOT ARISE, and it is now recorded instead of left to a reader. His tree grants no abilities at all — the summons, Hunter's Instinct and Kill Command are base kit, and Bestial Wrath, Spirit Bond, Primal Surge, Call of the Wild and Mark of the Hunt come from the boss-trophy pool — so he owes no AU §1 fallback in either direction, and no trophy can land on a node's grant. Asserted both ways.
  • §6 EVERY COUNTER IS ADDITIVE (the AR/AS/AT/AV/AW/AX form). Two hold the increase on a base the kit already pays and are named _step for it: wild_communion_step on the passive's own 5% and absolute_step on the boon's own 20%. BOTH ARE FLOATS AND ARE DELIBERATELY ABSENT FROM Runes.STAT_INT_KEYS — the Rune of the Deep Bond pays 1.5, and an int coercion would round it to 1 with nothing crashing (AT's conduit_step lesson). The name trap is asserted: wild_communion_step is not communion_ranks, which is the Devout's, and Batch 29 crossed the two once already.
  • RUNE AUDIT — ALL FOUR RE-POINTED, AND THREE STILL PAY EXACTLY WHAT THEY PAID. The Turning Pack (momentum_ranks 1 → 8), the Loosened Straps (masters_aim_ranks 2 → 12) and the Shared Wild (1 → 1.5 / 8 / 0.05 untouched) each advertise and pay their old number in the new units. THE RUNE OF THE DEEP BOND IS THE ONE THAT COULD NOT: its "Loyalty climbs one stack higher" clause has no equivalent value once there is no ceiling, so — flagged, not deleted — it keeps the relationship and pays into the boon's step instead (absolute_step 3, one fifth of the node, matching the ratio its other clause already had). The three Hunter class-wide runes touch no Beastmaster counter, asserted. ONE CONSEQUENCE WORTH NAMING: Quick Whistle now shaves the whole swap cooldown, so the Turning Pack's "returns a turn sooner" is inert for a player who takes that node — it still pays in every other build. loyalty_cap_bonus was deleted with its premise, not left unreachable.
  • §7 THE BOT summons first and keeps something standing (Loyalty only accrues while a beast lives, so a bot that summons late measures a spec nobody plays), fills both slots under The Pack rather than swapping the first, and swaps only when the incoming boon is the better one — _bot_boon_worth weighs each kind's own effect against the live field and multiplies by the Loyalty curve, so an established beast is genuinely expensive to give up, and a 25% margin stops it churning.
  • §8 THE OCCULTIST: RUIN GENERATION DOUBLED. AX measured 0.00 detonations per battle in trash and concluded the threshold was not the problem — generation was: his corruption accumulated to seven and the payoff sat at ten. Wrath of the Old Gods marks with 2 Ruin per debuff instead of 1 (OLD_GODS_MARK, one constant read at the four sites that are the passive marking a debuff he applied; Delirium, Unraveling and Spread of Madness keep their own magnitudes). The threshold stays at 10 and Avatar of Ruin stays at 5 — the variant AX named is still not shipped.
  • §9 THE DEVOUT: A RELEASE THAT CONSUMED NO STACKS GRANTS HALF GROWTH (1.5% of base rather than 3%). Apostle stops releases consuming stacks, so allies park at 5 and every further gain re-triggers, multiplying growth, release heal and Communion's spread together — AW measured +72.9% growth a battle, AX re-measured +83.7% with a peak of 390 HP on a 175 base, and nothing had yet addressed it. This hits the multiplier precisely and leaves the base spec, the step and the fantasy alone.
  • DOC DRIFT CORRECTED TOWARD THE CODE, found in passing: the Occultist's in-game passive description still described the pre-AX Ruin — "max 5", a flat 10% lifesteal, a threshold of 5, 50% of Attack and a 15% party heal. Every one of those numbers moved in Batch AX and that string was missed. It now matches the code.

2026-08-08 — Batch AX: the Occultist — corruption

Third of the Cleric three, and the class is done. His tree needed the least structural work of the three — Ruin / Madness / Leech are genuinely distinct axes with real cross-lane plumbing, and no lane was lying about its job (Batch L did good work). What he needed was the same 4–5× repricing and a spine: corruption — his power is not in him at all, it is in what he has done to them. Every one of his 24 ids survives and re-specs in place; no new ids, none deleted, no save version move (still v7). §7 carries a finding on the Devout's Fervor rather than a change.

  • §1 RUIN HAS NO MAXIMUM AND IT NEVER CLEARS. Corruption that resets is not corruption. A detonation now consumes the primer and nothing else: the mark stays, so the +2%-per-stack amplification climbs for the whole battle. The detonation moves from the 5th stack to every 10th — thresholds at 10, 20, 30 and onward — and the implementation tracks the next multiple crossed, not an equality: with stacks that survive, == 5 would arm once and never again, and >= 5 would arm on every stack forever. The blast is bigger because it costs twice as long to earn: 50% → 90% of the Occultist's Attack, and the party heal 15% → 25% of his max health. Not quite double, because the persisting stacks are themselves new value.
  • THE CONSEQUENCE, STATED RATHER THAN BURIED: his signature payoff largely leaves ordinary fights. On a focused target he builds roughly 1.5 Ruin a turn, so ten stacks is about seven turns and a trash fight ends in seven or eight. That is the intent — a trash enemy will not survive long enough for the stacks to run away and a boss will, which makes him the boss specialist by construction. It is also a real loss of moment-to-moment feel, so it is measured and reported, not assumed. IT MEASURED ZERO. Across 519 trash battles of a 40-run line (Ruin-lane build) the trash rate is 0.00 detonations/battle, deepest mark 10; a 200-battle standalone smoke with the entire Ruin lane force-learned also reads 0.00, and its deepest mark is 7 — ten stacks is not merely rare in an ordinary fight, it is out of reach. The boss half is thin rather than healthy: 0.13 detonations/battle over 23 boss fights, deepest mark 12. Per §1's own instruction the variant that would fix it — first detonation at 5, every 10 after, which keeps the trash payoff and the boss escalation both — was deliberately NOT shipped on this batch's own initiative. The number is the report; the decision is the designer's.
  • UNCAPPED STACKS MEAN EVERY PER-STACK EFFECT NEEDED A RUNAWAY CHECK. There are two, and they get opposite treatment. The damage amplification is left uncapped — +2% a stack, +5% with Deeper Hex, climbing all fight; on a boss that is the intended payoff of long corruption and it is the reason the spine works. The lifesteal is capped. It becomes per stack (2% each, replacing the flat 10%-while-any-Ruin) — the axis the passive had been wasting — and the whole draught is capped at 40% of the damage dealt, whatever the stacks and whatever the talents, because per-stack lifesteal against an uncapped number would let the party heal more than it deals. Soul Leech, Gluttony and Soul Glut all sit under it.
  • §2 THE MADNESS LANE'S BOSS PROBLEM IS MADE LEGIBLE, NOT PATCHED. Psychosis, Bewitch, Hysteria and Mass Hysteria all resist a boss unless it is Broken, so a Madness build read as dead in the nine fights a run that decide the run. It is not dead — it is gated behind a task, and the task is his other lane's job. Broken Will and Entropy exist to grind Break and they got much bigger here, so breaking the boss and then maddening it is a genuine two-step plan. No boss workaround was added. What changed is that the rule is now stated where a player meets it: the Madness lane's node text, the Psychosis / Bewitched / Mass Hysteria glossary entries (all three now name Break as the key), and the Bewitch / Mind Flay / Mass Hysteria ability tooltips.
  • §3 ALL 24 NODES RE-PRICED AT ROW PRICING, magnitudes 4–5× for the same reason Holy's and the Devout's were. All three lane names and theses stand — this is the one Cleric tree that did not need re-aiming. Full table in §7 of the Master Document. Deeper Hex is now the most dangerous node in the game and wants watching: against uncapped stacks it is a multiplier on a number with no ceiling, and at twenty stacks it is +100% damage taken (measured at ×1.96 in the batch test). That is the intended shape of a long boss fight, and it is the first thing to check if a boss row comes back absurd. Avatar of Ruin is re-specced rather than repriced: keeping the stacks is the default now, so the capstone moves the threshold instead — every 5th stack instead of every 10th, doubling his detonation rate. It is the answer for a player who wants the payoff back in ordinary fights.
  • §4 TWO AUTHORED FALLBACKS (AU §1) replacing his generics: Mind Flay already owned → it strikes three minions instead of two; Mass Hysteria already owned → its cooldown drops from 4 to 2. With that the Cleric class owes no generic fallbacks at all, and test_batch_au's floor moves 11 → 9 with the reason in the file (it falls on purpose, one class batch at a time).
  • §5 EVERY OCCULTIST COUNTER IS ADDITIVE (the AR/AS/AT/AV/AW form): the payload holds the magnitude and the read site applies no step of its own. Four hold the INCREASE on a base the kit already pays and are named _step for it — deep_hex_step on the passive's 2%, soul_leech_step on its 2% lifesteal, whispers_step on Psychosis's 50%, and the batch named three; the fourth (barter_step, on Dark Pact's 15%) has the same shape and takes the same treatment — reported, not silently generalised. spread_ranks SPLIT into spread_ranks (a chance) + spread_ruin (a stack count), because one counter cannot honestly hold both. pleasure_ranks was RENAMED pleasure_pct: its magnitude is 2.5 and Runes.STAT_INT_KEYS coerces anything ending in _ranks to an int, which would have silently rounded it down. avatar_ruin is the gate AND the magnitude in one field (it holds the threshold it installs).
  • RUNE AUDIT — three re-pointed, and two of them still pay exactly what they paid before this batch (only the units moved): the Deepening Ruin (deep_hex_ranks 1 → deep_hex_step 1, entropy_ranks 1→5) and the Whispering Dark (broken_will_ranks 1→5, spread_ranks 1→15, + spread_ruin 1 — the old spread site marked the newly maddened with 1 Ruin unconditionally, so the rune paid it and now has to say so, pleasure_ranks 1 → pleasure_pct 0.5). THE HOLLOW CHALICE IS THE ONE THAT COULD NOT. §1 changed the lifesteal's unit from a flat rate to a per-stack one, so "5% more from Ruined targets" has no equivalent value at all. It keeps the relationship it has always had — exactly one node's worth of each dial (soul_leech_step 3, gluttony_ranks 3) — and its description was rewritten to the new units rather than left lying. Reported rather than filed as a magnitude change (rune magnitude has been closed since Batch AF). The Hollow Chalice's healing clamp was CHECKED rather than trusted, as §5 asked: the worst reachable loadout sums to −0.60 against a floor of −1.00, so Batch AA's guard comment still holds — and the batch test now computes it rather than believing it. The three Cleric class-wide runes touch no Occultist counter, asserted. No Occultist exclusive pair survives in the data or in CLAUDE.md's prose: Batch L already retired Pact of Flesh ↔ Grim Focus and no other was authored.
  • §6 THE BOT: it works ONE target, and it holds its madness against a boss. Ten-stack thresholds reward focus, so a bot that debuffed whatever was convenient would never detonate and a sim would measure a spec nobody plays. _ruin_focus aims everything he casts at the deepest existing mark, and an unbroken boss outranks even that — Shadowrend and Hex go on it to grind Break, while Mass Hysteria, Mind Flay and Bewitch are held until it is Broken, since they are refused outright before that.
  • NEW INSTRUMENT (§0's two numbers): Ruin detonations per battle and the deepest mark observed, each SPLIT TRASH VS BOSS, in both the standalone report and the run report — the run report because only a run ever meets a boss. Printed only when an Occultist stood, because a zero on a party without one reads as a broken instrument rather than a line.
  • §7 THE DEVOUT'S FERVOR: THE BATCH BRIEF'S PREMISE WAS STALE, AND THIS IS A FINDING RATHER THAN A CHANGE. §7 asked to drop Fervor "from 2 Faith per ally per turn to 1, so Consecrated Ground pays 2 a turn rather than 3 with the node learned". The ground already paid 2 a turn with the node learned: Batch AW put a base drip of 1 in the kit and priced Fervor as a +1 increase, so the end state §7 specified was already shipped and the instruction is a no-op. Corrected toward the code (the house rule), and NOT generalised into a guess: the two readings that would have been real changes are fervor_step 1→0, which leaves a node doing nothing at all, and reverting AW §2's base drip, which §7 explicitly says stays. The smoke was re-run anyway and it is comparable with AW's: the FAITH row isolated on Apostle, n=200, measures 80% contribution, 2305 healing a battle, +83.7% of base growth per battle, peak 390 HP on a 175 base against AW's 78% / 1997 / +72.9% / 360. AW's overshoot is UNADDRESSED and is still open for a later batch to price.
  • DOCS. The Ruin glossary entry was rewritten wholesale (no ceiling, never clears, tenth-stack threshold, per-stack amplification, capped lifesteal); Psychosis, Bewitched and Mass Hysteria each gained the boss rule and name Break as the key; §6.3's Occultist entry and §7's three lane tables were rebuilt from the shipped tree. One more stale claim recorded rather than actioned: §8 asks for "the ×3 rank notation removed" from §7's Occultist tables. There is no such notation in master.html and there has not been since Batch AI made every node single-rank — the same correction Batch AW made when its own brief claimed it.
  • MAPPING TABLE — every id survives and re-specs in place. Ruin: oc_emp_hex Empowered Hex 25% → always · oc_deep_hex Deeper Hex 3%→5% per stack · oc_channeling Corrupted Channeling 25%→60% · oc_broken_will Broken Will 5%→25% · oc_grim Grim Focus 25%→80% · oc_entropy Entropy 5→20 Break damage · oc_unravel Unraveling 1→4 Ruin. Madness: oc_spread Spread of Madness 15%→60% and 1→2 Ruin · oc_whispers Whispers 60%→95% · oc_mind_flay Mind Flay unchanged, + an authored fallback · oc_mirror Umbral Mirror 10%→45% · oc_delirium Delirium 1→3 Ruin · oc_cackling Cackling Mirror 3%→15% · oc_torment Lingering Torment 2→5 turns. Leech: oc_soul_leech Soul Leech "15% flat" → 5% per stack · oc_invigoration Invigoration 2%→8% · oc_gluttony Gluttony "3% flat" → 3% per stack · oc_pleasure Pleasure from Pain 0.5%→2.5% · oc_murderous Murderous Intent 10%→35% · oc_pact_flesh Pact of Flesh −10%→−15% (a cost of 5%) · oc_barter Dark Barter 25%→35%. Capstones: oc_avatar_ruin Avatar of Ruin re-spec, "detonations keep their stacks" → "detonates every 5th stack" (its old subject is the passive now) · oc_hysteria Mass Hysteria unchanged, + an authored fallback · oc_soul_glut Soul Glut unchanged in intent, now under the 40% cap. No forced assignment was needed — every node's subject survived, and the one re-spec (Avatar of Ruin) keeps its own subject one step further out.

2026-08-08 — Batch AW: the Devout — investment

Second of the Cleric three. His tree was purpose-designed already (Batch K), so this is not a restructure either — it is a spine (he lends out his own bulk and collects dividends) plus the same 4–5× repricing Holy needed. Every one of his 24 ids survives and re-specs in place; no new ids, none deleted, no save version move (still v7).

  • §0 THE INSTRUMENT, AGAIN. The Devout is the lowest damage-share spec in the game (2–4%) and that number is meaningless. Every row below reads healing landed, damage prevented and the normalised contribution share, at a stated sample size and on matched flags — his numbers are small enough that sampling noise swamps them (Batch AA measured his heal/battle going 10→14 fixed-party and 25→13 rotated, i.e. opposite signs).
  • §1 CONVICTION GAINS A THIRD CLAUSE: THE PRINCIPAL GROWS. He collected a sip of Mana on every Faith release and never got richer — a lender running a charity. Now every Faith release raises the Devout's maximum health by 3% of his BASE maximum for the rest of the battle, and heals him for the same amount, so the dividend arrives as usable health rather than an empty bar. "3% of base" is load-bearing and is not 3% of current: the growth is linear (3% × N releases, never 1.03^N). The loop still compounds through the kit — bigger maximum → bigger shield → more absorbs → more Faith → more releases — which is the design; what must not compound is the clause against itself, because Apostle turns releases into a stream. Nothing else needed building: his entire kit already reads his maximum, so one clause makes the whole kit escalate, and it escalates off other people's survival rather than his own casting.
  • THE LANDMINE, HANDLED: max_hp does not leave the battle. This is exactly why rot was dropped from Batch AQ — the battle-end save sync writes each unit's max_hp straight back onto the party member, so a one-fight change follows the party out of it and the Devout would grow permanently enormous one battle at a time with nothing crashing to announce it. A single field, conviction_hp_gained, accumulates every point granted, and both victory syncs (battle.gd's and RunSim's) subtract it before writing max_hp back, with hp clamped under the restored maximum in the same step.
  • §2 CONSECRATED GROUND IS A FAITH SOURCE IN THE BASE KIT. Faith had one real source and it sat on a 2-turn cooldown — one shield, one target — so a passive whose description promised a party-wide system delivered to one ally at a time. Fervor's effect moved into Consecrated Ground itself: while it holds, every ally gains 1 Faith at the start of their turn, with no node required. Faith is party-wide from turn one, which is what Conviction always claimed, and the ground becomes an engine rather than a mitigation button. The Fervor node is re-specced rather than repriced — same id, same subject, one step deeper (2 per ally per turn).
  • §3 ALL 24 NODES RE-AUTHORED AT ROW PRICING. Magnitudes are 4–5× rather than the Mage trees' 3×, for the same reason Holy's were: these were rank-1 values on a support whose numbers were already the smallest in the game. The lane names stay; two theses are re-aimed. Zeal's thesis was "everything else he casts", which is the fault Holy's Sanctuary had — a lane named after the leftovers. The three lanes are now three shapes of the same act: BULWARK invests deeply in one ally · FAITH invests in what the returns pay · ZEAL invests shallowly in everyone. The full table is in §7 of the Master Document. Highlights: Bastion goes to NO COOLDOWN AT ALL (a shield every turn is a Faith engine, and it is the strongest node in the tree), Unwavering Faith at +20% max health is now the most investment-shaped node in the game (with §1 it raises the base every payout and every growth increment scales from), Purity is a third Faith source (the blessing already doubles Faith gain, so the doubling finally travels with a source), and Judgement is repriced 20% → 40% of the damage dealt.
  • §4 THE STALWART ↔ BASTION PAIR IS DISSOLVED, and nobody had noticed. Batch K authored them as an in-lane fork — a bigger shield, or a more frequent one — and Batch AI's row exclusivity destroyed that fork: they sit in rows 5 and 6, so a player holds both. Removed from CLAUDE.md's prose list (test_runes._exclusives has been a bare pass since AI, so the list survived only there — the same correction AS and AT each made for their own trees). The combination is now legal and it is large: a shield absorbing 50% of his maximum, on no cooldown. Measured, reported, not pre-emptively re-tuned.
  • §5 TWO AUTHORED TALENT FALLBACKS (Batch AU §1), replacing his generics: Sacred Resolve already owned → its split lasts 5 turns instead of 3; Bulwark of Fortitude already owned → its effect lasts 4 turns instead of 3.
  • §6 EVERY DEVOUT COUNTER IS ADDITIVE (the AR/AS/AT/AV form) — each writes its own magnitude in the units its read site sums. Four hold the INCREASE on a base the kit pays without the node and are named _step for it: Stalwart on Divine Shield's 30%, Righteous Fire on the ground's 10%, Blessed are the Faithful on the release's 15%, and Fervor on §2's new base drip of 1. (The batch named two of those four; the other two have the same shape and take the same treatment — reported, not silently generalised.) Sacred Covenant's single counter became two, covenant_heal and covenant_faith, because one counter cannot honestly hold both a percentage and a stack count.
  • RUNE AUDIT — every re-pointed rune still pays exactly what it advertises AND exactly what it paid before this batch. Only the units moved. Rune of the Warded Robes (blessed_barrier_ranks 1→4, warded_ranks 1→10); Rune of the Binding Oath (faithful_ranks 1 → faithful_step 5); Rune of the Burning Censer, the scarred one (righteous_ranks 2 → righteous_step 10, lifewell_ranks 1→20); Rune of the Standing Vow (blessed_barrier_ranks 1→4, devoutness_ranks 1→5, pulse_ranks 1→2). The four new _step names were added to Runes.STAT_INT_KEYS or JSON's float would slide into a typed int var and the hero would fail to spawn (the AA trap). The three Cleric class-wide runes touch no Devout counter, asserted. No Devout rune was left homeless: this batch retires no node.
  • §7 THE BOT. Consecrated Ground is the Faith engine now, and the old policy treated it as a mitigation button it only laid at three or more foes — so a sim measured a spec whose party-wide source was mostly absent. It now goes up whenever it is off cooldown, and comes first. Divine Shield goes on whoever is likeliest to be hit next (the only honest signal on this board is a taunt, which is a certainty rather than a lean; lowest health is the fallback) rather than round-robin. Blessing of Zeal onto a shield-holder is the Batch K fix and stays.
  • §9 ONE HOLY FIX, FOLDED IN: hl_beacon is renamed "Hour of Need". It shipped as SHARED VIGIL in Batch AV, which collides with the Warden's Banner row-6 node of the same name (AV flagged it rather than shipping it silently). His triggers on him standing strong and keeps the name that fits it; hers triggers on a hero being near death. A label only — holy_vigil_pct and every read site are exactly as they were.
  • MAPPING TABLE — every id survives and re-specs in place. Bulwark: dv_barrier Blessed Barrier 4%→20% · dv_aegis Radient Aegis 15%→60% · dv_afterglow Afterglow 5%→20% · dv_warded Warded Robes 10%→25% · dv_stalwart Stalwart 35%→50% · dv_bastion Bastion −1 cooldown → no cooldown · dv_unyielding Unyielding Aegis 30%→90%. Faith: dv_communion Communion 20→40 · dv_unwavering Unwavering Faith 5%→20% · dv_devoutness Devoutness 5%→20% · dv_faithful Blessed are the Faithful 20%→35% · dv_covenant Sacred Covenant 5%+1 Faith → 25%+2 Faith · dv_fervor Fervor "the ground drips 1" → re-spec, "the drip becomes 2" (its old subject is base kit now) · dv_oath Binding Oath keeps 1→3. Zeal: dv_waters Cleansing Waters 15%→50% · dv_righteous Righteous Fire 15%→35% · dv_resolve Sacred Resolve unchanged, + an authored fallback · dv_pulse Healing Pulse 2%→8% · dv_crusade Crusader's Tempo 1→3 turns · dv_purity Purity 10%→35% · dv_lifewell Lifewell 20%→80%. Capstones: dv_bulwark unchanged, + an authored fallback · dv_apostle unchanged · dv_judgement 20%→40%. No forced assignment was needed — every node's subject survived, and the one re-spec (Fervor) keeps its own subject one step deeper.
  • TWO CLAIMS IN THE BATCH BRIEF WERE STALE and are recorded rather than quietly actioned. (a) §1 says Tenacity's growth at the victory sync is permanent and that the two fields "do opposite things at the same site". It is not permanent — tenacity_hp_gained has been subtracted at that sync since Batch W. The instruction not to merge them stands for a better reason: tenacity_hp_gained has a second consumer, Unkillable's mend, which reads "the pool he brought into the battle" and must mean Tenacity's growth alone. (b) §5 calls Bulwark of Fortitude "the first capstone in the game that grants an ability". Nine capstones grant one and eight predate this batch; what is true is the reason the brief gives — Holy's three granted none, so it is the first Cleric capstone to owe a fallback. Also: §8 asked for the "×3 rank notation" to be removed from the Master Document's Devout lanes; there is none anywhere in the file. The Devout tables were genuinely stale in a different way — they still carried Batch AI's magnitudes and dead decimals — and have been regenerated from the live tree.

2026-08-08 — Batch AV: Holy — reversal

First of the Cleric three. Her tree was purpose-designed already, so this is not a restructure — it is a spine (nothing is final, no loss permanent) plus the most overdue repricing in the game. Every one of her 24 ids survives and re-specs in place; no new ids, none deleted, no save version move (still v7).

  • §0 THE INSTRUMENT, AND IT IS NOT DAMAGE SHARE. Batch W already measured her at 8% damage share and 33% contribution off 431 healing a battle. Every measurement in this batch reads healing landed, damage prevented and the normalised contribution share; a moving damage share is noise. This is a standing rule for the two Cleric batches still to come.
  • §1 RESURRECTION JOINS THE OPENING KIT, unchanged (1 Mercy, 4.0, 3cd, 20% health and resource; Empower +1 Mercy returns them at full with 5 turns of Renewal; Perfect 25%). The thing that most means "nothing is final" was a row-5 node and an Epic rune's whole payload, so her identity was a pick most builds skipped. Consequences handled rather than discovered: it left SPEC_POOLS["holy"] (a boss cannot offer what she starts with), Mercy row 5 is now Grace, and her kit is FOUR abilities where every other spec has three. That parity break is deliberate and is stated in the docs: she attacks at 50, so her abilities are not part of her contribution, they are all of it. Exactly one def of Resurrection exists — the kit calls Classes.pending_talent_ability rather than holding a copy (the AK resolver rule), because the Rune of the Last Rites still grants it by name.
  • §2 SANCTUARY IS RENAMED VIGIL. "Radiance is heal bigger, Sanctuary is stop people dying" was one axis with two names. VIGIL takes reversal — the fallen and the nearly-fallen — so she has three real questions instead of two: how much you heal, how you pay for it, and what you can take back.
  • §3 THE REPRICING. Her magnitudes were the worst-priced of the twelve: a 5% dispel chance, 1% of maximum Mana, 1% of an ally's health, a threshold moved 50 → 53. Those were rank-1 values in a three-rank tree, and as whole ROWS costing two other options they were not decisions. Where the Mage trees needed 3×, hers needed 4–5×. Mercy itself is untouched by designer decision — the generator, the +5% per stack, the spend costs, the Empower surcharge and the perfect-forgo all stand exactly as they were. The tree moves around it.
    • Guardian Angel 53% → 65% is the load-bearing reprice. Mercy is untouched, so this node is the only lever on her economy — and 50→53 was never going to be one.
    • Grace closes a real dead end: at maximum stacks every further crossing paid her nothing, the one place her reactive economy wasted what it earned.
    • Intercession is the reversal button — death refused rather than healed away. It costs Mercy on trigger, not on cast, so a Cleric holding nothing gets nothing.
    • Serenity is the row-7 statement: reversal stops being a once-a-fight miracle. It deliberately does not touch the health the ally returns at — that is Empower's job, and stepping on it would make Empower pointless. It is written as an ability payload for exactly that reason: there is no field in it that could reach the return health.
  • §8 THE FULL ID MAPPING. Every design is mapped onto an existing id by closest surviving intent. Three assignments are forced and are reported, not hidden.
    idBatch AI (was)Batch AV (now)
