    Holy13%9%6%8%43136333%
    Devout4%3%4%5%161364015%
    Rotated sweep difficulty curve: 100 / 98 / 92 / 82% wins at budgets 3/6/9/12 (deaths/battle 0.00/0.33/0.82/1.34) — a rotating field is markedly harder than the fixed party's 100/100/100/96, which is itself a finding: the standard test party is one of the stronger compositions, not a neutral one.
  • The Sharpshooter's 38% is retired. Measured on the current build he is 32-36% — mid-pack among damage specs, and NOT the game's worst outlier. Tuning him against the stale figure would have been exactly the mistake Batch R was written to stop.
  • Three specs were invisible, not weak. The Devout's 3-5% damage was an artefact of asking a Warder how much damage he does: he prevents 136/battle at budget 12 and reads 15% contribution. The Holy is the clearest case — 6-13% damage share, but 431 healing/battle and a 33% contribution share, the highest of any spec at budget 12. On the old instrument he looked like the weakest spec in the game; he is one of the strongest. The Occultist's healing (102/battle) is likewise a side effect of debuffing, and now visible.
  • Cryomancer — the diagnosis did not survive measurement. Ice Lance's Batch O clause was walked back +10% → +5% of Attack per Chilled stack (Crystal Edge still adds +5%/rank on top; Permafrost's permanence and Razor Ice's three shards untouched, as instructed — they fixed a real structural fault). It barely moved the number. Fixed-party control, 200/budget: 49/42/39/39% at budgets 3/6/9/12 versus Batch V's 48→40 — inside noise, despite Ice Lance being cast a real 1.1x/battle. Per the brief the second lever was then measured ALONE: freeze remainder 1 stack → 0 gave 47/42/40/38% — also noise, so it was reverted and the ember stays (Batch O's declared knob is spent, and now known not to be the lever). Under rotation he reads 38/39/40/37%. Both levers reported as required; only the Ice Lance halving is kept, on the grounds the brief gave for it (+10% on a permanent-stack passive plus an auto-crit did more than "a reason to exist outside the Frozen window"), not on grounds it solved anything.
  • Why his 40-48% was partly an instrument artefact: the fixed test party contains the Devout, the lowest-damage spec in the game (3-5%). With one member contributing ~4%, the other three split ~96% — every share in that party is inflated by construction. Measured against a rotating field the Cryomancer is 37-40%, and his own classmates read 34-47%: he is typical for a mage, not an outlier among mages. The mage class as a whole out-damages the rest of the roster, which is a class-balance question and not a Cryomancer question.
  • The real outlier is the Pyromancer — 42/46/46/47%, higher than the Cryomancer at every budget, and it CLIMBS with field size, which by this project's own diagnostic is the AoE signature (a share that falls with field size is baseline overtuning; one that climbs is the AoE). Per the brief's rule — "if the rotation turns up something worse than the Cryomancer, that's the next conversation, not this one" — he is deliberately untouched. No changes were made to the Sharpshooter, the Devout, or any of the eight newly-measured specs.
  • Harness bug found and fixed (pre-existing, exposed by rotation): _log appended to the battle history RichTextLabel even in headless sims, where nothing can read it. Every line allocates TextServer RIDs; a long verbose battle exhausts the pool, after which each _log call emits an 8-line engine backtrace. A 13-minute sweep ran 3+ hours and wrote a 500 MB log while still making forward progress — a hang that is really I/O thrash. _log now skips the panel when sim; DOD_SIM_DEBUG=1 still prints. It surfaced only under rotation because the never-simulated specs (Holy, Occultist, Survivalist) log several times more per battle. Documented in CLAUDE.md as a third sim-hang failure mode, distinguished from the other two by %CPU (30-50%, not 1-2%) and log size.
  • Rotated run harness, 50 runs (route=default, economy on): completions 12%, wipe median z1 t9.0, ratio@z1t8 0.97 — versus Batch V's fixed-party 14% / z2 t6. A rotating roster is harder than the standard party in the run harness too, the same direction the sweep showed. Runs sampled per spec 16-17 each; battle counts per spec vary (n=129 Swordmaster to n=273 Warden) because durable parties survive deeper and therefore fight more battles — that spread is itself a signal. The contribution metric pays off hardest here: the Warden posts 206 damage prevented per battle and 33% contribution — the highest of any spec in the run harness — off 27% damage share, and the Holy reads 28% contribution off 5% damage (337 healing/battle). On damage share alone, the game's two most valuable specs looked unremarkable and near-worthless respectively.
  • WARDEN SOFTLOCK FOUND (real play, not a sim artefact) — diagnosis corrected on 08-02. The symptom was Endurance reporting +97,521% armor, and that is what this entry first blamed. It was wrong: effective_armor() has always ended in minf(a, 0.85), so the runaway number never reached the damage path — the chip was lying, not driving. The real loop is Tenacity feeding Unkillable: Tenacity adds +5 max HP per Heavy Plating block with no bound (battle-long), and Unkillable mends 2%/rank of max HP per block — so every block enlarges the pool that the next block heals from. Measured: max HP ~127,000, mends of 7,607 per block. Sitting at the 0.85 armor clamp he takes 15% of incoming damage and out-heals it indefinitely, so the battle can never end. In real play that is a softlock, not just a slow sim. It had never surfaced because the fixed test party contains no Warden and the sweep grants no talents; only the run harness buys them, so rotation put a talent-invested Warden into a long fight for the first time.
  • Endurance capped at +75% armor (08-02, at the designer's direction). This stops the chip and combat log advertising a bonus the damage path can never grant, and bounds the term for any future armor source that reads it before the clamp. It does not close the softlock, and was not expected to: with base armor 0.32 the Warden reaches the 0.85 clamp after roughly 18 unhealed turns with or without the cap.
  • SOFTLOCK FIXED (08-02, designer chose the Unkillable side): Unkillable now mends 2%/rank of base max HP — max_hp - tenacity_hp_gained, the same "true max" idiom the end-of-battle save sync already uses — instead of the live pool. Tenacity is untouched and still grants +5 max HP per Heavy Plating block; the two simply no longer feed each other off their shared trigger. Verified with a talent-invested Warden (DOD_SIM_TALENTS="wd_unkillable:3,wd_tenacity:1"): the mend is a flat 12 across 56 blocks (6% of base 200) where it previously climbed to 7,607. The rule this teaches, worth applying to future kits: a percentage effect must never read off a value that its own trigger inflates.
  • ...but it did NOT close the endless battle, and the cause is now established by evidence. Re-running the rotated 50-run after the fix left the stalemate count unchanged (5 → 6 of ~700 battles), which retired the second diagnosis as well. The debug log of an actual stalled battle shows what is really happening: the party is down to a single surviving Warden — 169 of 169 hero actions in the final window are his — facing a five-strong warband holding a Shaman, a Totemist and a Shieldmaster, with 92 heal/shield events in that same window. He cannot die (0.85 armor clamp, Block, Unkillable) and his ~17-damage single-target strike cannot out-pace five enemies' healing. Neither side can finish. This is structural, not a talent bug: any last-hero-standing durable-but-low-damage survivor against a healing warband reproduces it, and rotation exposed it because rotated parties form low-damage compositions the fixed test party never did. Both earlier fixes are kept on their own merits — an absurd 7,607 mend and a lying armor chip were each worth removing — but neither was the cause, and this entry previously said otherwise. Open design question for real play: a human who reaches this state is stuck in an unwinnable, unloseable fight; the game currently has no resolution mechanism. Options are a turn limit, escalating pressure on both sides, or a flee/forfeit action — a design call left with the designer. Sims are covered by the counted stalemate guard.
  • Stalemate guard (SIMS ONLY, real play untouched): a battle exceeding 600 unit turns (~60 rounds; a long real battle is 4-12) is force-ended. Scored as a loss, never a win — a free victory would inflate completions — and no HP is touched, so death stats stay honest. Every trip is counted and printed in all three reports, so the cap can never pass silently. Measured: 5 stalemates across the 50-run invocation (~700 battles). Validated both directions: forced to 25 turns it fires on 8/8 battles and reports them; at 600 a normal 40-battle sim is 40/40 with zero.
  • Run harness now prints per-run progress ("run 12/50 — wiped z2 t7 (3 completed so far)"), mirroring the sweep's per-stage marker. The run report only printed at the very end, so a long invocation was indistinguishable from a hung one for its entire life — which cost this batch a 48-minute run that turned out to be healthy and was then lost to session teardown with nothing to show for it.
  • Known reporting limitation (stated, not silent): the "Hero ability uses/battle" line divides by ALL battles, not battles-that-spec-was-present, so under rotation it reads ~3x low. The contribution table divides by n correctly.

2026-08-01 — Batch V: enemies that ask questions

  • Four new enemy kinds, each posing a question a specific hero spec answers — the roster's sixteen kinds shared three special behaviours between them (enemy_shield, healing_wave, wild_growth), so enemies never engaged the heroes' status engines. No existing enemy's stats changed (Batch T just tuned the curve against that roster). The new kinds: Orc Ritual Chanter (power 2, mender, all rosters — fast, fragile, physically soft), Ash Hurler (power 3, brute·sniper, rosters 2–3 — slow ranged siege), Orc Bloodcaller (power 2, hexer, all rosters), and Grave Totem (power 2, mender·hexer, rosters 1–3 forest slots — Con 60 but physical resist 35%, fire/holy vulnerable 50%).
  • Four new special behaviours (the batch's working title said three; the data design needed four). cleanse_allies — Cleansing Rite strips the longest-remaining debuff from each living ally, with guardrails: Chilled thaws ONE stack (a single cast must not erase four turns of Cryomancer work), stripping Ruin also clears its primer, sticky poisons (Slow Acting/Epidemic) refuse it, and Broken/Bleed are meter states it never touches. windup — Siege Stone applies a visible Charging chip on cast and resolves the following turn as a party-wide attack through the normal pipeline; being Broken, Frozen, or Stunned while charging cancels it entirely, loudly logged — the first telegraphed enemy mechanic. blood_tribute — +25% damage and Break damage per enemy fallen this battle, resolved as a scaled attack copy so armor/resists/crits apply. totem_pulse — Dark Vigil heals every living ally 6% of its own max HP. All four ride the condition-gated support AI (the Chanter casts when any ally carries a strippable debuff, the Totem when two or more allies are wounded), and a psychotic Chanter or Totem turns its magic on the heroes, like every other enemy support.
  • Mender cap: the theme generator could roll three and four healer warbands (2,007 distinct ones existed — Ritual and Lightning Storm pools go to 3–4); with a cleanse joining the roster, that is a wall, not a question. _combo_ok now rejects any warband with more than two mender-TAGGED kinds, counted by role tag no matter which pool claimed them (a Grave Totem drawn through a hexer pool still heals). Theme-safety audit (recreated test_zone_rosters.gd): satisfiability matrices before/after show no theme lost a single budget in any roster — the new kinds only added rolls (Swarm r2 now reaches 10–12, Poison Volley r2 reaches 12) — and the Rot Behemoth still appears in roster-2 brute slots alongside the Hurler. Distinct warbands: 11,344 → 23,561.
  • Verified headless (forced-lineup autoplay battles): a Cleansing Rite stripped Pyromancer Burns; a Siege Stone charge resolved the following turn party-wide; Blood Tribute logged +25% after one death; Dark Vigil pulsed 53 HP across a five-body warband; and the load-bearing one — a Swordmaster Break cancelled a mid-charge Siege Stone ("slips from its grasp — CANCELLED"), with a Pommel-Strike stun cancelling another. Zero script errors; map-screen resist preview reads the new kinds from the same JSON as everything else.
  • Doc repair while in §9: the tier-scaling paragraph still described the pre-Batch-36 game-long ladder at the pre-Batch-T rates (+4%/+5%); corrected to zone-local +2%/+2.5% atop the ×1.0/1.5/2.2 slot multipliers.
  • Re-measure (report only, nothing tuned — Batch T owns the numbers): sweep 200, roster 1: 100 / 100 / 98.5 / 97.5% wins at budgets 3/6/9/12 vs Batch R's 100/100/98/93.5 — mid budgets flat, top band EASIER (+4pts; deaths/battle at 12 fell ~1.3–1.5 → 1.00), inverting the batch's own prediction. Two causes: the Hurler is rosters 2–3 only, so the roster-1 sweep never sees the one kind meant to spike the top; and the mender cap deleted the 3–4-healer warbands that lived exactly in the top budgets. Run 50 (route=default, economy on, vs Batch U's matching row): completions 6% → 14%, wipe median z1 t9-10 → z2 t6 — the wipe median moved MORE than one tier, so per this batch's own rule that is a tuning conversation to have separately, not a silent adjustment. The z1 curve itself is unchanged (t8 ratio 0.99 in band, t9-10 at 0.86/0.84, boss 0.72 vs T's 0.70; 33/50 runs reach the z1 boss, 94% win) — the easing is concentrated where heal-wall warbands used to grind runs down, which points at the cap, not the kinds. Caveats stated: n=50 completion counts are noisy (7 vs 3 runs); the corroborating signals are the median shift and the sweep's top-band death drop. z2/z3 tails sit where U left them (z2 t10 0.65, z3 t9-10 0.50/0.51, z3 boss 0.41). Damage share: Cryomancer 40–48% falling with field size, Devout 3–5% — the outlier pass's targets are untouched by this batch.

2026-08-01 — Batch U: an honest floor

  • Harness batch, zero tuning — Batch T owns the numbers; this batch establishes what they mean. Batch S's floor was measured by a bot that walked past every rest node into fights at 45% health, bought nothing, and never drank a potion it carried — so an unknown share of the tiers-4–7 wipe cluster was routing artefact, not difficulty fact. The harness now plays three route policies (DOD_SIM_ROUTE): greedy keeps the old ladder byte for byte (fight > elite always; rest only when no combat is offered and someone is under 60%), default rests when average party HP falls below 65% and a rest is reachable, cautious rests below 80%. Shops are played (heal-first: any hero under 50% buys a Health Potion each; then the priciest affordable offer not already carried — runes included, only onto a member with a free slot, equipped at purchase; no buy dips below the 40g reserve; DOD_SIM_SHOPS=off restores the buy-nothing floor). In battle a hero opening a turn below 35% HP drinks a carried Health Potion before acting (DOD_SIM_ITEMS=off restores the never-drinks floor; sweep/standalone battles always stay dry). The report adds the run economy — gold earned/spent/unspent, items used and carried unused, rests taken versus offered — and a machine-comparable matrix row per invocation.
  • Shop schema, reported before the policy was set: five consumables at flat prices (Health/Mana Potion 30g, Defense 40g, Bomb 45g, Revive 80g), unlimited stock, no tier scaling, only the relic discount moves prices; plus one rune offer per party member per visit (Common 50g / Rare 100g / Epic 160g — permanent run-scoped stat boosts, one copy each). A run STARTS carrying all five consumables, so "the priciest item not already carried" buys almost nothing on its own — runes are the shop's only real gold sink, which is why the purchase rule covers them.
  • The four-row matrix (50 runs each; the control row reproduces the Batch T floor before anything else is read — 2/50 completions, 22 z1-boss entries at 82%, Cryomancer 40%, boss ratio 0.69: the baseline is controlled): 
    Route policy          Completions   Wipe median      Ratio @ z1 t8
greedy (floor ctrl*)      4%          z1 t10             0.90
greedy                    0%          z1 t9              0.98
default                   6%          z1 boss            0.89
cautious                  8%          z1 boss            0.97
* shops+items OFF — the Batch S/T floor. Ratio@t8 wobble (0.89–0.98)
  is sampling noise: ~20 fights land on that tier per row.
  • Verdict — routing was NOT the story. The spread between greedy and cautious is 0–4% → 8% completions: real, roughly a doubling of the old floor, and far too small to reframe the difficulty question. Even resting at 80%, buying runes and drinking potions, 46/50 runs still wipe. The z1 t4–7 cluster thins (15 → 10 wipes) and the survivors die later (wipe median moves t9–10 → the z1 boss; z1-boss wipes 4 → 7), but the curve's shape survives every policy. Batch T's calibration was NOT made against a misleadingly depressed floor — its stage-3 numbers stand, and the deferred levers stay what they were: the z1 tail (t9–boss 0.86/0.82/0.68), the z2–z3 collapse (0.66 by z2 t7, 0.37–0.43 at the z3 tail — no 40 HP potion closes a 2× effective-power gap), and the outlier pass (Cryomancer 38–40% and Devout 4% at the honest floor too — the overtune is policy-independent, ready to measure).
  • Rests offered versus taken — the diagnostic line: taken went 1.6/run (floor control) → 2.6/run (cautious) against 3.3–3.9 offered. Five rest nodes exist per zone map, but one-step reachability means the walk is only ever OFFERED about four, and the most cautious policy uses two and a half. The recovery the old harness left on the table was ~one extra rest per run — party HP entering z1 t7 rose 55% → 62% — not the five-rests-a-zone the node deck implies. Route planning toward rests (map lookahead) is the one lever this batch deliberately did not model.
  • The gold pile was real: the floor accrued 422g unspent per run. The policy converts about half (spent 222–225/run: ~2.2 runes, ~1.5 heal potions, the rest reserve-blocked or unaffordable at death) and the bot drinks 3.8–4.3 potions per run — worth roughly +2–4 completion points and one wipe-band of depth, no more. The exclusions were costing measurement honesty, not hiding a solved game.
  • Deeper reads now on record: the honest floor puts 60% of cautious runs at the z1 boss (77% win there, ≈46% of runs clear zone 1 vs 28% under Batch T's floor) — zone 1 is roughly right; runs die in zones 2–3 where the measured ratio collapses regardless of policy. Talent income at the honest floor: 11.4–12.2 pts/hero/run (economy assumes ~35 — the gap is depth-of-run, not award rates).
  • Verification: the three correctness gates re-run green (recovered from the Batch T scratchpad); headless parse clean; the greedy control reproduced Batch T within noise BEFORE the policy rows were read, per the batch's stop-rule. Simulated runs still never touch the save/relics/profile — the shop policy spends Run.gold in memory only.

2026-08-01 — Batch T: close the gap

  • The first tuning batch of the difficulty-convergence arc, staged and measured: one change, then ./sim.sh --run 50, then decide whether the next stage was needed. Baseline (Batch S): 0/50 completions, 40 of 50 wipes in zone 1 tiers 4–7, the measured power ratio crossing under 1.0 exactly where the budget ladder stepped 3–6 → 6–9. Target: ratio 1.0–1.3 at every zone-1 tier; the bot floor reaching the zone-1 boss in most runs. No spec's damage numbers were touched (the Cryomancer/Sharpshooter/Devout outlier pass is deliberately deferred until it can be measured over a stable curve — their shares held at 39/27-ish/4% through all three stages, so the overtune is tier-independent and will still be there to measure).
  • Stage 1 — the awakening HP sync (correctness, not tuning): spec stat blocks raise max HP at the awakening, but current HP stayed at the class base until the first victory sync — the party entered its first specced fight at ~75% health. New Run.sync_spec_hp raises current HP by the same amount as the max-HP raise (never to full: damage already taken is preserved), called from BOTH awakening paths — the spec-choice screen and the run sim's direct assignment. Measured alone: still 0/50 completions and the ratio table barely moved (it reads max stats, as predicted) — but tier-4 win rate jumped 44% → 79%, tier-8 20% → 56%, and a run crossed into zone 2 for the first time. Ratios z1: t1 1.21 / t3 1.15 / t4 0.77 / t6 0.80 / t8 0.57 / t10 0.72.
  • Stage 2 — the budget ladder became a ramp: three bands over eleven tiers meant two discontinuities with the wipes sitting on the first; now a tier's fight budget rolls 3 + floor((tier−1)/2) to that +2 (t1 3–5 ... t10 7–9). Two guards preserved authored content: elite budgets floor at 6 (the cheapest elite theme, Rage Company, costs 6 in every roster — below that an elite node silently degraded to the plain-mob fallback while still paying elite rewards) and the boss node keeps its 10–12 band (the Escort composition is authored, so the tier-10 → boss step is now the zone's sharpest — accepted, see stage 3's table). Measured: the tier-4 cliff is gone (ratio 0.77 → 1.16, win 44% → 97%); 9 runs reached the z1 boss, 5 entered zone 2, 1 reached zone 3; talent income rose 3.8 → 5.7 points/hero/run as the attrition loop started unwinding. The wall moved to t7–t10 (0.73–0.78). Ratios z1: t1 1.42 / t2 1.28 / t3 1.13 / t4 1.16 / t5 0.92 / t6 0.94 / t7 0.78 / t8 0.73 / t9 0.75 / t10 0.75 / boss 0.57.
  • Stage 3 — enemy tier scaling halved (its condition — tiers 7–11 still below 1.0 — was met): +4% Attack / +5% HP per tier → +2% / +2.5%. The zone slot multiplier (×1.0 / ×1.5 / ×2.2) is untouched, so between-zone escalation still carries the run's progression arc; what fell is the second multiplicative source compounding on the budget ramp within a zone. Measured: the first completed runs in the game's history — 2/50 full three-zone clears; 20/50 reached the z1 boss (70% win there), 14 cleared zone 1. Talent income 9.3 points/hero/run (2.4× baseline). Ratios z1: t1 1.37 / t2 1.42 / t3 1.28 / t4 1.20 / t5 1.01 / t6 1.03 / t7 0.95 / t8 0.95 / t9 0.87 / t10 0.78 / boss 0.70.
  • Stage 4 — talent income: SKIPPED, and the skip is the finding. Its trigger was "only if stages 1–3 leave the mid-band short," and the mid-band (t4–7) closed at 0.95–1.20 with 78–100% win rates. What remains short is the zone-1 tail (t9 0.87, t10 0.78) and the deliberately-kept boss step (0.70) — one extra point per zone moves party power ~1–2% and cannot close that; spending an economy change there would buy nothing measurable. The 3×11-tier economy invariant stands untouched.
  • Where the target landed: tiers 3–8 sit in the 1.0–1.3 band; t1–t2 overshoot it (1.37/1.42 — the ramp's 3–5 floor makes the opening gentler than the band's letter; designer's call whether gentle onboarding outranks the metric); t9–t10 and the boss remain below. The difficulty line itself — 40% of floor runs reaching the z1 boss, 28% clearing zone 1, 4% full clears, remembering the floor bot never rests while a fight is reachable, never buys, never drinks — is the designer's taste question, flagged not assumed. If the tail wants closing later, the levers are the ramp's slope or the boss band, not spec numbers.
  • Sweep comparability note: the end-of-batch ./sim.sh --sweep 200 still probes fixed budgets 3/6/9/12 unscaled — those rows remain directly comparable to the Batch R and Batch S baselines even though the ladder that FEEDS budgets to real runs changed. The sweep measures kit-vs-budget; the run harness measures the ladder. Do not read one as contradicting the other. The end-of-batch sweep proved the point by replicating Batch S within noise: win% 100/100/100/99 at budgets 3/6/9/12, Cryomancer share 49/43/40/38% — the standalone rows didn't feel Batch T at all, which is exactly what "the run ladder changed, the kits didn't" should look like.
  • Verification: the three correctness gates (hero win scaling, talent spend conservation, enemy tier×slot scaling) were recreated as test_run_harness.gd and re-run green after every stage; a budget-ramp probe pinned the observed rolls per tier (t1 3–5 ... t10 7–9, boss 10–12); a theme satisfiability probe mapped every theme × budget 3–12 × roster before the ramp shipped (that map is what surfaced the elite floor and confirmed plain fights satisfiable at every budget ≥3 in all rosters).

2026-08-01 — Batch S: measure a real run

  • No tuning ships in this batch — like Batch R, it produces numbers. Two new instruments: the sweep now reports damage share and field size per budget, and ./sim.sh --run N plays N complete runs with progression on both sides and reports where they end.
  • Sweep, part 1 — damage share and field size per budget: every budget row now carries average enemy count, average enemies still alive entering round 3 (the field-size proxy AoE actually feeds on), and each hero's damage share at that budget — the previous report pooled share across the whole sweep, so an AoE spec's share was one number smeared across every field size. Because DOD_SIM_THEME narrows the sweep to one theme, a themed sweep now reads out per-theme damage share for free — the follow-up tool for whichever answer the field-size breakdown gives. The full report: 
    ===== DAWN OF DECAY — DIFFICULTY SWEEP =====
Zone roster: 1   Themes: all fight themes   Specs: berserker,cryomancer,inquisitor,beastmaster
budget   battles   wins    win%   rounds   deaths/battle   foes   alive@r3
   3       200      200    100%      4.5        0.01      2.6      0.0
   6       200      200    100%      7.0        0.27      4.4      0.0
   9       200      196     98%      9.2        0.61      5.5      0.1
  12       200      187     94%     10.8        1.18      5.9      0.3
Damage share per budget:
   3   Beastmaster 22% | Berserker 25% | Cryomancer 49% | Devout 4%
   6   Beastmaster 29% | Berserker 24% | Cryomancer 44% | Devout 3%
   9   Beastmaster 31% | Berserker 26% | Cryomancer 40% | Devout 4%
  12   Beastmaster 31% | Berserker 26% | Cryomancer 38% | Devout 4%
=============================================
  • The Cryomancer verdict the breakdown gives: his damage share is 49% at budget 3, where the average field is 2.6 bodies, and it FALLS as fields widen — 44% / 40% / 38% at budgets 6 / 9 / 12 (4.4 / 5.5 / 5.9 foes). That is the opposite of the AoE-outlier signature the batch set out to test for, and the new alive-at-round-3 column explains why AoE never gets to feed: fields are functionally empty entering round 3 at every budget (0.0-0.3 enemies standing) — wide fields do not stay wide. The Cryomancer is overtuned at the BASELINE — his small-field/single-target output carries the overage, and more bodies dilute him toward the pack. The fix, when a tuning batch takes it up, is his numbers, not his AoE. (For a second opinion the themed sweep now exists: DOD_SIM_THEME="Swarm" ./sim.sh --sweep reads out his share against the widest fields the game rolls.)
  • Run harness, part 2 — ./sim.sh --run N (DOD_SIM_RUN=N, default 50) plays complete runs: a real Run.new_run, the real 30-node deck (17 fight / 5 rest / 5 shop / 3 event), a tier-by-tier walk, real warbands at real budgets WITH tier stat scaling and the zone slot multiplier, state carried between battles exactly as a real run carries it (HP, mana, items; cooldowns reset per fight; node scaling accumulating; talent points banked and spent), all three zone slots or death. The bot is dumb on purpose — a clever bot makes numbers unattributable — and every policy prints in the report header: route DOD_SIM_ROUTE (default = fight > elite; rest only when no combat is offered and someone is under 60% HP), talent builds DOD_SIM_BUILDS (default = each tree's first lane, cheapest node first, capstone the moment its gate opens, spillover to the next lane when bought out), boss trophies DOD_SIM_TROPHIES (default = first in the spec's pool, resolved instantly), relics DOD_SIM_RELICS (armed at the draft), elite rune spoils auto-equip while a slot is free, events take the first valid choice.
  • What the run harness INCLUDES (the paragraph that matters): hero talents (earned at the real 1/2/3 economy AND spent), boss trophies, elite runes (equipped), relics if armed, event outcomes including mid-run relic grants, node scaling (+2% per win), enemy tier scaling (+4% Atk / +5% HP per tier), the zone slot multiplier (x1.0/x1.5/x2.2), HP/mana carry between fights, the 20%-HP return of the fallen after victories, rest heals, elite/boss gold and loot, all real map decks and warband pre-rolls. What it EXCLUDES (v1): shop purchases (gold accrues unspent — the report is a FLOOR on real play, not an estimate), item use in battle (the bot never drinks a potion it carries), and any routing intelligence (combat always outranks a rest node, so the bot fights at 45% HP where a human would rest). Deaths/fight counts companion deaths too, same as the sweep's metric. Simulated runs live entirely in memory: Run.sim_run makes save_run/clear_save no-ops, and RunSim never touches Relics.unlock_random or the Profile chronicle.
  • The report (50 runs, default policies, gated kits): 
    ===== DAWN OF DECAY — RUN REPORT =====

=============================================
  • The finding — progression does NOT close the gap at the floor: zero of 50 runs completed; none even reached the zone-1 boss (one made tier 10 and died there). Wipes cluster at tiers 4-7 (40 of 50) — exactly where the budget ladder steps from 3-6 to 6-9 and the measured power ratio crosses under 1.0 (1.25 at tier 1, 0.80 at tiers 4-6, 0.55 by tier 8). The deeper reading: early attrition THROTTLES progression — runs that die at tier 5 have earned ~3 talent points per hero, so the counterweight that is supposed to answer the x3+ enemy curve (roughly 35 points, trophies, relics) never gets fielded. This is a floor, not the player experience — the bot never rests while a fight is reachable, never buys, never drinks — but the shape is real: the game's difficulty curve front-loads its wall into the first zone's midsection, and the progression engine can't spin up fast enough to meet it. The three correctness gates passed before any of this was collected (scripted test: hero Attack x1.64 after 32 wins; 40 granted points = 40 paid + 0 banked with a capstone taken; raider Attack 104/132/198/290 across z1t1/z1t8/z2t8/z3t8 — tier ladder and slot multiplier both live).
  • Also: plain ./sim.sh N regression-checked (20/20, report unchanged); the party enters its first fight at ~75% HP by design inheritance — spec stat blocks raise max HP at awakening but current HP stays at the class base until the first victory sync, in real runs and simulated ones alike (recorded here, not changed).

2026-07-31 — Batch R: fix the instrument

  • The hypothesis, verified and corrected: the batch went in blaming the DOD_SIM_BUDGET default of 6. Reality is one step worse: that default only applies when DOD_SIM_THEME forces a theme, and the plain sim never touches the budget system at all — every ./sim.sh battle fought the same hardcoded raider/chief/archer/archer lineup (power 7, a shape from before the theme system existed) at completely unscaled stats, with a chief that regular fight nodes can't even roll (chiefs are elite-node only). Two hundred battles measured one easy fight two hundred times; nothing varied but dice. The hypothesis's conclusion — the 100% win rate was correct and meaningless — stands unchanged.
  • What the harness actually simulates (the answers every past sim number rests on): (1) every battle spawns a FRESH party at base power — the scene reloads between battles and only the stats dictionary survives. (2) Node scaling never applies: the +2% Attack/HP per win is gated on an active run and sims never have one — the sim models turn-one-of-the-game forever. (3) Talent points are never earned or spent; heroes fight with base kits plus spec abilities only. (4) Relics and boss trophies are absent (the DOD_SIM_RELICS / DOD_SIM_ABILITIES hooks exist but sim.sh never arms them). (5) Between battles of the old sim NOTHING varied but combat dice: initiative jitter, miss/parry/crit rolls, and the bot's fixed skill-check profile (20% perfect / 65% good / 15% fail). Items refresh to standalone defaults every battle. In short: every damage share, round count, and win rate ever collected described base-kit heroes fighting one unscaled early-game lineup.
  • The new instrument: ./sim.sh --sweep N (DOD_SIM_SWEEP=1) runs N battles at EACH budget 3/6/9/12 and prints win rate, rounds, and hero deaths per budget. Every battle rolls a FRESH warband from the fight-node themes at that exact budget (new Run.compose_budget — the real map's draw minus the budget roll). DOD_SIM_ZONE picks the roster, DOD_SIM_THEME narrows the roll to one theme, and plain ./sim.sh N is unchanged. Sweep enemies carry NO tier stat scaling and sweep heroes carry base kits — the sweep isolates the composition axis on purpose. It samples fight themes only: real tier-6+ fights are 25% elite-themed and the boss brings an escort, so top-band rows understate the real top of the ladder.
  • The measured curve (200 battles per budget per roster, gated kits, berserker/cryomancer/inquisitor/beastmaster; cells are win% — deaths/battle — avg rounds):
    BudgetRoster 1 (Forest)Roster 2 (Scarlands) Roster 3 (Forest finale)
    3100% — 0.01 — 4.6100% — 0.01 — 4.8 100% — 0.01 — 4.7
    6100% — 0.24 — 6.8100% — 0.15 — 6.8 100% — 0.17 — 6.9
    998.0% — 0.65 — 9.397.5% — 0.74 — 8.8 99.5% — 0.41 — 9.5
    1293.5% — 1.25 — 10.788.5% — 1.51 — 10.1 98.5% — 0.74 — 11.5
    The curve is real: saturation through budget 6, first losses at 9, and a meaningful spread at 12. Attrition moves earlier and harder than win rate — deaths per battle grow a hundredfold across the ladder while win% barely leaves the ceiling, so deaths/battle is the sensitive dial and win% the blunt one.
  • Roster finding (not a bug, recorded as asked): zones 1 and 3 do NOT differ only by scaling — roster 3 genuinely swaps slinger/wolfrider for the behemoth — but at equal budget and unscaled stats roster 3 reads EASIEST (98.5% vs 93.5% / 88.5% at budget 12): its budgets buy fewer, bigger bodies, and fewer enemy actions per round is safer for the party. Zone 3's intended difficulty rests almost entirely on the x2.2 slot multiplier the sweep deliberately excludes. Roster 2 is the hardest composition pool.
  • The two scaling curves (zone 1 ladder, budget-band midpoints; party = test party base totals with node scaling at the expected 17-fights-per-30-nodes pace; warband = roster-1 fight kinds' mean Attack/effective-HP per power point (58.7 / 100.1) times budget times tier scaling; effective HP = HP ÷ (1 − armor); hero talents, trophies, relics, items and enemy elite/boss kinds all excluded):
    TierBudgetParty AtkParty eff. HP Warband AtkWarband eff. HP
    14.5350754275473
    47.5364784511901
    8113788158531541
    11113928459301706
    ENEMIES grow faster, and it is not close: warband Attack x3.4 and effective HP x3.6 across a zone against the party's x1.12 from node scaling. Across zones the enemy side restarts its ladder at x1.5 then x2.2 base while hero wins keep compounding (~x1.14 at zone 2 entry, ~x1.26 at zone 3 entry) — still far apart on raw stats. The entire counterweight is what the table excludes: roughly 35 talent points a run, boss trophies, relics, items, and kit ramps. Whether that closes a x3 gap is now a measurable question instead of a mood.
  • Baseline corrected: the recorded "88% gated" figure was 7 wins in EIGHT battles — noise, not a baseline — and the ~85% target had no encounter attached. Both citations (CLAUDE.md, Master Document §16) now point at the sweep table above; the ~85% target is read as describing top-band (budget 10-12) encounters, where composition alone already lands 88-98% before tier scaling enters. No tuning ships in this batch — these numbers are the reference the next balance decision designs against.
  • Also: two stale comments claiming node scaling is +1% per win were corrected to the code's actual +2% (battle.gd victory block, classes.gd ROLE_ATTACK); the code itself is untouched.

2026-07-31 — Batch Q: Hunter stat blocks + The Pack goes live

  • All three Hunter specs get their stat blocks (the last class without them — every spec in the game now carries one): Beastmaster 160 HP / 15% armor / +20% nature, +5% physical; Sharpshooter 140 HP / 10% armor / +10% nature, −10% physical; Survivalist 170 HP / 18% armor / +25% nature, +10% shadow, −15% arcane. Constitutions unchanged (100/90/100), Attack stays 100 for all three. The Beastmaster's armor sits below the Survivalist's on purpose: companions inherit it at summon, so it multiplies across up to three bodies (verified live — beasts spawn with the spec's 15%, not the old class base). The Sharpshooter's flagged 38% damage share was deliberately NOT touched — that belongs to the difficulty pass, where it can be measured.
  • The Pack capstone is implemented — the Beastmaster's take-one-of-three was really take-one-of-two, with a visible node the player could never buy. Now: TWO beasts active at once, both strike when the hunter attacks, each keeps its own Loyalty meter and grants its own Pack Bond boon; summoning a third replaces whichever active beast has lower Loyalty (never two of the same kind). Kill Command fires on BOTH beasts (tooltip says so; if the prey falls mid-order the pack turns on the next weakest), Hunter's Instinct mends both, Bestial Wrath takes both, Spirit Bond flows through both, and Primal Surge spends every beast's Loyalty for the summed burn. One beast dying leaves the other standing with its meter intact.
  • Under the hood: the hunter's single companion reference became an array — the single-beast case is a one-element array, so no companion code path forks (the rescope trigger in the batch never fired). Beast plates stack below the party in the first free slot, and a replaced beast now frees its nameplate (a longstanding silent leak). The bot summons a second beast under The Pack (wolf, then eagle).
  • Checked before shipping: capstone exclusivity holds (One Soul / Apex Predator can never coexist with The Pack — the one-capstone rule bars them), and the feared Ursus + Aguila pairing is fine: the two boons ride different pipelines (hunter-only damage-taken multiplier vs party-wide crit), nothing compounds.
  • Sim 200 (berserker/cryomancer/inquisitor/beastmaster): 200/200 wins, 0.17 hero deaths/battle, 7.4 rounds avg; damage share Cryomancer 45% / Beastmaster 31% / Berserker 21% / Devout 3%. With The Pack force-learned (100 battles): 100/100, Beastmaster 31→32%, party crit 12.7→17.3% (Aguila's boon working party-wide), enemy damage taken 210→182 — a healthy capstone, not a degenerate one. Win rate stays saturated; the difficulty pass is next.

2026-07-31 — Batch P: the Arcanist's ramp stops flatlining

  • The diagnosis: Resonance built +1 per damaging cast, capped at 5, and persisted all battle — so a RAMP spec hit its ceiling around turn five of a 7-9 round fight and then every further cast wasted its own mechanic. Backlash Ward paid exactly once per battle (it fired only on the transition INTO max), and Unlimited Power — one talent in one lane — was the only fix, which no spec should need just to stop wasting its resource. Stabilize was a reset, not a decision: it consumed ALL stacks, +75% damage to zero in one cast, which is why the bot only ever pulled it as a panic button.
  • Backlash Ward repeats: EVERY Resonance gain that arrives while already at maximum restores 15 Mana — a maxed Arcanist is a Mana engine fuelling the Cannon and Wrath casts he wants to be making at full stacks. Unlimited Power's overflow branch still takes priority when learned (verified in the log: Ward on the climb, Power at the top).
  • Stabilize leaves 2: consumes only the stacks ABOVE 2 — +5 Mana and +10% damage reduction (2 turns) per stack consumed, unusable at 2 or fewer, Perfect still heals 5%. At 5 stacks that is 15 Mana and 30% reduction while KEEPING +30% damage and the crit. The deliberate trade: the emergency full-vent got weaker (no more 50-80% walls) in exchange for venting often without erasing himself — the Berserker's Frenzy floor, the Pyromancer's ember, the Cryomancer's remainder.
  • Tree: the Arcanist moves off the last Batch 31 conversion onto purpose-designed node-gated lanes (tiers at 2/4 nodes in-lane, capstone at 6) — every tree in the game is now purpose-built. RESONANCE is the stack engine, OVERLOAD the risk half, CONTROL survival. Suppressing Fire and Temporal Rift move Control → Overload (an offensive Barrage ramp and a crit echo were never Control). Every id survives and the twelve conversion fillers re-spec in place, so saved ranks migrate: Harmonics (was an Arcane Mastery duplicate → Arcane Explosion +1 Resonance/rank ×2), Conduit (was an Attunement echo → +2%/rank damage per stack ×3), Resonant Core (was +max HP → max Resonance +1/rank ×2), Charged Bolts (was an Explosion dial → damaging casts at max restore 5%/rank max Mana ×3), Cannoneer (was flat Cannon damage → its per-stack term +2.5%/rank ×3), Barrage Master (was a Mana discount → +1 bolt/rank ×2), Volatility (was flat crit → Cannon and Wrath +5%/rank damage AND recoil ×3), Arcane Ward (was +armor → the damage-taken penalty falls 1%/rank per stack ×3), Still Mind (was flat damage reduction → Stabilize leaves +1 stack/rank ×2), Feedback Loop (was Meltdown, a dmg-dealt/taken dial → 10%/rank of recoil paid as Mana ×3, pairing with Conversion so a committed Control build turns self-harm into fuel). The cross-lane Meltdown ↔ Still Mind exclusive is gone; the new exclusive is Arcane Ward ↔ Still Mind, in-lane in Control — run permanently hot, or vent constantly for almost nothing: never stabilise, or stabilise all the time.
  • Capstones: Magi's Wrath (Overload, unchanged), Singularity (was a stat pile → Resonance has NO maximum, the damage-taken penalty stops rising past 5 stacks; with no cap there is no overflow, so Ward/Power payouts retire for that build), Master of Moments (was a stat bundle → Stabilize consumes nothing and pays full value from current stacks, every 3 turns).
  • Bot rotation: Stabilize at 4+ stacks below 80% health (was max-stacks below 70% — correct for a full reset, far too conservative for a vent). The sim bot now vents 0.9 times per battle instead of almost never.
  • Verified live: both scenes parse clean; the lane audit passes (all 12 trees 24 nodes / 3 capstones / 7-7-7; arcanist node-gated, exclusive pair in-lane, all 24 ids preserved). Autoplay: Backlash Ward fires three casts in a row at max; Stabilize logs "3 stacks vented (2 remain)" and the bot vents mid-fight repeatedly; with Unlimited Power force-learned the overflow converts instead of triggering the Ward; Charged Bolts, Feedback Loop, Resonant Core (cap 7), Singularity (9 banked, no Ward) and Master of Moments ("5 stacks channelled, none consumed") all proc on cue. Sim 200 × berserker/arcanist/inquisitor/beastmaster: 199/200 wins, 8.0 rounds avg, Arcanist 47% damage share (286/battle; recorded, not tuned — win% sits at the pre-difficulty-pass ceiling), Stabilize 0.9 uses/battle.

2026-07-31 — Batch O: the Cryomancer's cold finally holds

  • The diagnosis: his win condition was 4 Chilled stacks on ONE target, but his main applier (Razor Ice) scattered stacks across 2 RANDOM enemies, every reapplication reset a shared 3-turn clock so whole piles evaporated at once, and the freeze itself WIPED the stacks — deleting Hypothermia and Hungering Cold, the per-stack auras, at the exact moment they mattered. Concentrating 4 stacks on a chosen enemy was close to luck, and the payoff destroyed the engine (the same fault the Berserker's bleedout and the Pyromancer's detonation had). Half the passive was dead weight besides: a 25% Frostbite proc whose −50% healing does nothing against the many warbands with no healer.
  • Permafrost v2 — the name has always meant this: Chilled stacks applied by the Cryomancer never expire (scoped to HIS applications; chill from any other source keeps the 3-turn clock). Frozen enemies still take 15% increased damage from all sources. The Frostbite clause leaves the passive — Rime still applies it on cast, where the status is chosen rather than rolled. And freezing now leaves 1 Chilled stack instead of 0 (the Berserker's Frenzy-floor principle: cashing in shouldn't erase the engine), so the rebuild starts from something and the per-stack auras keep ticking. A boss that resists the freeze keeps sitting on all 4 stacks — Break it, chill it once, and the ice takes hold.
  • Kit: Razor Ice concentrates instead of scattering — THREE shards at a SINGLE chosen target, one stack each (20 Mana / 20% Atk / 10 BD / 2.5 / 3cd unchanged; Perfect still 25% per shard). Blizzard is worth its cost at 30 Mana and 3.5 initiative (was 35/4.0) — with permanent stacks its field-wide 1-2 stacks are real preparation, not evaporation. Ice Lance gains +10% of Attack per Chilled stack on the target (65% at three stacks) — it finally pulls its weight BEFORE the freeze, not just during the auto-crit.
  • Tree: the Cryomancer moves off the Batch 31 conversion onto purpose-designed node-gated lanes (tiers at 2/4 nodes in-lane, capstone at 6): DEEP FREEZE reaches and holds Frozen, SHATTERPOINT is the burst payoff, WINTER is the field aura. Every id survives and the twelve conversion fillers re-spec in place, so saved ranks migrate: Cold Snap (was a Frostbolt dial → Frozen +1 turn/rank ×2) ↔ Bitter Cold (was a flat damage dial → every freeze chills the whole field 1 stack/rank ×3) as the new IN-LANE exclusive — depth against breadth; Glacial Economy (was a Blizzard discount → freezes return 5%/rank max Mana ×2), Freezing Advance (re-spec: was a Rime rider in the wrong lane → his next attack on a fresh freeze +10%/rank ×3), Splintering Shards (re-spec: was an extra RANDOM target that fought the win condition → a 20%/rank chance at a FOURTH shard, 60% at rank 3 to freeze out of one Razor Ice), Lance Focus (unchanged concept, −5 Mana/rank ×2), Crystal Edge (was a crit dial → Lance +5%/rank per stack ×3), Honed Shards (was a Razor dial → Lance crits apply 1 stack/rank ×2), Frost Ward (was +armor → 4%/rank less damage from Chilled enemies ×3), Icy Resolve (was +max HP → Rime +1 turn/rank ×2), Winter's Grasp (was a Frigid dial → 1 random Chilled enemy/rank gains a stack at his turn start ×3), Numbing Veil (was a damage-taken dial → Chilled enemies 5%/rank chance to miss ×3). Capstones: Shatter (unchanged), Absolute Zero (re-spec: freezes keep ALL 4 stacks and the target refreezes the moment its freeze ends — total lockdown, every per-stack aura permanently maxed against it), Eternal Winter (re-spec: EVERY enemy gains a stack at the start of each of his turns — inevitability).
  • Bot rotation per the design doc: Razor Ice at the most-chilled target → Ice Lance against the Frozen, else at 3+ stacks → Blizzard when 3+ enemies hold fewer than 2 stacks → Rime → Shatter when 2+ enemies are Chilled → Frostbolt at the MOST CHILLED target, which is what makes the bot demonstrate concentration instead of spreading.
  • Verified live: both scenes parse clean; the talent audit passes — all lane trees 24 nodes / 3 caps / 7-7-7, every Cryomancer id survives, Cold Snap ↔ Bitter Cold bars both ways, every payload lands on a real unit field. Autoplay receipts: Blizzard preps the field ("never thaws" on every stack), Razor concentrates the Chief to a freeze, the freeze leaves x1 (15 of 15 freezes in the 8-battle sample), the pile rebuilds 2-3-4 and refreezes the SAME target — the cycle the spec was designed around; an uncritted 59-damage Ice Lance at 3 stacks (35% + 30% — the formula lands exact). Forced-talent sims: Bitter Cold cascades one freeze into two more (the re-entry guard holds — no ice age), Glacial refunds 10 Mana per freeze in the chain, Winter's Grasp and Eternal Winter advance the field each turn, Absolute Zero holds x4 through the freeze, Freezing Advance lands its +30%. Freezes per battle 1.5 → 1.9 (8-battle A/B) with battles 12% SHORTER — per-round freeze rate +35% — and lost-turns-to-freeze nearly tripled (3 → 8): the old freezes clustered at battle's end where the lost turn never came; these land mid-fight. Sim line (200 battles, standard party): 200/200 wins, Cryomancer damage share 46% (277/battle), Razor Ice 2.0 / Ice Lance 1.2 / Frostbolt 2.7 casts per battle. He was the flagged outlier at 36% before the batch; the rise was expected and is RECORDED, not tuned — that dial belongs to the difficulty pass, where it can be measured properly.

2026-07-31 — Batch N: the Pyromancer's burn engine finally ignites

  • The diagnosis: Flamewave extended Burn but never APPLIED it — the spec's only AoE could not start a fire — and Wildfire's spread was strict-Adjacent, so corpses choked it. The only reliable igniter was the single-target Fireball, which made Inferno Master's five-burning-enemy cap physically unreachable: the passive promised a payoff the kit could not deliver.
  • Kit: Flamewave now applies 2 turns of Burn to every enemy — already-burning targets gain +2 turns instead, the existing behaviour (Perfect: 3 either way). Wildfire spreads the target's Burn to EVERY other living enemy at half duration rounded up (was Adjacent-only and corpse-blocked; the pre-hit capture stays, so a killing blast still spreads). Detonation becomes a real Nuker payoff: 25 Mana (was 20), 25% Atk (was 15%), and it consumes 150% of the burn's remaining damage (tick × turns left × 1.5; Perfect re-applies 2 turns, was 1). It stays single-target — hitting the whole field is a talent build, not a baseline.
  • Tree: Pyromancer is now a purpose-designed node-gated lane tree (tiers at 2/4 nodes in-lane, capstone at 6) — the first Mage on lanes, and the lanes are named for the loop itself: KINDLING builds the fire, INFERNO profits while it burns, DETONATION cashes it in. Every id survives and the twelve conversion fillers re-spec in place, so saved ranks migrate: Arsonist→Conflagration (Flamewave +1 Burn turn/rank ×2), Kindling→Cinder Trail (Fireball embers one random other enemy 1 turn/rank ×2), Spreading Flames→Ember Wind (an enemy dying Burning passes its remaining Burn to a random living enemy), Firebrand→Heat Haze (Inferno Master cap 5 → 8 ×3) ↔ Cauterize→Scorched Earth (burning enemies deal 5%/rank less ×3) as the in-lane exclusive — the same burning field read as multiplier or as smother — Undying Flame→Living Flame (3%/rank max Mana per turn while 3+ burn ×2), Warm Glow→Chain Reaction (Detonation splashes 20%/rank of its damage to every other burning enemy ×3), Rekindle→Fuse (Detonation 15%/rank chance to reset its cooldown ×2), Shockwave→Blast Radius (consume +25%/rank more ×3), Focused Blast→White Heat (Detonation always crits vs 5+ turn burns, 4+ at rank 2 ×2), True Phoenix→Avatar of Flame (Inferno Master uncapped + fire ignores enemy fire resistance), Hellfire→Cataclysm (after Detonation resolves it chains to the burning enemy with the most Burn left at 60% power — damage and BD — up to 3 more links, each 60% of the one before, HARD-CAPPED at 4 hits total; each link consumes its target's Burn, so the chain starves itself).
  • Bot rotation per the design doc: Flamewave when 2+ enemies aren't burning → Wildfire when one target holds 4+ turns and 2+ enemies aren't burning → Detonation against the enemy with the MOST remaining Burn at 3+ turns → Firestorm → Fireball. One honest note: in bot hands Wildfire almost never fires (0.0 casts/battle over 200) — Flamewave's higher priority keeps the whole field lit, which pre-empts Wildfire's own gate. It remains the stack-then-spread combo button for human play, and the machinery is verified live ("Wildfire spreads the Burn to 4 other enemies").
  • Verified live: both scenes parse clean; the talent audit passes — all twelve trees 24 nodes / 3 caps / 7-7-7, every Pyromancer id survives, the exclusive bars both ways, capstone gating at 6 nodes. Autoplay receipts: one Flamewave lights the field (4-5 "Burn on ..." lines → Inferno Master live at +20-25%, where the old kit rarely passed +10%); "Detonation on Withered Warden — 96 fire dmg CRIT ... consumes the Burn (+79 bonus damage)" against a 5-turn burn (tick 6 × 5 × 1.5 × 1.75 Blast Radius — the formula lands exact); Cataclysm chains 60% → 36% walking down the stacks; Chain Reaction leaps to 4 burning enemies; Ember Wind passes dead enemies' flames on; Living Flame and Fuse proc on cue; Avatar of Flame's Burn ticks on a fire-resistant Shaman land untrimmed (no "(resisted)" tag). Standard-party sim unaffected (199/200 wins). Pyromancer-party sim (berserker,pyromancer,inquisitor,beastmaster), 200 battles: 200/200 wins, Pyromancer damage share 50% (293/battle), Flamewave 1.8 / Detonation 1.4 / Fireball 3.8 casts per battle. Flagged for the difficulty pass: 50% share on the BASE kit is the hottest lead in the game (the Sharpshooter's 38% drew the same flag in Batch 32). The loop working is this batch's brief; the number is the designer's dial.

2026-07-31 — Batch M: the Mage spec stat blocks land

  • All three Mage specs get their own health, armour and resists via the per-spec stat block (apply_spec_stats — same helper on the battle spawn and the party sheet, so the two surfaces cannot drift; a headless parity script asserted both build chains agree on every stat for all three). The class base — 99 HP / 10% armor — retires at awakening: Pyromancer 135 HP / 8% / +30% fire, −20% frost · Cryomancer 135 HP / 8% / +30% frost, −20% fire · Arcanist 155 HP / 6% / +20% arcane, +10% shadow, −15% physical. Constitution and Attack were already live (85/85/90; all three are Damage-role 100, so no Attack override).
  • Why the Arcanist carries the most health and the least armour: his health bar is a resource he spends — Resonance bills him +5% damage taken per stack (25% at 5; Overcharge raises the cap to 8) and Arcane Cannon recoils a flat 15% of the damage it deals — so the Ramp archetype needs the pool to survive its own payoff. The physical vulnerability is the flavor cost: raw energy in robes, anything that closes the distance hurts. The Pyromancer and Cryomancer take no such penalty and are built as deliberate mirror images — each armoured in his own element, soft to his opposite.
  • "physical" confirmed a live resist key before shipping: it is the DEFAULT dmg_type (ability.gd) — untyped melee IS physical — and both attack directions read the victim's resist at the same _resolve site; 22 of the enemy roster's 37 damage abilities are physical-typed. Autoplay receipt: "Orc Raider: Sundering Strike on Arcanist — 25 physical dmg (WEAK! +3) (armor −1)". One nuance logged: no enemy DEALS frost today, so the frost half of the twins' mirror (Pyromancer's weakness, Cryomancer's shrug) is identity awaiting future frost warbands; the fire half bites both ways right now (ashblade, Ash Tyrant, and fire-typed Burn ticks).
  • Party-sheet fix in passing: the Resistances hover listed only the six elements; physical joined the list (the battle plate's hover card already shows it — the Arcanist's −15% must read identically on both surfaces).
  • Verified live: both scenes parse clean. Standard sim, 200 battles before → after: wins 200/200 → 200/200, hero deaths/battle 0.32 → 0.28, survivors' avg HP 72% → 75%, Cryomancer damage share 36% → 37% (207 → 216/battle — the flagged outlier is the difficulty pass's job; the block deliberately adds no damage). Arcanist-party sim (berserker,arcanist,inquisitor,beastmaster), 200 battles before → after: wins 199 → 200/200, hero deaths/battle 0.94 → 0.86, survivors' avg HP 62% → 65%, Arcanist share 44% → 48% (269 → 295/battle) — living longer IS his ramp: the extra health converts to more Resonance turns, and the party's one loss disappears. He remains the bleediest lineup by far (0.86 vs the standard party's 0.28 deaths/battle) — his surcharge is the design, and he stays on the watch list for the difficulty pass.

2026-07-31 — Batch L: the Occultist tree becomes a designed set of lanes

  • The diagnosis: the Occultist is the most mechanically complete spec in the game — Ruin, Psychosis, Decay, Bewitch and Hysteria all interlock — but his three lanes never talked to each other: Madness turned enemies on their fellows, Ruin built toward detonation, Leech converted suffering into health, and nothing converted madness into Ruin or either into healing. The conversion fillers were "deepen another node" steps and Mana discounts, and the exclusive ran cross-lane (Grim Focus ↔ Pact of Flesh), which reads as a bug.
  • Occultist is now a purpose-designed node-gated lane tree (tiers at 2/4 nodes in-lane, capstone at 6) — Ruin (the detonation engine), Madness (enemies turned on each other), Leech (suffering into sustain). Every id survives and the fillers re-spec in place, so saved ranks migrate; Grim Focus and Pact of Flesh shrank (3 → 2 ranks) and the loader refunds the extra point. The conversion entry is gone; the 11 mapped original nodes moved verbatim (Dark Infusion was already dropped by the Batch 31 conversion and stays out).
  • The lanes now feed one another. Delirium: a Psychotic, Bewitched or Hysterical enemy striking a fellow marks the victim with 1 Ruin per rank — Madness generates Ruin. Cackling Mirror: any enemy striking a fellow heals the party for 3%/rank of the damage dealt — Madness generates healing. After this batch, turning an enemy on its own builds toward a detonation AND sustains the party: three lanes running one loop instead of three.
  • Ruin becomes the detonation engine it promised. Grim Focus re-specs from a flat damage dial onto the blast itself (+25%/rank). Deeper Hex widens every stack's crack (target takes 3/4/5% more damage per stack, up from the base 2%). Entropy is the Pressure archetype made literal — any Ruin at all grinds 5/rank Break damage at its bearer's every turn, feeding the Swordmaster's Broken-window loop. Unraveling seeds 1/rank Ruin in every OTHER enemy when a detonation fires — the next meter is already filling. The chain caps at one propagation per detonation by construction: seeded enemies only get primed, and primed Ruin detonates at its bearer's own turn start, never inside another detonation.
  • Madness stops wasting turns. Whispers raises Psychosis's seize chance to 60/70/80% (up from the base 50%, where half of every Psychosis was a dead turn). Lingering Torment: an expiring madness effect — Psychosis and Bewitch ticking out, or Hysteria consumed by its strike — leaves Decay for 2/rank turns (a status-expiry hook now runs on every unit for it).
  • Leech forks in-lane. Gluttony gets its own dial (+3%/rank Ruin lifesteal atop Soul Leech's). The new exclusive is Pact of Flesh ↔ Dark Barter — one ability, pay less or get more: Dark Pact bleeds 10%/rank less (free at two ranks) or heals the party 25/35% of each member's max health (up from 15%).
  • New capstones. Avatar of Ruin (Ruin): detonations no longer consume their stacks — a target held at 5 detonates again at the start of each of its turns, a recurring bomb. Soul Glut (Leech): whenever a hero heals by striking a Ruined target, the whole party heals the same amount — the "lesser healer" becomes a real one through his own mechanic (Holy restores, the Devout prevents, the Occultist siphons).
  • Doc hygiene: §7 also dropped a stale pre-Batch-K "Devout tree" paragraph that had survived alongside K's lane entry.
  • Verified: battle + party scenes parse clean; the lane audit passes 24 nodes / 3 capstones / 7-7-7 with the Pact of Flesh ↔ Dark Barter exclusive symmetric and in-lane (the cross-lane pair is gone); forced-learn sims (DOD_SIM_TALENTS= "oc_grim:2,oc_deep_hex:3,oc_unravel:3,oc_entropy:3,oc_whispers:3,oc_delirium:2, oc_cackling:2,oc_torment:2,oc_gluttony:3,oc_barter:2", specs berserker/cryomancer/ occultist/beastmaster) show every re-specced node firing — Delirium marking fellow-strike victims, Entropy breaking an enemy on its own, Torment curdling an expired Bewitch into Decay, detonations chaining through Unraveling across a full field with no recursion; capstone runs proved Avatar of Ruin re-detonating the same Chief on consecutive turns, Soul Glut echoing party-wide, Pact of Flesh bleeding 0 at two ranks, and Dark Barter healing 35%; ./sim.sh 200: 200/200 wins, 8.3 avg rounds, 0.27 hero deaths/battle — the no-talent baseline is untouched.

2026-07-30 — Batch K: the Devout tree becomes a designed set of lanes

  • The diagnosis: Conviction promised a party-wide Faith system, but Faith had exactly ONE source — Divine Shield absorbing damage — a single-target ability on a 2-turn cooldown, so most of the party sat at zero for most of the fight. Blessing of Zeal doubled a Faith gain its target almost never had, and the bot made it worse by shielding the weakest ally while kindling the hardest hitter — the two never met (the doubling did literally nothing in every sim). Structurally: Faith and Bulwark were one lane split in half (Faith comes FROM shields), three of Zeal's seven nodes were riders on Sacred Resolve — itself a node in that lane — and the exclusive ran cross-lane (Righteous Fire ↔ Stalwart), which reads as a bug.
  • Devout is now a purpose-designed node-gated lane tree (tiers at 2/4 nodes in-lane, capstone at 6) — Bulwark (the shield itself), Faith (the stack economy), Zeal (everything else he casts). Every id survives and the fillers re-spec in place, so saved ranks migrate; only Bastion's cap shrank (2 → 1) and the loader refunds the extra point. The conversion entry is gone; unchanged payloads moved verbatim. Spec id stays "inquisitor" (legacy — saves and trees key on it).
  • Faith gets its second source. Fervor: while Consecrated Ground holds, every ally gains 1 Faith per rank at the start of their turn — party-wide, riding a base-kit cast, so Conviction finally behaves like the system its description promises (and Consecrated Ground is worth casting beyond its mitigation). Binding Oath: releases keep 1 Faith per rank instead of resetting, so allies live in the useful 3–5 band.
  • Bulwark owns the shield. Warded Robes re-specs onto the shield itself (+10%/rank armor while it holds, a rider stamped at grant). Stalwart deepens the absorb (35/40/45% of the Devout's max health) and forks IN-LANE against Bastion (Divine Shield cooldown −1): a bigger shield, or a more frequent one — the second fork directly attacks the one-source-on-a-2cd problem. Unyielding Aegis re-forms a breaking shield at 30%/rank of its original strength, once per cast (Afterglow still fires — the shield did break).
  • Zeal loses its dead-lane risk. Cleansing Waters and Healing Pulse key off EITHER banner now — Consecrated Ground or Sacred Resolve — read live off the living Devout (no more cast-time snapshot), so skipping Sacred Resolve no longer strands two three-rank nodes. Righteous Fire re-specs from a flat damage dial to Consecrated Ground's reflect (15/20/25%); Lifewell converts the reflected damage into party healing (20%/rank) — together the ground becomes a healing engine. Crusader's Tempo deepens the blessing's cast-time cooldown tick (1 extra turn per rank, ×2). Purity gives Blessing of Zeal a Divine Shield of its own (10%/rank of the Devout's max health) — flagged divine through the same grant path as every other shield, so it builds Faith and the doubling finally travels WITH a source.
  • New capstones. Apostle (Faith): releases no longer consume stacks — an ally at 5 stays at 5 and every further gain re-triggers the release (a Communion chain-guard now stops release cascades from recursing forever, which Apostle made possible). Judgement (Zeal): while Consecrated Ground holds, every enemy that damages a hero is Sundered 2 turns and takes Break damage equal to 20% of what it dealt — a party role that is neither healing nor damage, and plays straight into the Swordmaster's Break loop.
  • Bot fix: Blessing of Zeal now targets whoever holds a Divine Shield (highest Attack only as fallback), so the shield+zeal combo actually occurs in sims instead of never.
  • Verified: battle + party scenes parse clean; the lane audit passes 24 nodes / 3 capstones / 7-7-7 with the Stalwart ↔ Bastion exclusive symmetric and in-lane; forced-learn sims (DOD_SIM_TALENTS="dv_fervor:2,dv_oath:2,dv_warded:2, dv_stalwart:3,dv_unyielding:2,dv_righteous:3,dv_crusade:2,dv_purity:2,dv_lifewell:3", specs berserker/cryomancer/inquisitor/beastmaster) show every new node firing and Faith stacks standing on ALL FOUR allies at once (releases on two different heroes); a capstone run proved the Apostle engine (30 releases across 2 battles, no recursion), Judgement (12 sentences passed), and Pulse/Waters firing under Consecrated Ground with Sacred Resolve unlearned; ./sim.sh 200: 200/200 wins, 8.4 avg rounds, 0.30 hero deaths/battle — the no-talent baseline is untouched.

2026-07-30 — Batch J: the Holy tree becomes a designed set of lanes

  • The diagnosis: six of the twenty-four converted nodes were "deepen another node," and two of the three capstones were the same trick — Avatar of Mercy was +2 Heavenly Aura steps, Living Sanctum +2 Guardian Angel steps. Worse, nothing in the entire tree touched Empower, the most interesting decision in the kit, and the Zealous Light ↔ Serenity exclusive ran across lanes, which read as a bug. hl_martyr even duplicated hl_inner_faith (both wrote max_hp_pct).
  • Holy is now a purpose-designed node-gated lane tree (tiers at 2/4 nodes in-lane, capstone at 6) — Radiance (throughput) / Mercy (the resource economy) / Sanctuary (the safety net). Every id survives and re-specs in place, so saved ranks migrate; only Serenity's cap shrank (2 → 1) and the loader refunds the extra point. The conversion entry is gone; unchanged payloads moved verbatim.
  • The Mercy lane now runs the whole economy. Zealous Light: begin each battle with 1 Mercy per rank (×3) — Mercy was purely reactive, Hymn literally uncastable on turn one. Ardor: while holding 4+ Mercy (3+ at rank 2), Empowering consumes no stack — bank to the threshold, then Empower freely. Martyr's Vigor: the Mercy ceiling rises to 6, then 7. Avatar of Mercy (capstone): +1 Mercy at the start of each of her turns and Empower costs no stack — it supersedes Ardor and Sanctified in the no-cost chain (either/or, never a double refund).
  • Radiance forks on wasted healing: Radiant Cascade (a CRITICAL heal splashes 25%/rank onto the lowest-health other ally — needs Triage to fire at all) is exclusive with Overflow (15%/rank of any overhealing spills onto the lowest-health other ally, reading the same overheal Capacitor banks from, at the same three sites: Heal, Hymn voices, Renewal's perfect burst). In-lane, one decision, two builds.
  • Sanctuary stops healing after the fact: Blessed Vestments (allies with Renewal take 5%/rank less damage — target-side, while the Cleric stands), Beacon (at the start of each of her turns, every ally below 25% heals 5%/rank of their max — no cast spent), Serenity (once per battle, the FIRST ally to take lethal damage survives at 1 HP; checked after a unit's own saves like Undying Rage and Ashes, and spent party-wide). Living Sanctum (capstone): all the healing she grants a single ally — Heal, Renewal casts AND ticks (snapshotted on the status), Divine Plea — also heals the whole party for 25% of its value; Hymn is already the whole party, so it does not echo itself, and echoes never echo.
  • Verified: both scenes parse clean; the talent audit passes 24 / 3 capstones / 7-7-7 for all 12 specs plus the Batch J checks (node-gated, all 24 ids preserved, in-lane exclusive, old cross-lane pair gone, gating and capstone bars behave). Every re-specced node fires in forced-learn sims (30 battles: Zealous 30, Martyr 30, Ardor 19, Cascade 9, Overflow 64, Vestments 49, Beacon 5, Serenity 2; capstones separately: Avatar 57, Sanctum 55 over 10). The user's real pre-change save loads with ranks intact, and a synthetic hl_serenity:2 save migrates to 1 with the point refunded (the live save was byte-restored after the test).
  • Balance watch: sim 200 with no talents is flat against the pre-change baseline (wins 100% → 100%, healing/battle 168 → 157, deaths 0.31 → 0.28 — noise; every new hook is rank-gated). With the full 18-point forced loadout, healing/battle rises 168 → 295 and deaths drop to 0.20 — Zealous Light and the Mercy multiplier compound exactly as the design doc predicted; flagged for playtest tuning.
  • Docs housekeeping in the same pass: §7's framework counts still said "four purpose-designed / eight conversions" (stale since Batches F and H) — now seven and five, with the node-gated list corrected; a leftover pre-lanes "Warden tree" paragraph (contradicting Batch H's block) was removed, as was the old row-tree Holy paragraph. The batch doc's report of a missing Mercy lane in §7 was already fixed in an earlier docs pass — the lane was present; the entry is now replaced wholesale by the designed tree.

2026-07-30 — Batch I: the Cleric spec stat blocks land

  • All three Cleric specs get their own health, armour and resists via the per-spec stat block (apply_spec_stats, same helper on the battle spawn and the party sheet, so the two surfaces cannot drift). The class base — 121 HP / 15% armor — retires at awakening: Holy 150 HP / 10% / +20% holy, −15% shadow · Devout 175 HP / 18% / +15% holy, +10% fire, −10% shadow · Occultist 155 HP / 8% / +25% shadow, +10% nature, −20% fire. Constitution and Attack were already live (100/110/95; Support 50, the Occultist's Pressure archetype attacks at 100).
  • Why the pools are tall: Cleric max HP is an OUTPUT stat. Heal is 40% of the Holy's maximum, Renewal ticks 15% of it; Divine Shield absorbs 30% of the Devout's, and Afterglow, Healing Pulse and the Faith release all pay out from the same number; the Occultist's Ruin detonation and Dark Pact trade in his. A beefier cleric doesn't just survive longer — it heals harder.
  • The Occultist's planned holy weakness was swapped for fire. The enemy roster audit found NOTHING that deals holy damage (dealt types today: physical everywhere; nature from archer, shaman, bog troll, behemoth and the Withered Warden; fire from the ashblade and the Ash Tyrant; shadow from the hexer) — a −20% that never bites is not a weakness. Fire keeps every entry in his block live across the run: shadow resist against z2's hexers, nature resist against both forests, and the burn zone gets a real line on him — cleansing flame finds the corrupt.
  • Verified live: Divine Shield absorbs 53 at spawn (was 36 off the class base; perfect 61) — proportional to the HP bump, so nothing upstream still reads the class base. Both scenes parse clean; the party-sheet numbers and the battle nameplate agree for all three specs. Sim, 200 battles before → after: wins 100% → 100% (the known ceiling), hero deaths/battle 0.39 → 0.35, survivors' average HP 69% → 74%, healing landed/battle 1 → 4. A stale code comment over the Divine Shield special (it still said 50/55%) was corrected to the real 30/35%.

2026-07-30 — Batch H: the Warden tree becomes a designed set of lanes

  • The diagnosis: half the converted tree was damage on a 75-Attack character. Iron Will granted +5%/rank damage per debuff — at full investment with three debuffs that's +45% of a number so small the bonus was about fourteen damage. Tank-role Attack means every offensive node is dead weight. So the offensive payloads were converted into currencies a tank actually spends: mitigation, Break pressure, threat, and party protection.
  • The tree moves from the Batch 31 conversion into a purpose-designed LANE_TREES entry — node-gated like Berserker/Swordmaster (tiers open at 2/4 nodes bought in the lane, capstone at 6). Lanes: Plate / Threat / Banner. No node was deleted: every id survives and re-specs in place, and no new rank cap sits below its old one, so a pre-change save loads with every rank intact and zero refunds.
  • Iron Will keeps its name and its idea — adversity makes him stronger — converted into the currency a tank banks: the Warden takes 4%/rank LESS damage per debuff on him (was +damage). Three debuffs at full ranks is 36% less damage taken instead of +14 damage. The permanent chip now reads "-X%" and follows the live count.
  • Ten fillers re-specced in place (same ids, new effects): Layered Plating → Plate Discipline (the Heavy Plating climb steepens +3%/rank — 11/14/17% per unblocked hit, so blocks arrive every second hit instead of every third), Immovable-the- filler → Battered Not Broken (blocks remove 8/rank Break from his own meter — a Broken unit cannot Block at all, so blocking now holds his identity on), Taunt Master → Provoke (Mocking Blow taunts +1 foe/rank), Spiked Bulwark → Spite (attackers take 8%/rank of dealt damage back), Shattering Blow → Bruising Guard (blocks deal 10/rank BD to the attacker — the most-attacked hero becomes a quiet party Break engine), Grudge → Grudge v2 (+6%/rank damage vs enemies HIS taunt holds), Stomp Drill → Rallying Stomp (War Stomp refuels +5%/rank more), Bannerman → Bulwark Line (Interpose grants +1 charge/rank per ally), Fortress → Shared Vigil (allies take 3%/rank less damage while the Warden is above 50% health), Veteran's Will → Steadfast (damage that would drop an ally below 20% health: the Warden absorbs 15%/rank of it — battle-layer split with full death bookkeeping, Pressure stays on the struck hero like Unity).
  • Both invented capstones re-specced: The Mountain → Immovable (Plate: the Warden CANNOT be Broken — Break damage is refused at the same take_hit gate as the Devout's Bulwark of Fortitude — and +20% Block chance), Avenger → Vengeful Guardian (Threat: the FIRST attack he Blocks each turn is answered with a free Crushing Blow via the Opportunist counter machinery — once per turn, re-armed at his turn start; per-block would be absurd at his block rate). Hold the Line caps Banner unchanged.
  • Exclusives: the old invented cross-lane pair (Immovable ↔ Grudge) is gone — cross-lane exclusives read as bugs rather than choices (no Tenacity ↔ Rally link existed in code). The one pair is Spite ↔ Bruising Guard, in-lane and a genuine fork: convert the punishment you soak into damage, or into Break pressure.
  • Audit: all twelve trees 24/3/7-7-7 plus warden structure (node gating, lane homes, rank caps, exclusive barring both ways, zero-refund migration) — 134 asserts green. Forced-learn verification: every one of the twelve re-specced payloads demonstrably fires in sim logs (standard comp for the always-on nodes; a forced boss/chief/shaman/brute lineup to trigger Bulwark Line, Steadfast and the capstones); Provoke at 2 ranks drags 3 foes, Bulwark Line at 2 ranks grants 3 charges, Vengeful Guardian proced 57 times across ~100 Warden turns — the per-turn cap holds.
  • Sim (200 battles, warden/cryomancer/inquisitor/beastmaster, no talents): 200/200 wins, 9.6 avg rounds, hero deaths 0.08/battle, enemy damage 140/battle, Breaks on heroes 0.39/battle, Warden damage share 19% — the Batch G baseline holds through the rewired block/taunt/Stomp/Interpose sites. Standard-party 200 regression: 200/200.

2026-07-30 — Batch G: Warden — the wall you can rely on

  • The diagnosis: Block was his identity and he had no say in it — a flat 20% roll, with four talents (Unkillable, Tenacity, Rally, Richocet) paying off on a Block a bad streak could refuse to supply. A tank's fantasy isn't gambling, it's RELIABILITY. He also had no way to take a hit for someone — the purest tank verb in the genre wasn't in the kit.
  • Stat block (via the same apply_spec_stats helper as the other Warriors, so the battle spawn and the party sheet can never drift): 200 HP / 32% armor / Constitution 130 / 10% base Block (block_chance joins the spec-stat vocabulary alongside the Swordmaster's parry_chance) / +15% fire & frost resist, −20% arcane. Attack stays 75 (Tank role). Effective HP against physical ≈294 — versus the Berserker's 206 and the Swordmaster's 212, before Block — and Con 130 makes him the hardest hero to Break. The arcane hole is deliberate: enemy casters keep a real line on him.
  • Heavy Plating v2 — reliability, not luck: +15% Block chance, and every attack against the Warden that is NOT Blocked raises his Block chance by +8% for the rest of the battle (cap +40%); any Block resets the bonus. Starting at 25%, an unlucky streak climbs 33 → 41 → 49 → 57 → 65%. It deliberately doesn't let him CHOOSE to Block (that would undercut Shieldwall) — it guarantees the on-Block talents fire on a dependable cadence of roughly every third attack. The spec chip shows the LIVE total as it climbs (one fresh-per-battle var on the unit, incremented and reset at the block-roll site; both moves are logged, and block sources still log correctly — verified in autoplay).
  • Shieldwall is base kit now — his one moment of control over the signature mechanic was locked behind a talent. Moved into spec_abilities unchanged (25 Rage, 2.0, 2cd, Blocks 3 / Perfect 5). The wd_shieldwall node is re-specced in place, same id (saves keep their ranks) as Shield Mastery: Shieldwall grants +1 charge per rank, 2 ranks, perfect casts included.
  • NEW — Interpose (25 Rage, 2.5 int, 4cd, self-cast, no target selection): every OTHER living ally gains 1 Shieldwall charge — the next attack against them is BLOCKED (Perfect: the Warden gains one too). Charges ADD to any already held, and it rides the existing shield_charges machinery — chip, countdown, and block priority all came free. The tank verb the kit was missing, and the party feels him even on turns nothing targets him.
  • War Stomp's empty perfect got its payload: allies regain 20% of their resource instead of 10% — the refuel is the real cargo on a 75-Attack tank, so that's what the perfect deepens.
  • Bot rotation (deterministic, so sims exercise the real kit): Interpose when 2+ allies are below 60% HP → Shieldwall below 50% HP or vs 3+ enemies → Mocking Blow whenever HIS taunt has lapsed (free + 10 Rage — his engine, never skipped) → Crushing Blow if Sunder is down → War Stomp when 2+ allies are below 70% resource → Strike.
  • Sim (200 battles, warden/cryomancer/inquisitor/beastmaster): 200/200 wins, 9.6 avg rounds, hero deaths 0.03/battle (the Berserker party's own 200-run: 0.48), enemy damage dealt 137/battle (Berserker party: 246), Breaks suffered 0.35/battle (1.04), survivors at 81% HP. Warden damage share 18% — a tank's share. Whole kit exercised (Mocking 2.0 / Crushing 2.1 / Shieldwall 1.4 / Stomp 0.4 / Interpose 0.1 per battle; Interpose is rare because the bot only reaches for it when the party is actually hurt, which this comp rarely is). Standard-party 200-run unchanged at 200/200 — no regression. Autoplay verified the loop end to end: climbs 33 → 41 → 49 logged on unblocked hits, reset on the next Block, and an Interpose charge saved the Cryomancer from a boss Timber Slam.

2026-07-30 — Batch F: the Swordmaster tree becomes a designed set of lanes
